## 3. Full construction rows

### PR-A1 — Converter wiring, contextual replacement, and ordinary UC construction

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Existing generic qualitative composition/dummy security for PM, RS and every listed family setup; native random-system cascade, independent parallel resources and converters are the relevant source operations. This row is coverage, not a new construction.
2. **Precise source.** M02 Def. 8, invocation and Lemma 4 (`151–179`); MR11 Defs. 13–17 (`445–538`); M11CC Def. 1, Def. 2, Lemma 1, Thm. 1 (`201–261,298–345`); MR16 Def. 1/Lemma 1 (`189–201`). Public links are in §1.
3. **Native signature/laws.** A resource R with named interfaces; converters with inside/outside ports; serial converter monoid, interfacewise commuting application, parallel separated resources; distinguishers closed under the allowed converter/resource emulations. M02 additionally permits an invoking wrapper C(·) making adaptive zero/one/many internal calls. Exact behavioral equivalence is preserved by such invocation. These are operational laws before a security theorem.
4. **Repository state/API.** `CC/UCSetup.agda:21–26,55–57`; `CC/Abstract2.agda:45–221`; `CC/Standard2.agda:19–34`. PM and RS instantiate Standard2 on their lawful monoidal carriers. RS native `CC/UC/RandomSystems/Composition.agda:67–107,115–180` supplies absorption and composition/tensor estimates; qualitative/native security is related in `Construction.agda:78–115`. These are source-present declarations, not newly checked.
5. **Class.** **A** for implemented categorical substitution, dummy completeness and UC serial composition. **C** for interpreting a new native invoking wrapper in that structure; its evaluator and admitted-test closure are not supplied by the generic theorem.
6. **Exact instantiation / missing data.** Generic dummy form is `∃s:Y→X. f≈ᵁsub(s)g`, equivalent to the attacked form `∀a:X→X′.∃s′:Y→X′.sub(a)f≈ᵁsub(s′)g`. PM uses its sealed carrier/tests; RS uses the quotient/readout. A native C requires a typed implementation and an equation that closes any external distinguisher around C to an admitted test of its argument. C need not have tensor in arbitrary UCSetup; Standard2 supplies that extra structure in these concrete instances.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** PM and RS are actual consumers; PM family views reuse the same theorem after construction. RS `play-cup`/full abstraction supplies an actual native-test bridge (`FullAbstraction.agda:151–181`). A wrapper whose executions do not fit the chosen source interface/evaluator is not made lawful merely by naming it composition.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The PM bare-kernel separator `reveal-≈ℰ` and `¬GradeStableᵒ` (`FullAbstraction.agda:186–190`) refutes treating bare test-kernel equality as already ancilla-closed U-agreement. This is a concrete code control, not an unknown theorem. Native loop/linker construction is a separate structural question handled in the parent report.
9. **Quantifiers / preservation.** The dummy simulator is fixed before all ancillary grades/tests. Ordinary state-map simulation is not that grade converter. Native behavior equality, hom equality, bare kernel, U-agreement and UC emulation remain distinct. Restricting distinguishers or changing carrier representations needs the existing refinement/transfer hypotheses, not just composition.
10. **Exact obligations.** For each new native wrapper: prove typed operational identity/composition, resource separation, test-closing/run equation and ancilla coverage; then reuse A. Reflection additionally needs representable/full simulators/grades, not merely test soundness. No second UC-composition API is warranted.

### PR-A2 — Distinguishing advantage, closed slack, fixed simulators and finite hybrids

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Existing QUC approximation, quantitative security and hybrid arithmetic; actual PM and QRS instances, with family QUC views as further consumers.
2. **Precise source.** M02 Defs. 9–10/Lemma 5 (`198–211`); MPR07 Def. 6 and Def. 12 (`138–170,240–250`); M11CC Def. 2 and §4.5 (`218–265`).
3. **Native signature/laws.** For a terminating Boolean distinguisher D, `Δ^D(F,G)=|Pr[D(F)=1]−Pr[D(G)=1]|`; class/query-bounded distance maximizes/supremizes over the specified class. Triangle and admissible wrapper absorption hold. MPR07 separately defines transcript TV `δ_k^D`; `Δ_k^D≤δ_k^D`, with equality only for optimal final statistical decision, and equality of maxima in the all-information-theoretic class.
4. **Repository state/API.** `CC/UC/Quantitative.agda:40–70,112–186`; `Witness.agda:41–80`; `Bridge.agda:46–145`; `CC/Approx/Rational.agda:40–99`. PM readout in `UC/Model/Observation.agda:48–76`, PM QUC in `UC/Model/Quantitative.agda:19–29`. `src/ProbabilisticLogic/Dp/Advantage.agda:3–9,65–69` compares **both verdict masses**, as a rational-slack relation, with no rational-valued supremum formed. RS closed relation is in `RandomSystems/Advantage.agda:37–92`; native/full QUC bridges in `RandomSystems/Quantitative.agda:79–108,125–148`.
5. **Class.** **A** for test approximation, fixed-error Witness composition, closed-slack bridge, finite hybrid summation. **D** for identifying this Approx enrichment with a convex/probabilistic carrier or with transcript TV without extra semantics.
6. **Exact instantiation.** `At s ε f g = f≈ᵁ[ε]sub(s)g`; `Witness ε f g = ∃s.At s ε f g`. `ctx-trans` costs ε⊕δ; `at-trans` composes s∘t. `at-compose` uses `(id⊗t)∘(s⊗id)` and **ε_u⊕ε_f in that order**. Rational errors happen to commute; arbitrary error algebras need not. PM uses `qevaluationᵒ`; QRS uses `Verdict.closure` and `qevaluationᴿ`.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** `Examples/FaultFlag/UC.agda:80–109` realizes quarter plus quarter equals half with the composed simulator and a smaller-error separation. `Examples/RandomSystems/Availability.agda` is a second concrete security consumer. Returning false versus divergence has equal true mass but different false mass (`Dp/Advantage.agda:7–9`); `Upper` (`113–126`) is a one-sided success bound, not closeness to `return false`.
8. **Obstruction scope.** [INFERENCE] A constant terminal Approx presheaf on a nonprobabilistic category is still a QUCSetup; no convex mixture/coupling operation on arrows follows. Also a fixed D that always outputs zero has Δ^D=0 even for disjoint transcripts, so Δ^D cannot be replaced by transcript TV. PM partiality prevents silently using the normalized-system identity that true-mass gap alone determines Boolean distance.
9. **Quantifiers / errors.** QRS U-agreement exactly matches native `∼ᴿ[ε]`; QRS Witness exactly matches **security-only** `Constructs ε`. PM fixed-error Witness preserves into QRS. The converse is **one simulator before every positive slack**: `∃s.∀δ>0. PM.At s (ε+δ)`, not `∀δ>0.∃s`, and not automatically PM.At at exactly ε. Generic `UC.Quantitative.Witness.Witness⁺` means attack-universal form; the identically spelled RS `Construction.Witness⁺` means fixed-simulator all-positive slack. They must not be conflated.
10. **Exact obligations.** Reuse existing hybrid/witness APIs. A new native distance bridge must prove equality/soundness for the actual total/subprobability observation, all admitted tests and ancillas, plus any closed-slack limit. A finite hybrid chain does not itself certify how many resource calls an adaptive wrapper makes.

### PR-A3 — Query-aware wrapper bounds and measured family composition

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Already implemented **structured** generic rate/query machinery, instantiated on PM query grading and its measured family relations. Native random-system resource accounting remains wrapper-specific. Not a new generic composition candidate.
2. **Precise source.** M02 invocation function `m_C(k)` and Lemma 5 (`175,205–211`); M11CC Lemma 1/§4.5 (`249–265`); RSS11 Thm. 1 (`103–105`); MR16 `RO[q,q′]` (`251ff`); DGHM13 Def. 2/Thm. 2 (`84–93,119–135`).
3. **Native signature/laws.** `Δ_k(C(F),C(G))≤Δ_{m_C(k)}(F,G)` requires a finite maximal internal-call bound. A k-query distinguisher class is generally not closed under a wrapper spending more than k internal calls. RSS11 exposes simulator overhead `q_B≤q_A q_S`, `t_B≤t_A+q_A t_S`, with corresponding game/distinguisher costs. MR16 distinguishes honest query availability (minimum guaranteed) and adversarial allowance (maximum), not one untyped query count.
4. **Repository state/API.** `CC/UC/Quantitative/Query.agda:84–91,117–138,178–205`; `.Contextual.agda:48–80,143–172,192–216`; `.Family.agda:287–313,369–393,493–529`. PM `Queryᵒ` is `Model/Quantitative.agda:31–34`; `Model/Family/Contextual.agda:21–23` **publicly instantiates** `UC.Quantitative.Family` at `𝔾ᵒ qevaluationᵒ gradingᵒ`, `P.∼ᵃ-isEquivalence`, `P.zero⇒positive`, and identity reflection `(λ h→h)`. Its `Compose` is a public nested module; no separate `Contextual/Compose.agda` is required. Machine certificates/grading are `UC/QueryBound.agda:38–158` and `UC/Machine/Grading.agda`; `UC.Family` measured agreement/absorption is `:161–255`. Use these live declarations rather than the separately unlocated historical `UC.Approximate.Induced` name.
5. **Class.** **A** for these existing structured rate/family operations. **C** for a particular primary-literature C(·) and its cost/test backtranslation. **D** for same-budget nonexpansion under arbitrary C or for equating query cost with time/memory/PPT.
6. **Exact instantiation.** `.Contextual` takes a monoidal carrier M, a Rates-graded subcategory Rg, rational Approximation, distinguished closed/readout objects and a hom-respecting readout. Tests and closures have separate certificates. `ctx-sub` needs `Image forget r s` and reindexes ε to `q↦ε(scale q r)`. `at-trans` yields `ε₁(q)+ε₂(scale q r)` with first simulator's rate r. Family `≤UC[s,ε]` names simulator family and two-argument bound; `≤UC^ωᵉ` packages `∃ certified s ∃ε. NegligibleBound ε × bound`.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** Actual PM `gradingᵒ`/`Queryᵒ`, `Model.Family` and the explicit `Model.Family.Contextual` public instance are consumers of the graded/readout APIs; scalar QUC and QRS provide contrasting fixed-error views, not substitute query-bound instances. M02 arbitrary m_C(k) may be nonlinear or undefined; the existing multiplicative-rate certificate does not automatically cover every such C. RSS11 storage auditing and DGHM13 memory are concrete non-query-only controls.
8. **Obstruction scope.** [INFERENCE] A one-query wrapper that makes n internal Bernoulli trials and ORs them has distinguishing gap `1−(1−η)^n`, not η, against always-zero. This refutes the same-budget claim; it does not refute QUC fixed-error nonexpansion, whose initial hypothesis already ranges over its entire admitted test universe.
9. **Quantifiers / errors.** Family transitivity keeps one certified s and one named ε before all levels and permitted contexts, and produces `ε(n,q)+δ(n,scale q(cost s(n)))`. Serial protocol composition requires certificates for the **first real process f** and the **simulated continuation sub(t)v**, then yields `ε_u(n,scale q r_f(n))+ε_f(n,scale q r_tv(n))`. It does not assume all compared arrows certified by default. No ε-monotonicity in q is needed by the generic polynomial reindex theorem; polynomial-preserving reindexing is what matters.
10. **Exact obligations.** Prove the native wrapper's query/activation schedule certificate and operational test-absorption equation, then use the existing API. If memory/time matter, add separate explicit state/resource semantics and certificates; a rate integer is not a proof of either. To lift an arbitrary m_C, show it fits the available reindex/graded interface or formulate a genuinely broader certified usage structure rather than duplicating Witness composition.

### PR-A4 — Vanishing, arbitrary-negligible, local-negligible and uniform-small family views

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Existing family/reobservation constructions on the PM family carrier. Canonical vanishing, scalar QUC/all-positive, canonical local-negligible, schedule QUC/small-image and measured query-family bounds are separate views, not extra operational theories.
2. **Precise source.** M11CC §4.5 (`265`) explicitly permits asymptotic families, function-valued distance and a negligibility class closed under feasible magnification; MR11 Def. 17 and discussion (`532–538`) distinguishes feasibility from efficiency. The exact quantifier placements below are **repository definitions**, not attributed to either paper.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Given family index i, cofinal security projection κ and polynomial context allowances: vanishing means eventually below every positive constant; negligible means every polynomial magnification vanishes. A measured ε(n,q) can be classified at every polynomial q(n). Local test equality may existentially choose a negligible δ after choosing the context; uniform collapse moves that existential outward only to the scope actually stated.
4. **Repository state/API.** `CC/UC/Family.agda:69–149,161–255`; `UC/Model/Family.agda:25–35`; `UC/Family/Quantitative.agda:63–111`; `UC/Approximate.agda:24–63,92–117`; `UC/Approximate/Local.agda:52–99`; `UC/Family/Negligible.agda:60–108`; `.Setup.agda:49–75`; `.Quantitative.agda:67–113`; `UC/Quantitative/Family.agda:311–313`. `UC/Approximate/LocalTests.agda:39–48` is a scalar-metric control, not a whole-PM-context separation theorem.
5. **Class.** **A** for family creation, small-class collapse, refinement, negligible/vanishing absorption and measured-error composition. **D** for swapping local and uniform existential quantifiers without an additional uniformity theorem, or equating vanishing with negligible. No new family API is proposed.
6. **Exact instantiation.** PM `Model.Family` supplies `𝔾ᵒ`, `qevaluationᵒ`, `gradingᵒ`, Ix=ℕ, κ=id and cofinality. `GradedBound G ε=∀p. Poly p→G(n↦ε(n,p(n)))`; `VanishingBound=GradedBound(→0)`; `NegligibleBound=GradedBound Negligible`; `Negligible δ=∀p. Poly p→(p·δ→0)`. `Qᴺ.QSetup` uses pointwise schedule errors; `setupᴺ⁺=underlyingSmall negligible`. The local module is already an `Approx.Small` instance.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** Scalar local equality admits `2^-n` and rejects `1/(n+1)` in `LocalTests:39–48`. Canonical vanishing and scalar QUC positive collapse have implemented refinements both ways; small-image negligible has an implemented implication into canonical local-negligible, not a converse. These actual views give multiple consumers of one existing abstraction, not separate native theories.
8. **Obstruction scope.** [INFERENCE] For context labels m let gap `d_m(n)=1` if n=m and 0 otherwise. Every fixed m has a finite-support negligible bound, but no single negligible δ bounds all m, since it would satisfy δ(n)≥1 for every n. This refutes the unrestricted logical quantifier swap; it is **not** by itself a proved full-PM representability/separation result. [INFERENCE] `2^-floor(sqrt n)` is negligible but not bounded by a polynomial multiple of `2^-n`: allowing an arbitrary negligible witness is not choosing one fixed exponential template. [INFERENCE] n independent trials with per-trial gap 1/n have OR gap tending to `1−e^-1`; vanishing alone is not stable under polynomially many replacements.
9. **Exact quantifiers / errors.** Suppressing the common pointwise readout predicate, the dummy-security forms are:
   - canonical local-negligible: `∃s. ∀W,e,m. ∃δ. Negligible δ ∧ ∀i. comparison at δ(κi)`;
   - small-image `setupᴺ⁺`: `∃s. ∀W,e. ∃δ. Negligible δ ∧ ∀m,i. comparison at δ(κi)`;
   - stronger named global quantitative witness: `∃s. ∃δ. Negligible δ ∧ ∀W,e,m,i. comparison at δ(κi)`.
   Thus “uniform” in `uniform⇒local` is over closures **inside one test pair**. It is not one δ for all U-contexts. `uniform-witness⇒≤UCᴺ` accepts the third form and preserves s. Vanishing thresholds may depend on the context and positive constant; universal context/positive-constant quantifiers commute, but their existential thresholds do not become globally uniform. “Arbitrary negligible” means some witness in the whole class where ∃ is written, not `∀negligible δ`; since zero belongs to that class, the latter pointwise requirement would include zero error. `NegligibleBound` says nothing uniformly over arbitrary exponential q. Negligible implies vanishing; the reverse fails.
10. **Exact obligations.** Reuse generic Refine and existing family APIs. A converse uniformization needs a concrete finite/dominated/cofinal context argument giving one majorant; no such argument is supplied by UCSetup. A claimed PM separator needs actual context families, allowance certificates and run calculations. Polynomial repetition needs a uniform polynomial hybrid bound and a suitable negligible error envelope before collapse, not merely per-step local vanishing. No blanket reflection through a coarser view is justified.

### PR-A5 — One-sided event/audit bounds, finite-query potentials and capped negligible saturation

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Existing generic/PM-specialized property-bound machinery: permitted audit contexts, one-sided event readers, strategy-to-context adequacy, potential certificates and family saturation. This is A coverage, not a missing generic event framework or an extra QUC node.
2. **Precise source.** M02 Def. 11/Lemmas 6–7/Thm. 1 (`219–246`) and MPR07 Def. 11/Lemma 4 (`213–230`) supply the native hit-probability/fundamental-lemma contracts; M11CC §4.5 (`232–265`) supplies the compatible observation/feasibility setting. Exact `Reader`, `AuditBound`, `Saturated` and lower-real slack contracts below are repository definitions; no primary paper is claimed to use these exact signatures.
3. **Native signature/laws.** An event bound is `Pr[event]≤ε(q)` for a specified family of event-reading, q-admissible contexts, not the symmetric distance between two resources. Uniformly bounding every arbitrary Boolean test would include the constant-true test. A bounded-use rare-event drift yields a union-type mε bound, while an independent-product amplification theorem is a different statement. Lower-real observation equality can give every strictly positive rational slack without producing an exact rational maximum.
4. **Repository state/API.** `CC/UC/Audit.agda:35–38,55–117,129–135` gives rated simulator witnesses, certified contexts/budgets, permitted context classes, `AuditBound`, `Absorbs` and `audit-carry`. `UC/Machine/EventBounds.agda:64–69,90–134` gives `Reader`, `BoundedAt`, `Saturated`, `Boundedᶠ/Boundedᴺ`, zero-slack inclusion and cap reindexing. `UC/Quantitative/EventLift.agda:49–101` gives completed-run `HitsAt/Hitsᴺ`, exact strategy/context lifting `hitsᵘ`, `agree`, `hits⇒upper`. `Protocol/Safety.agda:48–81` specializes `SuperCert⊥` at `kernel P`, proves completed-run hit≤prefix-hit, and `hit-bounded`. `UC/Machine/StateEvent/Adequacy.agda:185–236` proves `stateRead-hit`, same-cap `stateBounded⇒hit`/`hit⇒stateBounded` and family/saturated versions.
5. **Class.** **A** for those APIs and their concrete PM/protocol instances. **C** only for a new native Reader/kernel/observation interpretation not already covered. **D** for turning an unrestricted one-sided bound into two-sided distance, silently swapping cap/context slack quantifiers, or treating a reader's overhead as the environment's original cap.
6. **Exact instantiation/inputs.** `Audit` needs monoidal M, Evaluation R, Rates-graded subcategory Rg and a mass map into lower reals. A rated dummy witness and `Absorbs` transport an ideal audit bound to `q↦ε(scale q r)+δ` for any supplied δ>0; this δ is numeric lower-real comparison slack, not a freely chosen new simulator. PM `Reader B B′` maps a budgeted tester on B to an event-reading tester on B′. `BoundedAt q r` bounds every certified test/closure whose **original** allowance is ≤q. `StateBoundedAt` is that existing construction at `M▷P`/`flagReader`. `flagStrat` itself spends one extra ask (`StateEvent/Read.agda:48–78`); exact same-cap adequacy separately accounts for the reader, rather than pretending its cost is zero.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** Native protocol `BoundedHit` and the PM compiled state-read bound are already equivalent at the same q under a state predicate agreeing at `idle`; the family/saturated equivalences are already present too. The observable monitor has its own exact `EventLift` bridge. Existing `GamePlaying/Potential.agda:61–67` builds `rare-cert` from `RareRaise` with mε; `:93–103` handles a fresh uniform guess; `:110–123` unions two certificates additively; `:153–161` builds `collision-cert` from `KeepOrSample`, with `(m²+m)2^-n`. These are reusable supplied-certificate constructors, not MPR07 neutralization/product amplification.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The bad-then-dead code witness (PR-C2) explains why `Protocol.Safety.hit≤badProb⊥` is an inequality, not unconditional equality. The local/uniform diagonal (PR-A4) prevents moving per-context negligible witnesses outside every context at a cap. [INFERENCE] Capping only the schedule argument but admitting arbitrary larger contexts would make q=0 purport to bound unbounded experiments; the actual definition requires the certificate/cap inequality. RSS memory-sensitive auditing still needs its memory semantics; the name `Audit` does not make that extra source model automatic.
9. **Exact quantifiers/errors.** `Saturated X ε = ∀p. Poly p→∃ν. Negligible ν ∧ ∀n. X(n,p(n),ε(n,p(n))+ν(n))`. For event `X=BoundedAt`, the **same ν for a chosen p** covers all n and all contexts admitted at cap p(n); ν may vary with p. This is stronger than choosing ν separately after each context, but not one global ν before all p. It also is not `∀negligible ν`. Neither canonical local-negligible U nor closure-uniform-within-test small-image U supplies this capped uniform envelope by pure quantifier rearrangement. `audit-carry` keeps its certified simulator fixed before contexts and numeric slack. The potential is fixed before each adaptive strategy; it bounds prefix-hit, which then bounds completed-run hit.
10. **Exact obligations.** Reuse these actual constructors and adequacy results where their inputs fit. For a new source, provide its Reader/permitted-context interpretation, native kernel and state-event boundary, marginal/readout equations and exact environment/readout/test certificates. Prove `Absorbs` for the permitted class and a uniform negligible envelope at each polynomial cap before claiming saturated property transfer. Keep the actual one-sided event theorem separate from QUC distance, native availability and MPR07 independent amplification.

### PR-B1 — Two-budget security plus designated-cap availability

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Parent-proposed arbitrary-QUC availability pair is valid **as a derived definition/theorem**, with supplied caps. Native RS availability and its two-budget transitivity are already implemented. Candidate consumers are PM QUC, QRS and schedule-family QUC; these are distinct setup views, not three independent stochastic representations.
2. **Precise source.** M11CC §5.1 Def. 3 (`271–289`) has separate availability and security; its Thm. 1 (`298–345`) composes them. The cap may actively set a no-adversary mode. MR11 filtered honest guarantees and MR16 explicit resource guarantees are compatible context, not an implication of security alone.
3. **Signature/laws.** For arbitrary QUC S, supplied grade caps `a:X→I`, `b:Y→I`, and `f:A→T_X B`, `g:A→T_Y B`, define
   `Avail_ε(a,b;f,g) := (sub a∘f) ≈ᵁ[ε] (sub b∘g)` at **unit grade**,
   and `Full_(εs,εa)(a,b;f,g) := Witness εs f g × Avail_εa(a,b;f,g)`.
   [INFERENCE] Same-middle-cap transitivity follows directly: from Full(a,b;f,g) and Full(b,c;g,h), compose simulators s∘t with security error εs⊕εs′; use `ctx-trans` for availability error εa⊕εa′. No stochastic, base-tensor, terminal-unit or automatic-discard hypothesis is needed. Caps must actually be supplied; arbitrary grade categories need not have them.
4. **Actual API coverage.** `CC/UC/Quantitative.agda:117–154` and `Witness.agda:66–71` contain the required generic proof components. The package itself is not in those inspected generic modules. Native `CC/UC/RandomSystems/Availability.agda:38–76` defines `no-adversary a f = unitorˡ.from ∘ sub a ∘ f`, `Available`, `AvailableWithin`, `SecureConstruction` and `SecurelyConstructs εs εa`; `:109–132` already proves compatible-cap, independently additive transitivity. Source-specific presence must not be called missing.
5. **Class.** **B** for packaging the above arbitrary-QUC consequence; **A** for the existing RS-native record/theorem; **C** for identifying the generic unit-grade observation with native RS honest behavior; **D** for dropping cap compatibility or claiming security implies availability.
6. **Minimal compatibility / bridge data.** [INFERENCE] Equal middle caps suffice. If b and b′ are hom-equal in the grade category, functoriality of `sub` and `ctx-resp` splice them at zero extra cost. More generally the explicit premise `(sub b∘g)≈ᵁ[0](sub b′∘g)` suffices; if that comparison costs κ, availability costs `εa⊕κ⊕εa′` in order. Arbitrary QUC supplies no approximate relation on caps themselves. RS uses behavioral converter compatibility `b Q.∼ b′` via `no-adversary-resp-converter:82–85`. The native identification requires QRS full abstraction `Quantitative.agda:87–89` **and** two-way nonexpansive transport through Standard2's invertible unitor. An arbitrary graded monad only supplies its unit structure, not an isomorphism `T_I B≅B`, so literal native unitor elimination is not universal.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** Actual `Examples/RandomSystems/Availability.agda:210–235` has compatible true/flipped-mode full constructions and their composition. `:192–208` has perfect security for a force-false converter against `ideal`, but no availability with designated true modes. The generic pair can immediately be stated for both PM/QRS; family views preserve their own error/small-class quantifier scope rather than inventing global negligibility.
8. **Concrete incompatible-cap counterexample.** [INFERENCE, assembled from inspected code] Let `f=g=h=ideal`; choose `a=b=mode false` and `b′=c=mode true`. Each premise has identity security simulator and zero availability error by reflexivity. With compatibility omitted, the conclusion would compare `no-adversary(mode false)ideal` to `no-adversary(mode true)ideal`. `mode-bit` (`Examples/.../Availability.agda:169–170`) identifies these with constant false and true resources, and `tell`/`bits-separated` (`172–185`) distinguish them. Thus both premises can be perfect full constructions while the proposed conclusion fails. This is stronger than merely observing that the proof needs a missing equality.
9. **Quantifiers / independent errors.** Security is `∃s` before every test; availability has no new simulator and compares the **designated** modes. It does not follow by hiding an adversary. [INFERENCE] A sufficient additional premise is a mode-preserving security witness: if `At s ε f g` and `a∘s≈b` as grade maps, then `ctx-sub a`, `sub` functoriality and `ctx-resp` imply Avail at the same ε. Without that premise the code's security-only counterexample applies. Security and availability budgets add independently; neither need equal the other. A cap-comparison error affects only availability in this transitivity argument.
10. **Exact obligations.** Generic packaging needs only the typed definitions and the short `ctx-trans`/`at-trans`/`sub` derivation; no new semantic axioms. To advertise it as the RS-native construction, prove the exact cap/unitor/full-abstraction equivalence and transport the existing examples. Preserve grade equality versus native behavioral cap compatibility explicitly. A source-to-target transfer additionally needs the cap interpretation square; ordinary UC preservation alone does not supply it. This is the strongest genuine B candidate found in this slice, not a new native probability construction.

### PR-C1 — Lifetime random choice, independent random resources, and domain-separated slices

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Native stochastic resource constructors: source, beacon, persistent function/permutation table, independent parallel product, lifetime mixture, and injectively tagged slices. PM/RS are the immediate source/quotient consumers; SSPdist and finite TaskPIOA are prospective separate-theory consumers of a finite stochastic implementation, not already claimed QUC instances.
2. **Precise source.** M02 Defs. 1–4 (`77–115`); MPR07 Def. 7 and Lemma 3 (`173–196`); MR16 proof of Thm. 2, domain splitting (`367–379`). These are actual definitions/proof constructions, not protocol enumeration.
3. **Native signature/laws.** A source ignores inputs; a beacon returns fresh independent uniform values; a URF stores a single sampled table and repeats the same answer on repeated input; a URP has bijective persistent table. Lifetime mixture `⟨F/G⟩_Z` samples an independent unbiased selector once and thereafter uses the selected system. Independent product means separate sampled states/coins. Injective disjoint tags into a uniform function table induce independent uniform sub-tables, with total query accounting. MPR07 Lemma 3 gives the half-distance relation for the unbiased mixture under its native all-query model.
4. **Repository state/API.** PM machine state and probabilistic operations provide concrete implementation material. `CC/ND/PM/Choice.agda:51–93` is an **externally supplied**, on-demand lifetime pick: it retains the first activation, obtains one pick, initializes that branch, and keeps the branch active. It is not itself MPR07's hidden independent unbiased mixture. The scout's queued SSP choice at `ND/SSP/Choice.agda` has another lifetime. No carrier mixture follows from `UC/Quantitative.agda`. Existing game/machine-run averaging and SSP sampling laws are already A (PR-C3). RS full abstraction does not establish a representation of every M02 conditional-kernel system.
5. **Class.** **C** for these stochastic/stateful constructors; **D** for deriving them from QUC or equating independent resources with copies sharing hidden randomness; **E** for an unrestricted representation theorem from all native random systems into the current PM representation.
6. **Minimal extra data.** A chosen normalized stochastic semantics (or explicitly totalized subprobability semantics), admitted probability/seed types, initialization and persistent state, independent product/commuting separated effects, and deterministic tag/query routing. For a finite rational slice, finite distributions and table operations suffice. Do not assume arbitrary real probabilities or measurable kernels are automatically representable by the existing program datatype. For a UC consequence, additionally prove the run/test equation and ancilla closure.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** Existing PM persistent branch state is one implementation ingredient, but a native unbiased mixture additionally needs a hidden independent sampler and its joint-run law; an adversarially supplied choice does not satisfy that premise. Actual PM machine-run and SSP sampling consumers in PR-C3 witness finite stochastic-state implementations, and RS can consume behavior-respecting ones. A finite random table split by disjoint injective tags is a primary positive construction. Two interfaces to **one** RO share a table, not independent ROs; DGHM13 §5 (`224–232`) explicitly uses that distinction.
8. **Counterexamples.** [INFERENCE] F always returns 0, G always returns 1: choosing once makes two outputs equal with probability 1; resampling the choice at each call makes them differ with probability 1/2. [INFERENCE] A fair bit sampled once and copied yields only 00/11; independent draws also yield 01/10. [INFERENCE] Non-disjoint tags alias table entries, destroying slice independence. These are operational/lifetime obstructions, not failed generic composition laws.
9. **Quantifiers / errors.** Choice/seed is fixed at the specified lifetime before interaction; replacing it by test-dependent randomness changes the resource. Exact distributional equations can imply native agreement if all runs preserve them; mixture convexity of distance still needs linear observation semantics. No simulator is manufactured by probabilistic sampling, and no security reflection follows from merely forgetting hidden state.
10. **Exact obligations.** Specify lifetime, independence, table persistence and termination; prove the joint adaptive run equation, not just one-call marginals. Prove tag injectivity/disjointness and query resource accounting. Show operation respects the PM/RS quotient and all required ancillary tests. The E question requires an actual representation theorem with the admitted probability and totality class stated; finite strategy full abstraction is not that theorem.

### PR-C2 — Monotone bad flags, masking, prefix stopping and the fundamental lemma

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Native equal-until-bad proof principle, with **already generic** total/partial game-kernel fundamental lemmas, coupling hops and finite-query potential bounds, plus PM state-event/observable-transcript monitors. The reusable C target is adapters and native test/ancilla correspondence, **not a new coupling/run framework**. PM/RS are immediate representation targets; SSPdist/TaskPIOA are additional consumers only through their own kernel/scheduler semantics.
2. **Precise source.** M02 Defs. 5/7, Lemma 4 and Lemma 7/Thm. 1 (`119–140,175–179,236–246`); MPR07 Defs. 9–11 and Lemma 4 (`198–230`).
3. **Native signature/laws.** A monotone binary output starts good and becomes permanently bad; forgetting its bit yields the original system. Masking replaces ordinary replies after bad by a fixed dummy **while still answering**, not by divergence. Restricted equivalence means equality of these masked systems, including the flag law. For every admitted D, `Δ_k^D(S^−,T^−)≤Pr_D[bad by k]`, with equal bad probability on both flagged systems under the restricted-equivalence premise. Joint output-and-survival equality is the stable premise; arbitrary conditional equality alone is weaker.
4. **Repository state/API.** `CC/GamePlaying/Hop.agda:44–64,73–85,165–189` contains `StepBisim/runWith-bisim`, `JoinStep/runWith-join`, `FLGP` and `hop-bound`. `GamePlaying.agda:42–66,95–112` defines prefix `badProb`, `SuperCert`, `badProb-bounded` and projections; `.Partial.agda:47–60,94–107,147–172,238–275,323–342` supplies `badProb⊥`, `SuperCert⊥`, `FLGP⊥`, all-payoff partial/reindexed bisimulation and `hop-bound⊥`. `GamePlaying/Potential.agda:61–67,93–123,153–161` already builds rare-raise, uniform-guess, union and collision certificates. PM `UC/Machine/StateEvent.agda:44–59,84–99,118–137,165–181` supplies StateTest/EventSim/annotation/erasure; monitor agreement and hit/descent controls are in `Monitor/Agree.agda:214–250` and `StateEvent/HitTests.agda:61–106,154–172`. **Existing native-to-PM property bridges are also substantial**: `Protocol/Safety`, `StateEvent/Adequacy` and `Quantitative/EventLift` are detailed in PR-A5; do not propose their reconstruction.
5. **Class.** **A** for existing total/partial kernel proof principles, finite-query potentials, PM/protocol event adequacy, strategy-to-context lifts and ordinary approximation arithmetic. **C** only for new source-specific extraction/representation adapters outside the implemented instances or further native-to-UC security identification. **D** for exposing arbitrary hidden predicates on a behavioral quotient, identifying completed-run flag read with prefix stopping, or erasing partial mass.
6. **Exact A parameters / minimal C data.** `JoinStep` gives a joint rational draw at each related state/query, both marginal expectation laws, and support on either bad successor or equal replies/related successors. `Coupling⊥` constructs real/ideal projections of the **same partial joint kernel**, carrying the sink identically. `hop-bound⊥` additionally takes reference-run equalities and `SuperCert⊥`: invariant, nonnegative remaining-query potential φ, φ≥1 on bad, expected-step decrease, and initial bound ε. `runWith⊥-bisim` quantifies every reward `Bool→ℚ`, not only acceptance. A native adapter must give an initialized step/kernel model, correct failure/idling semantics and event correspondence; quotient descent needs a compatible state predicate or an observable-transcript event. These are concrete obligations, not “bad events preserve UC” assumed as an axiom.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** `Hop.FLGP` is an actual `JoinStep` instance at coupled projections and `s=s′ ∧ bad(s)=false`; total games embed into the partial implementation. The protocol-image `stateBounded⇒hit`/`hit⇒stateBounded` and monitor `hitsᵘ`/`hits⇒upper` are actual same-cap PM bridges, not candidate work (PR-A5). [INFERENCE] Observable transcript transformations are the right route for RS descent, unlike arbitrary hidden StateTest. SSP/TaskPIOA adapters must respect their own semantics; a disabled Task action is a successful stutter, not `prune` failure. The package slice independently records the A kernel contracts in [construction-generality-packages.md](#appendix-g), PK08.
8. **Concrete obstructions / two stopping notions.** `StateEvent/HitTests.agda:97–106` has bad-then-dead: the completed longer experiment's hit mass is zero while stopping after the visible bad prefix gives one. This does **not** contradict `GamePlaying.Partial.badProb⊥`: its definition returns one immediately upon bad, before a later failed continuation, and needs no monotonicity of the supplied predicate. Completed-run flag read and prefix-hit probability are distinct existing operations. `HitTests:154–172` gives a hidden ghost bit with an erasure simulation but no compatible predicate descent. MPR07's masking dummy is an ordinary reply, not divergence. [INFERENCE] True-only advantage zero does not identify false-returning and failed processes.
9. **Quantifiers / errors.** Coupled kernel, relation, event and potential are fixed before d. `hop-bound`/`hop-bound⊥` conclude a **Pr1 difference** at `asks≤m`, with ε(m); to obtain the repository's two-verdict approximation prove the false-event version too (for example via a justified output-negation adapter), or use all-payoff partial bisimulation for exact agreement. Prefix-hit `Upper` is not closeness to a false-returning resource. A QUC/UC conclusion still requires all admitted ancillary tests and a single specified simulator. Kernel coupling witnesses are not simulator grade maps.
10. **Exact obligations.** Reuse `FLGP⊥`, `runWith⊥-bisim`, `hop-bound⊥`, `SuperCert⊥`, the existing potential constructors and PR-A5's PM/protocol adequacy. For a genuinely new source adapter supply exact divergence/stutter semantics, initialization, event timing, both-verdict reference equations and query/potential certificates. Align initially-good native flags with PM predicates and the chosen prefix/completed observation; supply only the missing test/ancilla or security-simulator bridge. Finite-query potential bounds are not unrestricted unbounded optional-stopping theorems.

### PR-C3 — Maximal flagged extensions versus already-generic deferred sampling

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** MPR07's converse equal-until-bad construction from transcript statistical distance is a structured **maximal flagged-extension** theorem. Existing `Hop/Partial` coupling/bisimulation and both game-level and machine-run `Defer` APIs already lift supplied step laws; they must be subtracted. What remains C is producing the coherent maximal flags or a source-specific adapter, not a new generic run/deferred-sampling induction.
2. **Precise source.** MPR07 Def. 12, Lemma 5 and its common-mass proof (`240–336`); M02 adjoining-event Def. 7/Lemma 1 (`136–140`). The parent structural report separately identifies stochastic conditional/seed-factorization hypotheses; they are not bare setup laws.
3. **Native signature/laws.** For any two native normalized systems S,T, MPR07 Lemma 5 constructs flagged extensions Ŝ,T̂ preserving both marginals, equal until bad, whose bad probability equals transcript TV for **all** D and k. The construction uses common mass/minimum of the relevant prefix distributions and coherent conditional extension. It is one pair of flagged systems before all D, not an unrelated optimal coupling chosen per experiment.
4. **Repository state/API.** `CC/GamePlaying/Defer.agda:43–78` takes normalized rational seed μ, response kernels and relation `(V→StE)→StL→Type`; `runWith-avg` lifts the averaged one-step law to acceptance equality for every adaptive finite distinguisher. `GamePlaying/Defer/Run.agda:50–108` already works directly at machine `Dₚ`/`runᴹ`: `DeferStep`, `runFrom-defer`, `run-defer`, with explicit seed affineness `μ-const : ∀e.(μ >>= λ_→e)≈ₚe`. Its conclusion is `≈ₚ`, not machine-state equality `≈ᴹ`. `Hop.StepBisim/JoinStep` and `Partial.StepBisim⊥/FLGP⊥` already handle supplied coupling data (PR-C2). SSP also has `SSP/Dist/Laws.agda:32–76` sampling/defer laws and the `Samples` stochastic-state commuting reply-plus-successor-state square, mapped in the package report. None of these inspected contracts constructs MPR07's maximal flagged extension for arbitrary native systems.
5. **Class.** **A** for the existing total/partial kernel coupling, deferred averaging, machine-run Defer and SSP sampling laws within their signatures; **C** for maximal flagged-extension construction on a chosen stochastic class and source adapters; **D** for a universal efficient/computational counterpart or carrier construction from Approx; **E** for realizing the full native MPR07 theorem in the present PM representation.
6. **Minimal extra data.** MPR07 additionally needs common-submeasure/minimum, marginal-preserving supported-prefix conditionals and coherent history/state. Finite rational kernels are a concrete candidate domain. Off-support conditionals are specified only modulo the appropriate almost-sure relation. Efficiency requires an effective representation/cost proof, not merely existence. For existing Defer supply its actual averaged step law and persistent-state relation; the machine-run version also requires μ-const, which a failed/nonterminating draw need not satisfy. For SSP, use the already defined stochastic-state commuting square rather than inventing an incompatible simulation relation.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** MPR07's static finite-distribution common-mass construction (`257ff`) is a positive native case. Actual existing Defer consumers are PM `Examples/CoinToss/Ideal/Receiver/Machine.agda:229–245,347–362` (`coinₚ uniform-Bool`, `coinₚ-const`, `deferStepʰ`, `run-defer`) and SSP `Examples/SSP/OneTimePad/Dist.agda:50–81,95–122` (`defer draw` and an actual UC simulator); these exact uses are recorded by the package literature delegate. Thus both PM machine-run and SSP quotient sampling reuse are already real, not proposed new construction families. A maximal flagged extension still needs its distinct data and native/run proof.
8. **Obstruction scope.** A computationally restricted D may not implement the statistically optimal transcript decision; hence `Δ^D<δ^D` and Lemma 5 cannot yield a bad probability exactly equal to every restricted D's output advantage. Per-test couplings do not automatically assemble into one interactive flagged resource. Arbitrary QUC observations do not contain a common-mass operation. These are precise scope failures; whether a selected finite PM fragment realizes Lemma 5 efficiently is an additional research question, not a claimed impossibility.
9. **Quantifiers / errors.** Maximal native extensions are chosen before all D; existing coupling/deferred-sampling kernels, relations and seed distributions are likewise fixed before adaptive testing. `runWith-avg` proves total-game Pr1 equality, `Defer.Run` proves full machine distribution observation, and SSP's defer works at its two-sided distributional quotient. These scopes are not interchangeable. None supplies an adversarial-grade simulator or unrestricted target ancilla coverage by itself. MPR07's maximal converse remains information-theoretic, while the forward bad-event estimate can apply to computational tests.
10. **Exact obligations.** First use `StepBisim⊥`, `JoinStep`, `Defer.Run` or SSP `Samples/defer` when their step premises suffice. For the genuinely additional MPR07 construction characterize an effective kernel fragment, construct coherent marginal-preserving flags and prove equality with transcript TV. Preserve normalization/partial mass and seed affineness. Then prove native test/ancilla correspondence and a fixed security simulator independently. No duplicate coupling/run framework is justified.

### PR-C4 — Conditioning, adjoining conditions and removing adaptivity

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Native conditioning and event-adjoining proof operations, plus M02's precise adaptivity-removal criterion. PM/RS are immediate consumers only for a represented normalized/flagged fragment; finite SSPdist/TaskPIOA kernels are prospective consumers of a stochastic conditional layer.
2. **Precise source.** M02 Defs. 5–7 and Lemma 1 (`119–140`), invocation footnote 13 (`179`), Thm. 2/Cor. 1 (`254–264`).
3. **Native signature/laws.** `F|A≡G` compares each next-output conditional law given current survival A_i, only on histories where it is defined. `F^A≡G^C` compares joint next-output/survival laws and implies the corresponding conditional equality, not conversely. M02 Lemma 1 can adjoin an appropriate event to G to obtain joint flagged equality. Thm. 2 removes adaptivity in provoking bad when survival hazard satisfies `P(A_i|X^i,Y^{i−1},A_{i−1})=P(A_i|X^i,A_{i−1})`, with the paper's stronger cumulative conditional-law criterion sufficient for it.
4. **Repository state/API.** The inspected `UC/Quantitative`, `RandomSystems/{Advantage,Composition,Construction,Quantitative,Availability}`, `Machine/StateEvent`, monitor and family modules do not themselves define a general normalized conditional-kernel operator. State-event/monitor data support proof annotations but do not entail division/conditioning. Existing Approx/Test operations must not be relabelled conditioning.
5. **Class.** **C** for conditioning/event adjoining on an explicitly closed stochastic class and for adaptivity removal under the hazard premise. **D** for arbitrary wrapper preservation of conditional equality, conditioning nonexpansion, or unconditional replacement of adaptive by nonadaptive distinguishers.
6. **Minimal extra data.** Joint probability/history semantics, support-aware conditional kernels, normalized event probabilities, persistent flags, and the displayed hazard factorization. A quantitative conditioning bound additionally needs a lower bound on the conditioned event probability and control of its mismatch. A native-to-UC bridge must preserve joint trace/event laws, not just pointwise conditional responses.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** M02 input-only bad conditions satisfy its criterion; the paper gives closure/cascade variants in Cor. 1. Finite positive-probability kernels support actual conditioning. Stateful transcript-dependent hazards and zero-probability events are controls; no single arbitrary off-support conditional value is determined.
8. **Concrete counterexamples.** M02 footnote 13 explicitly states that `F|A≡G` need not imply `C(F)|Ã≡C(G)`. [INFERENCE] A small witness: F=G returns a fair first bit B and then always 0; A₁ is true and A₂ onward is `B=0`. Each next-output conditional equality holds on supported histories. A wrapper making two calls and returning the cached first bit returns 0 conditioned on inherited survival, whereas its unconditioned G version is fair. [INFERENCE] Distributions agreeing except on an event of probability η can have conditional distance 1 on that event despite unconditional distance η. [INFERENCE] A first reply exposing a random challenge followed by a guess query makes an adaptive bad-event strategy succeed with probability 1 while the best fixed guess succeeds with probability 1/2; it violates the hazard premise.
9. **Quantifiers / errors.** Conditional equality is a supported-history statement, not an all-context kernel equality. The hazard premise must hold for all relevant histories before optimizing over adaptive strategies. There is no general ε-preserving operation; conditional error may scale inversely with event probability. Event-adjoining fixes a joint extension, not a test-dependent simulator.
10. **Exact obligations.** Specify event timing/support, construct lawful conditional kernels and prove the joint extension factorization. Prove the actual hazard-independence equation before using Thm. 2. For a wrapper use stable flagged equality or prove a specific stronger conditional property; never apply generic `ctx-sub` to a normalization operation not represented by a fixed admitted converter.

### PR-C5 — Independent neutralizing amplification versus mere repetition/hybrid summation

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Native finite amplification/product construction under independence and neutralization; PM and RS can consume a represented instance, and finite distributional state models are prospective additional consumers. Existing generic hybrid arithmetic is already A; product amplification is different.
2. **Precise source.** M02 Thm. 3 (`265–281`); MPR07 Def. 13, Lemma 6, Thm. 1 (`340–416`), Cor. 1 (`418–420`), Def. 14/Cor. 2 (`430–438`), Def. 15/Cor. 3 and footnote 20 (`440–453`), Thm. 2 (`461–494`).
3. **Native signature/laws.** Independently initialized pairs (F,I),(G,J), a binary invoking construction C and query bounds k′,k″. Neutralization is `C(F,J)≡C(I,G)≡C(I,J)≡Q`. MPR07 gives `Δ_k(C(F,G),Q)≤2Δ_k′(F,I)Δ_k″(G,J)`. Lemma 6 multiplies optimal probabilities of winning two independent flagged games even under arbitrary interleaving, with separate fixed budgets. Nonadaptive form additionally uses the stated feed-forward discipline. Pointwise quasigroup combination with an independent uniform function/beacon and appropriate permutation cascades are actual instances.
4. **Repository state/API.** `CC/Approx/Rational.agda:95–99` and `UC/RandomSystems/Composition.agda:79–107` already sum hybrids; `UC/Quantitative/Witness` and measured-family composition account for simulators/additive/scaled errors. `GamePlaying/Potential.agda:61–67,110–123,153–161` already supplies mε rare-event, union and `(m²+m)2^-n` collision certificates. Those declarations do not state MPR07's multiplicative neutralization theorem. Existing event/coupling machinery is reusable implementation material; a setup-level product-amplification theorem was not established by the inspected API map.
5. **Class.** **C** for the native independent/neutralizing stochastic construction; **D** for arbitrary repetition, shared randomness, computational tests without extra closure, or omitted state restrictions. **A** for existing rare-event/union/collision certificates, finite-chain summation and polynomial bound arithmetic.
6. **Minimal extra data.** Independent product semantics, lifetime state separation, admissible invoking wrapper with per-component budgets, normalized transcript observations/optimal-decision richness, and proof of the three exact neutralization equations. For the information-theoretic product proof, also a valid flagged-extension/game interpretation (PR-C3). These are concrete operations and equations, not the desired product inequality as an axiom.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** MPR07 Cor. 2 covers independent random functions under finite quasigroup combination and general random systems against a beacon. Cor. 3 covers cascaded compatible random permutations with **G stateless**, and requires F stateless too for its bidirectional version. MPR07 footnote 20 explicitly gives a stateful least-unused-output table as a counterexample to the missing neutralization equation. These two native consumers establish the theorem's genuine reuse without enumerating protocols.
8. **Counterexamples / boundaries.** [INFERENCE] Two copies of the **same** fair bit each have zero bias, but their XOR is constant; replacing independence by shared randomness breaks a zero product bound. [INFERENCE] n independent rare-event trials versus always-zero, observed by OR, have gap `1−(1−η)^n≤nη`; repetition normally makes distinguishing easier, not harder. Unlimited repeat-until-success yields gap 1. Thus no generic ε-nonexpansive repetition/feedback theorem follows from QUC. The stateful permutation source counterexample is not merely a lack of proof.
9. **Quantifiers / errors.** Systems/neutralizer and independent randomization are fixed before D. The theorem optimizes over information-theoretic distinguishers; it is not an automatic computational amplification theorem. [INFERENCE] Iterating the product bound over r inputs yields `2^(r−1)∏ε_i` only when a neutralizing construction and correct internal query budgets exist at **every** iteration. Finite additive hybrids are always distinct from this multiplication. Polynomial repetition of a common negligible envelope remains negligible; vanishing alone or unrelated per-context envelopes do not give that conclusion.
10. **Exact obligations.** Prove independence and neutralization at the full interactive behavior level, including persistence and any inverse-query interface. Provide per-component/adaptive usage bounds and correct distinguisher closure. For PM partial processes prove normalization/totalized-event semantics before importing the native theorem. For computational transfer replace the information-theoretic optimal-flag step with an effective restricted-class argument; do not claim it for free.

### PR-C6 — Saturated resource specifications, filters and local multi-party simulators

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Native specification constructors and filter/local-simulator security. PM/RS can interpret chosen fixed-interface resources; the repository's central dummy/UC relation does not itself declare the all-corruption-pattern local-simulator structure. Finite sequential specification composition is already covered, not a new theorem.
2. **Precise source.** MR11 Def. 18/Thm. 2 (`550–600`) and Appendix C (`685–691`); MR16 §3.4 (`151–171`), Def. 1/Lemmas 1–5 (`189–229`), §6 grouping discussion (`400–406`); DGHM13 §5/Thm. 3 (`220–232`).
3. **Native signature/laws.** Resource specification R is a set of resources; right adversarial saturation `R*=RΣ` is extensive/idempotent; right-outbound means no signaling from right to left, represented by `R*=R`. The leakage relaxation includes right-outbound resources with the same left behavior. A construction πR⊆S is a set-level guarantee; a simulator equation is sufficient for an ε-relaxed saturated target, not the definition of every construction. Filtered n-specifications give guaranteed choices `Σl_i` inside unknown actual choices `A_i⊆Σ`. MR11 Thm. 2 chooses a tuple of local protocol/simulator converters before every honest subset P and compares the resulting filtered resources for every P.
4. **Repository state/API.** Generic `Abstract2` provides one adversarial-grade simulator and its composition. `RandomSystems/Construction.agda:75–170` realizes the corresponding security-only simulator clause; `Availability.agda` adds designated honest modes. `Abstract2/Regrade` and `WideSubcategory` are existing restriction tools with explicit laws, not automatic models of all filtered n-choice specifications. No all-P local-simulator interpretation theorem follows from the inspected PM/RS setup records.
5. **Class.** **A** for ordinary pointwise/set-inclusion transitivity and central UC composition already supplied. **C** for the native set-of-resources/filter algebra and a local multi-party interpretation. **D** for replacing fixed local simulators by one unconstrained central simulator or treating leakage/outbound properties as arbitrary cap erasure.
6. **Minimal extra data.** Named interface actions commuting at distinct sites, protocol/filter choices, a family of admissible corruption/honesty subsets, explicit local converter products and separation/no-communication discipline. For quantitative specifications, a compatible metric and well-defined metric-ball/saturation operations. For the local lifting in DGHM13 Thm. 3, **both protocol and simulator are stateless**, the original reduction is strong, and every projected target distinguisher belongs to the source class.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** MR16 two-interface grouping supplies a positive central-adversary interpretation; DGHM13 Thm. 3 supplies a distinct positive n-party local lifting under statelessness. Its `Ŝ_n` gives all interfaces access to the **same** internal system/randomness, while local converter instances are separate. This is not independent tensor replication of the resource.
8. **Obstruction scope.** A stateful simulator's transcript-dependent state cannot in general be split across noncommunicating local simulators while preserving a shared function table; DGHM13 explicitly explains inconsistent answers and proves memory lower bounds ruling out the desired stateless domain extension. MR11 Appendix C records the extra all-pattern requirements compared with a centralized UC formulation. This controls the invalid inference, not the validity of centralized UC itself.
9. **Quantifiers / errors.** Local security has `∃(π_i),(σ_i).∀P.∀D`, with each local σ_i fixed across subsets/tests; central `∃σ.∀D` at one grouped interface is a different assertion. Strong stateless lifting preserves the source ε under the projected-test equation. MR16's saturated-specification conclusion deliberately hides which simulator witnesses it; it need not reflect a unique simulator or preserve availability unless honest guarantees are separately retained.
10. **Exact obligations.** Supply the interface/filter action model and prove every corruption-pattern run equation, including shared-resource state and local converter independence. Show projected-test closure and any computational bounds. Relate the resulting native property to the actual setup relation in both directions only with explicit local-simulator representability/fullness. Reuse ordinary composition after these premises; do not call the central theorem a proof of the local one.

### PR-C7 — Explicit simulator resources, memory-aware reduction and reset composition

1. **Identity / consumers / status.** Resource-aware security constructors and negative controls for unrestricted indifferentiability replacement. PM/RS have stateful resources and can represent selected memory/reset devices; ITM or finite TaskPIOA are prospective second-theory consumers only after a precise resource interpretation. No current query certificate is claimed to certify native memory.
2. **Precise source.** MR16 §3.5/§4.3 (`173–185,233–245`); DGHM13 Defs. 1–2, informal Thm. 1, Thm. 2/Cor. 1 and Thm. 3 (`78–135,201–209,220–232`); RSS11 Thm. 1 and single-stage failure (`103–121`), storage audit (`137–158`), Thm. 3 (`172–178`), reset §6/Thms. 4–5 (`192–215`).
3. **Native signature/laws.** DGHM13 attaches explicit public-only s-bit memory `M_s` to the ideal resource and restricts the simulator to no retained state **between outer calls**. Its weak reduction is `∃π.∀D.∃stateless σ. Δ^D(πS,[T||M_s]σ)≤ε`; the strong version fixes σ before D. Under the stated converter/distinguisher closure, informal Thm. 1 composes with error ε₁+ε₂ and memory s₁+s₂. MR16 externalizes a non-free simulator β as a public resource β̄ with a trivial routing converter, making cost visible. RSS11 reset operation restores simulator variables to initial values; reset indifferentiability supports separate stage simulators and Thm. 4's time/query bounds.
4. **Repository state/API.** PM/RS `Constructs`/QUC `Witness` are fixed-simulator claims; `QueryBound` and `.Contextual/.Family` account for certified query rates, not bits of retained state or multistage reset equivalence. `Machine/StateEvent` demonstrates access to explicit machine state but is not a memory-size discipline. Existing generic restriction/regrading may package a proven stateless/resource subclass; they do not establish its operational closure or reset law.
5. **Class.** **C** for explicit-memory/resource/reset construction with a specified execution model. **D** for ordinary indifferentiability implying security of arbitrary multistage/memory-sensitive experiments, for query cost substituting for memory, or for silently turning weak quantifiers into fixed-simulator UC. Existing composition arithmetic is **A** only after the resource and test-class hypotheses are fulfilled.
6. **Minimal extra data.** A bit-capacity memory resource with store/read operations and invariant, a stateless-between-calls converter semantics, separated/additive resource composition, public versus honest ports, and admitted distinguisher closure. For reset, explicit initialization/reset transitions and a joint experiment semantics specifying which state/coins persist. For time/query claims, counters and per-invocation bounds. Any restriction must include the simulator as well as the adversary.
7. **Positive / negative witnesses.** MR16's externalization equation `[S,β̄]σ=Sβ` is a positive resource construction. DGHM13's memory-aware composition and RSS11 reset composition are two non-identical valid frameworks. The native storage-audit counterexample has a small-memory server store a short chaining state instead of the long message and answer later challenges; the same scheme's RO success probability is bounded in the paper. It is a control, not an arbitrary protocol census.
8. **Concrete lower bounds / failed generalizations.** DGHM13 Thm. 2, when the Fig. 2 distinguisher is admitted, r≥2 and sufficiently small ε (in particular ε<0.04), bounds domain extension by `ℓ−m ≤ s + ceil(log₂(min{s,t}))`, with the paper's convention log 0=0 and t the simulator's per-invocation ideal-query bound. Hence s=0 forbids even one-bit extension; t=1 requires at least ℓ−m retained bits. Cor. 1 extends to arbitrary-length RO queries. RSS11 Thm. 1's proof breaks for disjoint stages because one persistent simulator state is not the independently instantiated stage states; Thm. 5 rules out the stated compressing single-pass domain extenders under resets, not every conceivable resource construction.
9. **Quantifiers / errors.** DGHM13 Def. 2's `∀D∃σ` is weaker than repository `∃σ∀tests`; no reordering is licensed. Its multi-party Thm. 3 explicitly selects the strong version. RSS11 focuses on strong indifferentiability and constructs transformed adversary/distinguisher from a **fixed** simulator. Memory and distinguishing error are separate budgets; the additive memory theorem is labelled informal in the inspected edition. RSS reset composition permits a new simulator instance per stage, justified by the reset experiment, not by generic tensor copying. DGHM13 explains the stateless/reset correspondence in its own formalism (`93`); this does not identify arbitrary PM reset semantics without a bridge.
10. **Exact obligations.** Define the source resource measure and semantics first; prove simulator/stateless-class closure, externalization/routing or reset execution equations, and test backtranslation with precise overhead. Then instantiate existing restriction/query/family APIs where they fit. Prove the fixed-simulator form if a UC witness is advertised. Resource-aware availability must retain honest guarantees as well as attack upper bounds. Do not “repair” the counterexample by suppressing memory, stage, or divergence observations.

## 4. Reusable shortlist and negative-control suite

This shortlist deliberately excludes A material already supplied by UC/dummy/QUC/hybrid/family/refinement and the existing `GamePlaying/Hop/Partial/Defer`, machine-run Defer and SSP sampling APIs.

| Candidate | At least two consumers / exact reuse boundary | Required controls |
|---|---|---|
| **B: designated-cap two-budget package** (PR-B1) | PM QUC and QRS, plus both family QUC error algebras. It is genuinely arbitrary-QUC once caps are supplied. Existing native RS package remains the semantic consumer, not an absent feature. | Same or compatible middle cap; true/false mode counterexample; unit-grade versus native unitor identification; security simulator before every test. |
| **C: adapters from new native event semantics to existing partial coupling/hop APIs** (PR-C2/A5) | Actual PM/protocol event adequacy and monitor-to-context bridges are already A. Reuse them for RS where behavior respect is proved; SSPdist/TaskPIOA are distinct-theory adapter targets with their own run/schedule semantics. Do not rebuild `FLGP⊥`, `StepBisim⊥`, `SuperCert⊥`, potential constructors or PM `stateRead-hit`. | Hidden-ghost non-descent; prefix `badProb⊥` versus completed-run flag read; false versus divergence; disabled-task stuttering, activation timing and readout overhead. |
| **C: coherent maximal flags and independent neutralization premises** (PR-C3/C5) | PM/RS finite fragments and finite distributional SSPdist/TaskPIOA carriers are candidate model consumers. Native MPR07 has two genuine consumers: quasigroup random-function/beacon combination and restricted permutation cascade. Existing coupling/run/defer APIs are reused after the new data are supplied. | Shared coin versus independent product; stateful permutation footnote; output Δ versus transcript TV; one extension before all D; effective realization and partial-mass boundaries. |
| **C: explicit memory/reset resource semantics** (PR-C7) | PM/RS and a resource-measured ITM or TaskPIOA interpretation are prospective consumers, not presently claimed equivalent security models. A shared signature must retain each native activation/stage discipline. | RSS storage audit and multistage equation failure; DGHM no-memory/domain-extension lower bound; weak versus strong simulation; query rate versus retained bits. |

Additional reusable proof obligations, rather than a new universal constructor: native wrapper test absorption with measured usage (PR-A3); local-to-uniform negligible majorants if actually available (PR-A4); and all-corruption-pattern local-simulator interpretation (PR-C6). None is licensed simply by an English label such as “probabilistic”, “resource”, “context”, or “composition”.

## 5. Bottom line

1. The existing repository already covers ordinary UC/dummy composition, fixed-simulator quantitative composition, finite hybrid sums, query/rate reindexing, measured family security, vanishing/negligible collapses, refinement, total/partial coupling and fundamental-lemma hops, finite-query potential bounds, game/machine-run deferred sampling and SSP sampling laws. They are A coverage, not new work.
2. The proposed cap-availability B candidate is mathematically valid with supplied caps and compatible middle mode, but native RS already has the source-specific two-budget record/transitivity. Generic-to-native identification still needs full abstraction and the Standard2 unitor; security alone is demonstrably insufficient.
3. The genuinely additional native content is persistent stochastic-resource interpretation, conditioning/coherent maximal-flag construction, source adapters to existing coupling/run APIs, independent neutralization/amplification premises, local filtered specifications, and explicit resource/reset semantics. These require concrete operations and run/test laws; QUC's test metric cannot supply them. Existing generic finite-game proof principles must not be rebuilt.
4. The crucial distinctions are fixed simulator before tests/slack; output versus transcript advantage; total replies versus PM missing verdict mass; independent versus shared state; query/time/memory budgets; local negligible versus closure-uniform-within-test versus globally uniform negligible; arbitrary negligible witnesses versus a fixed decay template; and negligible versus merely vanishing rates.
5. Unresolved representation/efficient-realization questions are identified narrowly as E. No unsupported global literature-completeness, new code verification, or blanket native-model equivalence is claimed.

Neither was independently checked. **No repository edits, builds, typechecks, tests, linters, formatters or Git validation were run.** “Present” below means declarations/bodies inspected, not newly verified.

Paths beginning `S/` abbreviate `src/CategoricalCrypto/`; `P/` abbreviates `docs/kb/sources/papers/` in that root. Structural controls are [construction-generality-structural-evidence.md](#appendix-d). New mathematical claims/proposals are marked **[INFERENCE]**.
