## 2. Equality, failure and security dictionary

1. `Calls._≈ᶜ_` is structural tree equality, including coin constructors; `Protocol._≲ᵖ_` is a **deterministic state map** preserving initialization and that tree relation, and `≈ᵖ` is its equivalence closure (`S/SSP/Protocol.agda:37–62,166–183`). A change of sampling activation need not satisfy it.
2. `≈Mℚ` is expectation/distribution equality for the rational distribution representation. `Dist⊥ X = Dist-ℚ (Maybe X)` is normalized total mass with explicit `nothing` cemetery outcome (`src/ProbabilisticLogic/Distribution/RationalDist/Partial.agda:3–5,45–57`). The induced successful mass is a finite subdistribution. It is not the same representation as CryptHOL's real `spmf`, TaskPIOA's total trace distributions, or machine `Dₚ`.
3. `≈ᵏ` compares open call trees under **every** imported oracle and initial oracle state, preserving the joint result/final-oracle-state distribution (`S/SSP/Dist/Kernel.agda:44–76`). Equal single-call answer marginals are weaker.
4. `≈ℰ` is the bare kernel of the chosen test presheaf. `≈ᵁ` additionally quantifies ancillary grades. `≈ᵈ` is specifically the two-sided monoidal contextual kernel `∀Y Z. (idY⊗P)⊗idZ ≈ℰ (idY⊗Q)`, not a synonym for arbitrary native stochastic bisimilarity (`S/SSP/Dist.agda:32–72`; `S/Standard2/UKernel.agda:34–35`). There are proved implications `≈ᵖ⇒≈ᵈ⇒≈ᵁ`; converse claims require their own evidence.
5. SSP `Approximation⊥` bounds **each** Boolean verdict probability, not only `Pr[true]` (`S/SSP/Observation.agda:45–89`). Exact equality of both masses preserves failure, since failure is their complement. **[INFERENCE]** Bounds ε for each verdict give a failure-mass bound at most `2ε`, not automatically ε. A true-only comparison can identify `return false` with total failure; it therefore cannot be substituted for this observation. CH20/SS23 exact couplings preserve total successful mass; BP17/H17 coupling existence likewise requires equal mass.
6. The setup UC quantifiers are `∀ attacker a:X→X′. ∃ simulator s:Y→X′. sub(a) f ≈ᵁ sub(s) g`; U then ranges over all ancillary grades/tests. Dummy completeness yields a single `s₀:Y→X` before those contexts and answers arbitrary `a` by `a∘s₀` (`S/Abstract2.agda:133–172`). A state map/coupling is **not** that simulator. Native game reduction instead transforms the distinguisher, `D=A∘M`.

Current arrow boundary:

- SSP → SSPdist: formal setup morphism and **preservation plus reflection** of source UC order.
- SSP → PM[wb]: direct strong-monoidal/setup compiler plus density-based preservation.
- SSPdist → PM[wb]: quotient-descended compiler plus preservation; its composite with SSP→SSPdist agrees **pointwise in the listed components** with the direct compiler.
- W/full → W/local → PM[wb]: formal observation/quotient maps; explicit Full-to-local/quotient U/UC preservation.
- W/full → ordinary PM: explicit inclusion U/UC preservation.
- There is **no resulting SSP/SSPdist→ordinary-PM UC transfer**, no reverse PM[wb]→W/full theorem, and no arbitrary-target-simulator reflection theorem for the SSP compiler.

## 3. Ten-field construction rows

### PK01 — Existing category, setup and UC composition coverage

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Typed composition, identity, tensor, standard setup, dummy completeness and UC composition for all five declared nodes. Operational interpretation is separated into PK02/PK11.
2. **Primary contract.** B18 Defs4–6/Lemmas1–4 and Lemma6 ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-03332-3_9), `P/brzuska2018.pdf:109–145,185–190`); CC19 §IV-C attachment/identity/interchange ([publication](https://crypto.ethz.ch/publications/LSBM19.html), `:182–195`).
3. **Native signature/laws.** Interface-compatible packages/converters compose; independent parallel juxtaposition has typing/separation premises; identity forwards legal calls. Associativity and typed interchange are algebraic laws, not a theorem that all raw code/context semantics has those laws.
4. **Repo state.** `S/SSP/Protocol.agda:116–159`, `Protocol/Monoidal.agda:40–104`; named `StdUC` instances in the node table. `S/Abstract2.agda:133–221` provides dummy/security composition. These are present.
5. **Class. A.** This is existing generic coverage, **not a new construction candidate**.
6. **Actual instantiation.** `Standard2.StdUC` receives `(Protᴹ,ℰᵖ)`, `(Protᵈ,ℰᵈ)`, `(𝒲ᴹ,ι*ℰᵒ)`, `(𝒲ᴹ,ℰᵂ)`, and `(PM[wb]ᴹ,ℰ[wb])`. `UC-compose` uses each resulting setup; no blanket `GradeStable` premise is needed for that composition theorem.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** SSP native tensor and PM[wb] quotient tensor; concrete `Examples/SSP/OneTimePad/Dist.agda:99–122` uses dummy completeness with `sim`, attacker response `a∘ᵖsim` and every ancilla/test/game. Arbitrary `UCSetup` itself need not have a base tensor.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The raw compiler's identity failure (PK13) is not repaired merely by invoking category axioms. Shared-global-state programs cannot be silently interpreted as the independent-state tensor (PK02).
9. **Security/error.** One simulator precedes all contexts; composition combines witnesses by the existing grade operations. These qualitative theorems do not construct probability distributions, query bounds or negligible rates.
10. **Remaining obligations.** None for this already supplied algebra. New native representations must separately prove identity/composition/tensor, observation naturality and relevant runtime/typing closure before using the factory.

### PK02 — Operational linking, renaming, framing, shared imports and separated state

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Package inlining, typed oracle relabeling, private-state renaming, frame extension and independent parallel state. Present source operations in SSP; general reuse across SSP/TaskPIOA/IPDL/machines is structured, not universal.
2. **Primary contract.** B18 Defs1–6/Lemmas1–4, `P/brzuska2018.pdf:91–145` ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-03332-3_9)); SS23 §§3.3–3.4, `:511–536` ([HAL](https://hal.science/hal-04273257v1)); CH20 §5.8, `:573–584` ([ePrint](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/753)); CC19 Def2, `:153–163` ([publication](https://crypto.ethz.ch/publications/LSBM19.html)).
3. **Native contract.** B18 linking is capture-avoiding inlining with matching imports/exports and disjoint renamed package state; parallel exports and state are disjoint, but imports may be shared. SS23 instead links free-monad `call p a k` by `bind(P.p a)(link k)` over one global memory; its syntactic interchange remains valid even with shared locations. CH20 distinguishes shared-state oracle dispatch `+O` from independent product-state `||`; CC19 explicitly warns `RES(O1+O O2;s) ≠ RES(O1;s)||RES(O2;s)`.
4. **Repo state.** SSP `graft/serve`, paired-state composition and channel-owned tensor are in `S/SSP/Protocol.agda:116–159`; container maps/reindexing and fold `∇` in `SSP/Iface.agda:32–76`, `Protocol.agda:49–54`. Shared imports are wired using `(P⊗P′)∘⌜∇⌝`; the hub is **not independent duplication of a stateful resource**. State-map simulations provide pure renaming/framing witnesses. TaskPIOA `product-simulation` is concrete independent-product closure (`Simulation.agda:61–97`).
5. **Class. C** for native state/locality realization; **A** for the resulting monoidal algebra; **D** for unrestricted conversion of shared state into independent tensor. No second generic machine constructor is proposed.
6. **Minimal extra data.** Typed boundaries and substitution/reindexing; state/init/step representation; state-product coherence; either disjoint ownership with frame laws or an explicit shared-memory semantics and footprint-stable invariants. Injective renaming must transport initialization and accesses. Runtime fresh allocation needs additional namespace/allocation laws, not just binary tensor.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** SSP separate product states and TaskPIOA paired-state products. SS23 global-memory linking is a positive different convention, not an invalid implementation. IPDL persistent cells are not ordinary mutable SSP cells.
8. **Concrete obstruction. [INFERENCE]** Commands `x:=0` and `x:=1` on the **same** cell end in different states under opposite execution orders; framing by an aliased cell is unsound. Two private cells do not have that conflict. This refutes semantic independent-state interchange/splitting, **not SS23's syntactic package interchange**.
9. **Security/error.** Pure renaming/frame equality is an exact run/hom proof. To use a stable relational invariant against an adversary, its state footprint must be disjoint as in SS23 Thm2.4. Reusing one key/state across two sessions is not an automatic tensor-security theorem.
10. **Obligations.** Prove the native handler/inlining square, initializer transport, private-write/read frame laws and tensor state locality; prove closure under admitted adversaries. Do not import B18 alpha-equivalence into SS23's literal global memory or infer unbounded session allocation from finite indexed copies.

### PK03 — Existing reduction, exact game bridge and hybrid arithmetic

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Associative game reduction, finite additive game hops and closed-game-to-U bridge in SSP; generic arithmetic available to the quantitative observation instance.
2. **Primary contract.** B18 Def8/Lemmas5–6, `P/brzuska2018.pdf:175–190` ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-03332-3_9)); SS23 Def2.1/Lemmas2.2–2.3, `:103–155` ([HAL](https://hal.science/hal-04273257v1)).
3. **Native signature.** Given matching `M` and game pair `G`, for each adversary `A`, `Pr1[A∘(M∘Gb)] = Pr1[(A∘M)∘Gb]`. A chain of game differences is bounded by the sum of adjacent differences. The paper advantage is true-only; the repository's partial Boolean observation is stronger.
4. **Repo state.** `S/SSP/Reduction.agda:43–64`: `_≈ᵍ[_]_`, `≈ᵍ-trans`, `reduction`, `≈ᵍ-reduce`; `:105–121`: `closed-bridge`, `≈ᵍ⇒≈ᵁ`. `S/SSP/Observation.agda:69–86` supplies `Approximation⊥`; `S/Approx/Rational.agda:91–99` supplies the parameterized finite `hybrid` theorem.
5. **Class. A.** Already present; not a proposed new hybrid framework.
6. **Actual instantiation.** SSP game transitivity explicitly uses `Approximation⊥.≈[]-trans`. `Approx.Rational.hybrid` can be applied with this existing approximation record; that is a valid specialization, not a claim that `Reduction` imports the named `hybrid` declaration. The closed bridge uses the standard monoidal closing equation and the trivial source unit closure, not a PM `GradeStable` assumption.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** SSP perfect-game proofs enter U via `≈ᵍ⇒≈ᵁ`; the OTP distribution proof alternatively enters via `≈ᵈ⇒≈ᵁ`. A fixed one-call ε bound is not an entire adaptive-lifetime bound.
8. **Obstruction. [INFERENCE]** Repeating independent success-probability ε trials n times can expose difference `1−(1−ε)^n`, so a converter making n uses cannot generally retain ε. Arithmetic alone does not prove that it made at most n uses.
9. **Security/error.** Reduction changes the distinguisher, not a UC simulator; `≈ᵍ⇒≈ᵁ` is a proved **perfect closed-game** bridge. Both Boolean probabilities are bounded by ε in `_≈ᵍ[ε]_`; failure is not silently identified with false. Asymptotic negligibility needs a specified index and polynomial/other admissible usage closure.
10. **Obligations.** None for the existing rules. A quantitative all-context compiler theorem would additionally need native query/usage bounds and nonexpansiveness; the qualitative closed bridge is not evidence of such a theorem.

### PK04 — Indexed multi-instance reduction and randomized hybrid selector

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Finite fresh package copies and a random-index reduction; SSP/SSPdist are candidate realizations, with finite tensor/relabeling algebra already present.
2. **Primary contract.** B18 Defs15–16/Lemmas8–9, `P/brzuska2018.pdf:334–370` ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-03332-3_9)).
3. **Native signature.** Rename exported/all oracle names by instance i; combine n disjoint copies. The reduction chooses `j` uniformly in `1..n`, fills positions before j with one game and after j with the other, exposing the single challenge at j. For n>0, multi-instance advantage is at most `n·Adv_M(A∘R)`; the n=0 case is handled separately rather than sampling an empty set.
4. **Repo state.** SSP sum-channel tensor/container wiring and normalized rational coins exist (`SSP/Iface.agda:32–76`; `Protocol.agda:37–62,152–159`). Generic hybrid arithmetic is PK03. A named implementation of the complete B18 random-index multi-instance reduction was not found in the mapped SSP modules inspected; no claim of whole-repository absence.
5. **Class. C** for the selector/operational reduction; **A** for finite tensor/coherence and additive arithmetic already available.
6. **Minimal extra data.** Finite indexed namespace embeddings; independent state allocation for each copy; a realizable normalized uniform index draw; interface routing at selected j; experiment closure and a cost bound for the reduction. For asymptotics, n(k) and the reduction's costs must preserve the intended adversary class.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** SSP finite rational coins and finite channel sums; IPDL finite persistent networks provide another finite naming/state domain but their general open linker is not supplied by this construction. A single reused key store is not n independent key stores.
8. **Obstruction. [INFERENCE]** Reusing a one-bit OTP key for two messages exposes their XOR; independent keys do not. This is a correlation/session-allocation obstruction, not a failure of abstract monoidal composition.
9. **Security/error.** The native reduction is one randomized adversary transformer, not n existential simulators selected after the environment. The n factor is concrete. Nothing in `UCSetup` supplies uniform randomness or polynomial closure.
10. **Obligations.** Build indexed copies and the selected-slot reduction, prove its exact experiment/distribution equation, then reuse hybrid arithmetic. Prove joint-state freshness and query/runtime bounds; do not count the abstract tensor itself as new work.

### PK05 — Exact relational lifting, stable invariants and state-map specialization

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Coupling-based relational reasoning and stable package invariants; existing rational/state-map proof APIs cover substantial pieces, while native cross-carrier adapters remain structured work.
2. **Primary contract.** CH20 §4.4/Lemma1 and §5.5, `P/lochbihler2020crypthol.pdf:270–291,523–538` ([ePrint](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/753)); SS23 Thms2.4/4.1 and §5.2, `:166–170,629–710` ([HAL](https://hal.science/hal-04273257v1)); H17 Def2.1.7/Props2.1.9–10, `P/hsu2017.txt:493–546` ([thesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.09951)).
3. **Native signature/laws.** `Lift R μ ν` means a joint subdistribution with exact μ/ν marginals supported on R. Equality lifts to distribution equality; return/bind and relation composition yield compositional proofs. SS23 requires an initially true relational memory predicate invariant for every exported procedure and same argument, equal returned values, and stability under simultaneous equal updates outside the games' footprints; disjoint adversaries then have zero advantage.
4. **Repo state.** `GamePlaying/Hop.agda:44–59` has expectation-based `StepBisim/runWith-bisim`; `GamePlaying/Partial.agda:238–275` adds partial kernels, alphabet maps and **all Bool→ℚ payoffs**. `SSP/Dist/Kernel.agda:239–245,285–295` has stochastic-map `Samples`, `sample-⟦⟧`, protocol `_⊑_`; `Dist/Laws.agda:73–76` gives deterministic `stepwise`. TaskPIOA `Simulation.agda:19–24,47–97` is deterministic state-map/independent-product closure, not the whole coupling relation. Machine `Machines/Sim.agda:39–56` likewise uses a pure state map.
5. **Class. A** for the existing parameterized game/partial/sampling APIs; **C** only for native adapters, general relational product-locality witnesses and observation bridges. No new generic coupling core is justified.
6. **Minimal extra data/instantiation.** Existing `StepBisim⊥` takes kernels `S→Q→Dist⊥(S×R)` and a state relation; `Samples` takes normalized `ρ:S→Distℚ T` with an exact **joint reply/next-state** commuting square. A deterministic θ specializes ρ to a Dirac draw, as the existing SSP `stepwise` construction demonstrates. A general coupling relation is not automatically a stochastic map in either direction.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** SSP's existing stochastic sampling simulation and TaskPIOA's deterministic `tick-match` are concrete two-consumer candidates for an adapter. IPDL's finite `turn` kernels fit the typed SSP interface already. SS23 stable global-memory predicates are a different representation of locality.
8. **Obstruction.** Exact coupling forces equal successful mass (H17 Prop2.1.9). **[INFERENCE]** `return false` and failure have equal true probability but cannot have such an exact coupling. Matching per-call answer marginals without correlated successor state does not imply adaptive equivalence (PK07's hidden-bit example).
9. **Security/error.** A relational invariant yields run equality only over its legal context class. Existing SSP `_⊑_⇒≈ᵈ` carries two-sided contexts (`Dist.agda:32–72`); existing Task security has its own wiring/test closure (`Security.agda:53–79`). Neither state relation is a UC simulator. Exact all-payoff partial equality preserves both verdict masses and failure.
10. **Obligations.** Supply typed-interface and native-run adapters, initialization, frame/product closure and admitted-test adequacy; then use existing finite-run theorems. For stochastic Task relations, additionally prove input/receiving and wired-product closure and retain its fixed schedule discipline. The precise reusable target is in §4, not a replacement framework.

### PK06 — Proof-relevant product programs and relational state transformers

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Construct a program denoting a coupling, rather than only prove a relation; SSProve-style relational logic/state-transformer reuse for SSP-like programs. Source literature construction, not a repository-wide implemented product-program generator.
2. **Primary contract.** BP17 Def5, RAND/SEQ/while rules, Thms7–8 (`P/barthe2017.txt:158–182,211–286,374,444–452`, [arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/1607.03455)); SS23 §5.2–5.3/Def5.1/Thm5.5 (`P/ssprove2023.pdf:692–818`, [HAL](https://hal.science/hal-04273257v1)).
3. **Native signature.** A valid ×pRHL judgment supplies a product program on **disjoint tagged stores** whose output distribution has the two source semantics as marginals and satisfies the postcondition. RAND supplies an actual joint sampler; SEQ composes joint computations. Asynchronous/one-sided loops need the stated termination/losslessness premises. SS23's relational semantics uses a lax relational effect observation and a lawful state transformation, with potentially distinct left/right state types.
4. **Repo state.** Existing `JoinStep` explicitly carries a joint rational draw and marginal/support laws (`S/GamePlaying/Hop.agda:73–85`). SSP is a finite call-tree language with state; it does not currently expose the inspected Coq syntax/logic or a ×pRHL product-program extraction API in the mapped modules.
5. **Class. C.** Existing joint-step machinery is A coverage, but proof-program extraction and a native relational logic require more structure than UC/QUC.
6. **Minimal extra data.** Chosen source language and effect semantics, disjoint paired store representation, a joint sampler for each probabilistic rule, return/bind laws, pre/postcondition semantics and loop admissibility. A numerical error on tests is not a coupling monad or a syntax-directed proof system.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** SSP finite call trees and IPDL finite reaction/strategy trees are potential proof-language consumers, but their persistence/interaction semantics differ. `JoinStep` is a present joint-kernel witness, not evidence that arbitrary two programs admit a coupling program.
8. **Obstruction.** BP17's one-sided loop rules cannot drop losslessness: a terminating weight-1 computation and a weight-0 divergent computation cannot be exact marginals of one coupling. Thm8 is relative completeness for deterministic programs, **not arbitrary probabilistic-program completeness**.
9. **Security/error.** A constructed coupling proves the stated event/run relation. To obtain source UC, close under legal adversaries and ancillas; to obtain compiler security, add native adequacy. H17's `(ε,δ)` approximate lifting uses two witnesses and asymmetric `dε`, not automatically QUC's symmetric additive approximation.
10. **Obligations.** Decide whether an adapter to existing `StepBisim⊥/JoinStep/Samples` suffices before adding proof syntax. If product extraction is wanted, prove semantics/marginals and each source instruction rule, including failure and loops, then an observation bridge. No unrequested new abstract probability hierarchy is needed.

### PK07 — Existing deferred/lazy/optimistic sampling, with joint-state discipline

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Sampling swaps, normalized dead-sample removal, bijection-based optimistic sampling, and movement across activations. Substantial generic and SSP-specific APIs already exist.
2. **Primary contract.** SS23 uniform/dead-sample rules (`P/ssprove2023.pdf:577–599`, [HAL](https://hal.science/hal-04273257v1)); CH20 §4.5 optimistic sampling and §5.5 hoisting warning (`P/lochbihler2020crypthol.pdf:322–340,565`, [ePrint](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/753)); CC19's lazy random-function example is identified as `trace_eq_lazy` in its primary formal-source guide (`:488`, [publication](https://crypto.ethz.ch/publications/LSBM19.html)).
3. **Native contract.** Uniform sampling commutes with a finite bijection; dropping an unused draw requires normalization. Moving sampling past an oracle call requires its query/continuation independence and an invariant for persistent joint state. Lazy and eager tables must represent the same **joint** answers on repeated/adaptive queries, not merely the same answer marginal once.
4. **Repo state.** `S/GamePlaying/Defer.agda:43–78`: `AvgBisim`, `runWith-avg` for normalized rational seed μ and relation `(V→StE)→StL`; it lifts an averaged one-query law to every finite adaptive distinguisher. `GamePlaying/Defer/Run.agda:50–108`: `DeferStep`, `runFrom-defer`, `run-defer` at machine `Dₚ`/`runᴹ`, with explicit seed affineness `μ-const`, concluding `≈ₚ`, not `≈ᴹ`. `SSP/Dist/Laws.agda:32–76`: `coins-comm`, `coin-call`, `coin-comm`, `coin-drop`, `coin-flip`, `coin-xor`, `defer`, `stepwise`.
5. **Class. A** for these already generic laws, **C** for adapters/source-specific adequacy or broader native table constructions; **D** for marginal-only or nonterminating-draw generalizations. This is **not a wholly missing lazy-sampling framework**.
6. **Exact existing parameters/consumer.** `AvgBisim` compares expectations under μ of response kernels at a related family of early states and one late state; answers in related families must agree across the secret. `Defer.Run` additionally requires `(μ >>= λ_→e)≈ₚe`. SSP `defer` takes `ρ:StP→Coins(StP′)`, a Dirac-initialization law and the open `≈ᵏ` per-step square. Actual consumer: `Examples/SSP/OneTimePad/Dist.agda:50–81` instantiates `defer draw`, where `draw(sent m)` samples ciphertext c and stores `(c,c xor m)`; `:95–122` obtains source and quotient UC with the actual `sim`.
   An actual machine-run consumer is `S/Examples/CoinToss/Ideal/Receiver/Machine.agda:229–245,347–362`: `μʰ=coinₚ uniform-Bool`, `fs≋ʰs := ∀v.fs(v)=canonʰ(s,v)`, and `deferStepʰ` instantiate `run-defer` with `eagerᶜʰ`, `deferᶜʰ` and `coinₚ-const uniform-Bool`; `coin-runʰ` composes this exact run agreement with the two machine-simulation legs. This is direct inspected API use, not merely a possible specialization.
   `S/GamePlaying/Test.agda:110–172` supplies an actual `AvgBisim/runWith-avg` instance: `DeferMachine k`, `μ=uniform-Vec k`, `St=Maybe Pt×Log`, and `respD` stores a point on first `readQ` while `logQ` never consults it. The relation is either a family `(just p,L)` against `(nothing,L)` at one common log, or a constant family; `avg-bisim:147–166` and `defer:169–172` instantiate the generic API directly.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** The OTP lazy-state consumer and IPDL's persistent hidden-bit linker (`S/IPDL/Linker.agda:83–131`) retain shared sampled state. The machine-run API is a different existing level, not an SSP-to-PM setup theorem.
8. **Counterexamples. [INFERENCE]** A fair bit stored and returned twice yields `(0,0)/(1,1)` with probability 1/2 each; independently resampling per read gives unequal answers with probability 1/2. A two-query equality tester distinguishes them although each one-read marginal is fair. SS23's null-draw example refutes discarding unused failure; machine `botₚ` similarly violates `μ-const`. Pure state-map equality cannot in general move a point state to a mixture; the machine-run file explicitly records this boundary (`:12–20`).
9. **Security/error.** Exact normalized sampler equations preserve failure of surrounding computations; they never normalize failure away. `runWith-avg` states Pr1 equality for total game kernels; `Defer.Run` states machine distribution observation, and SSP `defer` states two-sided `≈ᵈ`. None alone supplies a simulator or allows replacing W-local contexts by hostile PM contexts.
10. **Obligations.** Reuse the appropriate existing API; prove initialization, joint output/successor-state relation and seed lifetime, then source-native run/test/ancilla correspondence. For infinite tables or countable adaptive interactions, prove the appropriate measure/continuity/termination theorem rather than extrapolate the finite induction.

### PK08 — Existing identical-until-bad, coupling hops and query potentials

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Fundamental lemma, reference-kernel hop, separate-state join coupling and bad-event potentials. Generic finite-game and partial-kernel APIs are present; automatic SSP/W semantic adapters are not asserted.
2. **Primary contract.** CH20 Lemma2 and Prop2, `P/lochbihler2020crypthol.pdf:294–306,546–563`, plus Lemma3 `:586–599` ([ePrint](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/753)); H17 Thm2.1.16 (`:629–664`, [thesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.09951)).
3. **Native contract.** A coupling with equal bad flags and equal event outcomes off bad bounds the event-probability difference by bad probability. Oracle bisimulation up to bad additionally maintains a state relation, persistent bad and post-bad losslessness; the source Prop2's adversary is terminating/lossless. Per-relevant-call bad probability k and at most n calls yield kn. Equal-mass coupling yields the usual mismatch bound; unequal successful mass needs explicit cemetery completion/accounting.
4. **Repo state.** `S/GamePlaying/Hop.agda:44–64,73–85,165–189` gives `StepBisim`, `JoinStep/runWith-join`, `FLGP`, `hop-bound`. `GamePlaying/Partial.agda:94–107,147–172,238–275,323–342` gives `SuperCert⊥`, `badProb⊥-bounded`, `Coupling⊥.FLGP⊥`, `StepBisim⊥ʳ/runWith⊥-bisimʳ`, `StepBisim⊥/runWith⊥-bisim`, `hop-boundᵇ⊥`, `hop-bound⊥`. Present, not proposed.
   Further existing A APIs: `S/GamePlaying.agda:56–98` gives total `SuperCert`, `prune-cert` and `badProb-bounded`. `GamePlaying/Potential.agda:61–67,93–103,110–123,153–161` gives `RareRaise/rare-cert` (mε), fresh-uniform `guess-drift/drift-∧`, additive `∨-cert`, and `KeepOrSample/collision-cert` ((m²+m)·2⁻ⁿ); actual adaptive-bound instances are `GamePlaying/Test.agda:71–76,100–105`. The fresh draw premise is essential: an already fixed, state-known secret can be guessed with probability1.
   Existing **flat Protocol→machine event adapters**, not missing bridges: `S/Protocol/Safety.agda:48–81` instantiates `HitCert=SuperCert⊥(kernel P)` and proves `hit≤badProb⊥`, `super`, `hit-bounded`. `UC/Machine/StateEvent/Adequacy.agda:185–209` proves `stateRead-hit`, `stateBounded⇒hit`, `hit⇒stateBounded` at the same cap; `:216–236` supplies family/saturated forms. These concrete APIs are A coverage; adapting the typed SSP/open-oracle or Task/IPDL semantics remains a different task.
5. **Class. A** for these parameterized proof principles; **C** for native partial-kernel extraction, event correspondence and source U bridges.
6. **Actual instantiations.** `Hop.FLGP` instantiates `JoinStep` with the two projections of `respB` and relation `s=s′ ∧ bad(s)=false` (`Hop.agda:142–178`). In `Coupling⊥`, `realK/idealK` are `Dmap⊥` projections of the **same** partial joint kernel; the sink is propagated identically. `hop-bound⊥` accepts separately proved reference-run equalities plus a `SuperCert⊥` containing invariant, nonnegative potential, bad lower bound, expected-step decrease and initial bound.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** Total `Hop` and partial `Partial` are actual existing specializations. SSP closed response kernels and PM pruned machine activations are possible adapters; Task disabled tasks must remain successful stutters, not be fed to `prune` as failed responses.
8. **Obstruction. [INFERENCE]** Setting bad permanently false while one side returns false and the other fails cannot justify full partial-observation equality from a true-only advantage bound. Treating failure as an unobserved bad event erases an availability distinction. CH20 Prop2 explicitly excludes uncontrolled post-bad nontermination.
9. **Security/error.** Existing hop conclusions bound **Pr1**; to match SSP/W's two verdict masses one must prove the corresponding false event too (e.g. a justified output-negation adapter) or use all-payoff partial bisimulation for exact equality. `asks≤m`/potential bounds are explicit; no fixed-ε lifetime claim follows. The kernel/coupling certificate is chosen before d, but it is not a UC simulator.
   **Completed versus prefix hits:** `badProb⊥` stops immediately at a bad state; `Protocol.Observe.PrHit` and the machine `stateRead` report accumulated badness only when the experiment completes. A later divergence therefore makes completed-hit mass0 even after a hit. This distinction is implemented, not conjectural: `UC/Machine/StateEvent/HitTests.agda:97–106` gives `badThenDead`, two-ask reading0 and one-ask-prefix reading1. `StateEvent/Read.agda:48–78` defines the flag reader and its extra flag query; `StateEvent.agda:56–59,165–181` requires an `EventSim` preserving the predicate. `HitTests.agda:154–172` proves an erased ghost-bit predicate cannot descend along an otherwise valid machine simulation. Thus state-event instrumentation is not automatically invariant under unannotated machine equality.
   Existing event-bound transport must also be subtracted from new work: `S/UC/Audit.agda:113–135` defines event-class `Absorbs` and `audit-carry`; a rated simulator plus absorption transports an ideal one-sided bound ε to the real bound `q ↦ ε(scale q r)+δ` for every strictly positive δ. This uses the actual observation-mass/slack contract, not a claim that every source event or query grade is automatically compatible.
10. **Obligations.** Prove native kernel extraction with exact divergence/idling semantics; establish marginal/reference equations for both verdicts; supply query/potential certificate; prove admitted context/test and ancilla coverage. Reuse `FLGP⊥` rather than add a duplicate fundamental lemma.

### PK09 — Existing two-sided contextual quotients and SSP-order reflection

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Observation-respecting monoidal quotient for SSPdist and PM[wb]; source-to-distribution UC preservation/reflection.
2. **Primary context.** CC19 Defs4–5/Thms1–2 distinguish operational state proofs from contextual trace equality (`P/lochbihler2019cc.pdf:227–298`, [publication](https://crypto.ethz.ch/publications/LSBM19.html)). The **exact two-sided quotient construction below is repository evidence**, not attributed as a theorem of that paper.
3. **Signature/laws.** Given monoidal M and a test presheaf E, define `f∼g := ∀Y Z. (idY⊗f)⊗idZ≈ℰ(idY⊗g)⊗idZ`; prove composition and tensor congruence, quotient M and descend E. This is stronger structural data than an arbitrary UC setup's bare kernel.
4. **Repo state.** `S/Standard2/UKernel.agda:34–35,61–125`; SSP wrapper/congruence `S/SSP/Dist.agda:32–72`; quotient/test/morphism and preservation/reflection `S/SSP/Dist/Setup.agda:26–72`; W quotient/test descent `S/UC/Bracket/Setup.agda:80–89,150–157`.
5. **Class. A.** Existing generic quotient with actual consumers; no new quotient proposal.
6. **Actual instantiations.** `UKernel Protᴹ ℰᵖ` yields `congᵈ` and `Protᵈ`; `UKernel 𝒲ᴹ ℰᵂ` yields PM[wb]. SSP→SSPdist uses identity object/representative maps and descended tests. Its reverse UC result uses the unchanged prefix contexts and monic test comparison (`dist≈ᵁ⇒ssp≈ᵁ`, `dist≤⇒ssp≤`, `reflects-≤UC`), not an unproved source GradeStable property.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** Both assigned quotients are actual consumers. `Examples/SSP/OneTimePad/Dist.agda:124–129` reflects a hypothetical quotient security proof to the source `leaky-not-secure`, giving an actual negative-security consumer of reflection.
8. **Obstruction.** A bare observation relation need not be tensor/ancilla closed. Conversely, quotienting by a chosen observation does not make a raw compiler respect identity under a **different**, more discriminating target observation.
9. **Security/error.** SSP and SSPdist have the same UC preorder on their protocol representatives by the explicit two directions, despite different hom equality. This is not an assertion that hom equality equals UC emulation, or that PM[wb] reflects all source security. No new fixed-error quantitative quotient theorem is claimed.
10. **Obligations.** Already discharged for these consumers. Any further quotient must prove the two-sided congruence and test descent; reflection requires its own context/simulator argument, not merely a quotient functor.

### PK10 — Existing W observation restriction, quotient map and full-PM inclusion

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** W/full, W/local and PM[wb] are distinct observation/quotient constructions over the well-bracketed operational source.
2. **Primary comparison.** CC19 Defs1/3 respectfulness and Thm2's quantification over respectful distinguishers (`P/lochbihler2019cc.pdf:123–175,288–298`, [publication](https://crypto.ethz.ch/publications/LSBM19.html)); CH20's legal oracle/GPV interfaces ([ePrint](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/753), §5). Exact W facts come from inspected source, not an asserted source-model identity.
3. **Signature.** Keep W computations fixed, change tests from full pulled-back PM observations to WB-local observations; then quotient the local two-sided kernel. Separately include W/full into ordinary PM with full observations.
4. **Repo state.** `S/UC/Bracket.agda:50–74` defines idle/serving/calling legality. `ObservationRestriction.agda:56–79,103–187` supplies `restrict-test`, `full-test`, `ν-restrict`, `restriction⇒wb`, `full⇒PM[wb]` and one-way U/UC results. `InclusionTransfer.agda:39–62` supplies essential-surjectivity coverage and full-PM preservation.
5. **Class. A.** Actual generic reobservation/refinement/setup-transfer instances, not new native models.
6. **Actual instantiation.** `Abstract2.Morphism.Refine` is applied to the same W computational fields and the full⇒local bare-kernel implication. `Standard2.Morphism.surjective⇒preserves-≈ᵁ` is used for W/full inclusion: singleton-channel representations cover PM objects. PM GradeStable is not assumed.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** `Examples/SSP/OneTimePad/FullTests.agda:25–36` transports raw inlining equality to W/full U and ordinary PM; `:38–52` gives a separate explicit compiled-simulator construction. These are **not full-context OTP confidentiality**. All verdict-target raw tests are WB due to the single output channel (`ObservationRestriction.agda:56–79`); the difference cannot be summarized merely as “a smaller list of test programs.”
8. **Obstruction.** The `full-test` representative does not respect the coarser local test equivalence under hostile closures (`ObservationRestriction.agda:113–116`). Thus its existence is not an inverse presheaf/security map. PK13 supplies the illegal-interaction control.
9. **Security/error.** Full⇒local uses the same simulator; full⇒PM preserves U/UC by target-ancilla coverage. No reverse full-security result follows from the quotient. A local all-positive quantitative readout is not proof of fixed-ε nonexpansive reflection.
10. **Obligations.** None for the proved one-way maps. A proposed reverse map would need a well-defined test comparison and hostile-context adequacy, and its failure cannot be bypassed by choosing arbitrary representatives. A source-specific full-PM theorem can still be proved independently, as the inlining example demonstrates.

### PK11 — Native SSP compilation and the two different density squares

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Direct SSP→PM[wb] compiler and security preservation, using finite closure/test density. Native representation theorem plus existing generic transfer.
2. **Primary contract being implemented/compared.** CH20 §5.3–5.4 inline/exec equations, including inline associativity and exec-inline with reassociated state (`P/lochbihler2020crypthol.pdf:465–519`, [ePrint](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/753)); CC19 converter attachment and respectful interfaces (§IV, [publication](https://crypto.ethz.ch/publications/LSBM19.html)). The repository density proofs are not a theorem imported from those sources.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Typed SSP calls flatten to tagged machine queries/replies; continuations become suspended machine states. Compilation preserves source simulation and composition; channel checks and phase monitoring handle malformed interactions. Identity/tensor become lawful only after WB-local observational quotienting.
4. **Repo state.** `S/SSP/Compile.agda:32–53,66–112` tags/checks replies and preserves simulations/composition. `SSP/Semantics.agda:57–73,98–112,131–178` proves WB monitor absorption and strong-monoidal `Fᵂ`. `SSP/Dense.agda:365–388` gives `games-dense`, test map `νᵂ`, `SSP→PM[wb]`; `SSP/Dense/Tests.agda:460–490` gives `tests-dense`, bare-kernel and UC preservation.
5. **Class. C** for the already implemented native compiler/density theorem's generalization; **A** for the actual `StdUCMorphism` and transfer applications. Not missing SSP compilation.
6. **Minimal extra data / exact API use.** A source-to-target representation respecting the quotient laws; finite target-run cuts with error/sandwich control; faithful source finite testers/closures; target all-positive/closed observation rule; image-ancilla coverage. `StdUCMorphism` takes `Fᵂ` and `νᵂ`; `surjective⇒preserves-≈ᵁ` uses identity-object essential surjectivity plus the proved bare-kernel implication, then `transfer⁺` supplies security preservation.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** SSP→PM[wb] is actual; TaskPIOA native tests and IPDL finite experiment compilation are other **different** adequacy examples, not the same density proof. SSP closure truncation checks channels; target test approximation needs WB of the closure.
8. **Obstruction.** Finite source call trees do not literally represent every unbounded target machine/simulator. Density in observations does not supply a finite syntactic representation, grade fullness or simulator backtranslation. Hostile PM interactions are excluded from this target.
9. **Security/error.** `games-dense` makes source **test equivalence** map to target test equivalence by approximating closures. `tests-dense` establishes preservation of **bare hom-kernel agreement** by approximating arbitrary target tests. Its premise/conclusion use every positive rational slack; it is not a stated fixed-ε nonexpansiveness theorem. Target U then follows with all ancillas; simulator translation is fixed before target contexts.
10. **Exact squares already discharged / still missing.** Discharged: operational inlining, WB monitor identity/tensor, source test equality→target test equality, compiled-test agreement→arbitrary WB-test agreement, all-image ancilla transport. Missing for stronger claims: hostile-PM identity/context square; arbitrary target-simulator lifting; quantitative uniform error/cost bounds and asymptotic rate closure. Do not call the two density directions interchangeable.

### PK12 — Existing quotient-descended compiler and pointwise triangle

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** **Newly present in the source boundary**, SSPdist→PM[wb], and agreement of direct/via-quotient SSP setup morphisms. This report does not implement it.
2. **Primary context.** B18/SS23 linking and distributional program laws plus CH20 inline/exec contracts (PK02/PK05/PK11, [SS23](https://hal.science/hal-04273257v1), [CH20](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/753)). The exact descent/triangle is repository-specific; no paper theorem number is invented for it.
3. **Signature/laws.** Compile representatives, prove `P≈ᵈQ ⇒ FᵂP≈PM[wb]FᵂQ`, descend to a strong monoidal functor and reuse the test map. Compose setup morphisms SSP→SSPdist→PM[wb]; compare the composed/direct components in the appropriate target equalities.
4. **Repo state.** `S/SSP/Dist/Compile.agda:37–50`: `compile-pad`, `⟦⟧ᵂ-resp-≈ᵈ`; `:52–82`: `Fᵂᵈ`, `νᵂᵈ`, `dist⇒PM[wb]`, preservation of bare/U/UC; `:88–147`: `ssp-via-dist`, `Triangle`.
5. **Class. A.** Actual quotient/descent, morphism composition and transfer instantiations. The source-specific descent proof is implemented, not a missing research square.
6. **Actual instantiation.** `compile-pad` relates compiling two-sided padded SSP protocols to target WB padding using quotient wire/tensor equations; `Tests.preserves-≈ℰ` then proves descent. `StdUCMorphism SSPᵈ.Protᵈ WB.PM[wb]ᴹ SSPᵈ.ℰᵈ WB.ℰ[wb] Fᵂᵈ νᵂᵈ` is explicit. UC preservation uses source quotient reflection followed by direct preservation (`:81–82`).
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** Both direct `Dense.SSP→PM[wb]` and actual `composeᵁ dist⇒PM[wb] ssp⇒dist` are compared. The quotient OTP witness is a source security input; its target is PM[wb], not ordinary PM or RS.
8. **Scope/control.** `Triangle.homs/grade-homs/κ/tensor/unit` are **target hom equations**; objects/grade objects use propositional equality, tests use target presheaf equality. This is not equality of entire dependent morphism records, nor an equality of raw compiled machines.
9. **Security/error.** Both routes preserve qualitative UC and have pointwise agreement on computations and return/ext/sub comparisons. No target-security reflection or ordinary-PM transfer follows. No fixed-error quantitative triangle theorem is stated.
10. **Obligations.** None for the claimed component triangle. Any stronger record equality, full-PM route, quantitative transfer or reflection needs additional proof; do not list the already proved padding/descent square as missing.

### PK13 — Failed raw identity generalization; genuinely open stronger compiler bridges

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** Attempted unqualified SSP→ordinary-PM functor/security transfer, and possible reverse target-security transfer. Distinguish refuted raw law from unproved security/reflection claims.
2. **Primary interface warning.** CC19 Defs1/3 require respectful resources/converters because sum tagging alone does not force the reply to use the query's interface (`P/lochbihler2019cc.pdf:123–175`, [publication](https://crypto.ethz.ch/publications/LSBM19.html)). Exact negative behavior is inspected code below.
3. **Attempted contract.** A raw strong-monoidal compiler would preserve source forwarding identity and tensor under target hom/full observations. Reflection would additionally lift any target simulator into an admissible source simulator before all contexts.
4. **Repo evidence.** `S/Protocol/Machine.agda:12–19,52–72` explicitly shows compiled wire rejects an unsolicited return in idle state, whereas raw PM identity is a stateless forwarder. `S/SSP/Compile.agda:32–53` additionally kills wrong-channel replies. `SSP/Semantics.agda:3–11` records that identity/tensor hold only in PM[wb]. The direct and descended preservation theorems target PM[wb].
5. **Class. D** for the **same raw compiler's ordinary-PM identity law**; **E** for unqualified security preservation via a different construction or arbitrary PM[wb]→SSP security reflection. Absence of a proof is not itself a no-go theorem.
6. **Required extra data.** For a different positive target, explicitly restrict/monitor legal interaction and prove the lawful quotient/context map, or change the representation with a new identity theorem. Reflection needs grade/simulator lifting, observation monicity and contextual adequacy in the actual transfer signature. Object surjectivity and tester density alone do not provide this.
7. **Positive controls.** WB monitor absorption proves the lawful PM[wb] compiler. Individual raw SSP simulation equations can still yield W/full and PM theorems (`Examples/SSP/OneTimePad/FullTests.agda:29–36`); this prevents overstating D as “no SSP fact can ever hold in PM.”
8. **Concrete counterexample.** At `(idle tt, inj₁ a⁺)`, compiled identity steps to `botₚ`, raw identity to `returnₚ(tt,inj₂ a⁺)` (`Protocol/Machine.agda:15–18`). **[INFERENCE]** A hostile closure supplying that unsolicited return and observing the forwarded result separates absence of verdict from success. A distinct two-channel control asks on a and replies on b: the typed compiler aborts rather than retagging b as an a-answer. Neither context is a legal SSP/W-local context.
9. **Security/error.** W/full→PM is a valid existing arrow, but its premise is a W/full theorem; a PM[wb] theorem cannot be fed backwards through it. The target simulator is existential **before** all contexts, so finite per-test approximants do not give source simulator reflection. Preserve both verdict masses: failure is the separating observation, not a disposable artifact.
10. **Exact obligations.** Ordinary-PM extension: a lawful identity/tensor representation and hostile-test adequacy/backtranslation. Reflection: a single representable simulator with compatible κ/grade action and all-context observation agreement. No proof of these was found in the mapped compiler/density modules; do not silently assert them or negate every possible future encoding.

### PK14 — Distribution-state trace equivalence versus point-state bisimulation

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** A relational proof principle on **distributions of states/resources**, with history-conditioned successors; relevant to SSPdist and PM behavioral observations, not a replacement of state-map hom equality.
2. **Primary contract.** CC19 Defs4–5, Figure6, Thms1–2, `P/lochbihler2019cc.pdf:227–298` ([publication](https://crypto.ethz.ch/publications/LSBM19.html)); CH20 §5.5 (`:523–538`, [ePrint](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/753)).
3. **Native signature.** Point-state bisimulation couples each query's equal replies and related successor resources. The complete trace principle instead relates subdistributions over resources: initial Dirac distributions are related; for every query, reply probabilities agree, and each positive-probability reply yields related conditional successor distributions. Thm2 equates trace equivalence with equality of output distributions for every respectful distinguisher.
4. **Repo state.** `SSP/Dist/Kernel.Samples` carries a one-direction stochastic state map and all-oracle squares, while `AvgBisim` carries related state families; these are expressive existing proof principles, but neither is literally the CC19 posterior-distribution relation/complete trace characterization. No such named general characterization was found in the inspected SSP/W modules. PM/RS behavioral theory belongs to the parent/PM report.
5. **Class. C.** A structured additional proof/adequacy theorem, not a consequence of arbitrary UC/QUC; **A** credit remains with the existing sampling/averaging APIs.
6. **Minimal extra data.** A specified stochastic response semantics, joint next-state/reply distributions, legal-query predicate, positive-event conditioning or equivalent unnormalized update, finite/history semantics and its testing adequacy. Avoid choosing arbitrary off-support conditionals as canonical equalities.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** CC19's resources are the primary positive construction; finite SSP/IPDL response kernels can support such distribution-state relations. TaskPIOA needs its task schedule and trace semantics retained rather than replaced by arbitrary adaptive polling.
8. **Concrete source counterexample.** CC19 Figure6 compares an early hidden probabilistic branch after first answer a with a late branch on the next call: both have the same a-then-b/c traces, but no point-state bisimulation matches deterministic early branches against the late branching state. This is not a counterexample to distribution-state bisimulation. PK07's repeated-bit example separately refutes using only unconditioned marginals.
9. **Security/error.** Trace equality gives equality for the **respectful** native distinguisher class in Thm2. Reaching repository U still requires its tests/ancillas to be represented. Conditioning can amplify approximate errors by the reciprocal of rare-event probability **[INFERENCE]**; no uniform nonexpansiveness follows without a lower bound or a different unnormalized metric.
10. **Obligations.** Identify the exact finite/infinite trace and failure readout, prove posterior update/conditioning laws and all-native-test adequacy, then decide whether existing `Samples`/`AvgBisim` witnesses suffice for intended examples. Do not implement a second relation hierarchy merely because the names differ.

### PK15 — Functional availability, query-sensitive secure realization and asymptotic scope

1. **Construction/status/consumers.** CC19 secure construction is **functional realization plus simulation security**; query budgets and negligible error are part of its native contract. Existing repository QUC/RS constructions cover algebra, not automatically these SSP/W source semantics.
2. **Primary contract.** CC19 Defs7–8/Thms3–6, `P/lochbihler2019cc.pdf:309–388`, printed pp.10–11 ([publication](https://crypto.ethz.ch/publications/LSBM19.html)); CH20 query counting/Lemma3 `:586–599` ([ePrint](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/753)).
3. **Native signature.** Functional realization fixes an embedding of the ideal adversary interface into the real one and compares resources against **all respectful distinguishers up to a query bound**. Security additionally supplies `∃S.∀D` negligible advantage with S attached only to the adversary side. Parallel realization has bound `min(b1,b2)` and parallel simulators (Thm5); honest/user converter attachment preserves the same simulator/embedding under `b′·max(k,1)≤b` (Thm6). The paper explicitly states no analogous arbitrary adversary-interface attachment theorem.
4. **Repo state.** Assigned SSP/W nodes have qualitative setups and quantitative evaluations, not named independent QUC instances. Existing generic quantitative arithmetic/witness APIs are `S/UC/Quantitative.agda:112–186`, `UC/Quantitative/Witness.agda:41–80`; actual RS designated-mode/security+availability consumers are `UC/RandomSystems/Availability.agda:38–76,107–132` (owner-reviewed generic catalogue G13). Query certificates are separately in `UC/QueryBound.agda:38–158`. No native CC19↔SSP/W realization identification is asserted.
5. **Class. A** for the existing QUC witness, query and RS two-budget APIs; **C** for source-specific designated modes, runtime/query and family identification. The parent's proposed generic two-cap packaging is a separate B candidate, not duplicated here.
6. **Minimal data.** Designated honest/embedding modes, compatibility at a composition boundary, legal test class, one fixed simulator, query-cost accounting and a security-parameter family/admissible rate class. QUC observation errors do not themselves supply any of these operational interpretations.
7. **Positive/control witnesses.** QRS explicitly keeps security and availability errors separate. SSP partial Boolean reads observe failure; CH20's subprobability semantics also does. A security simulator is not automatically a designated honest-mode execution.
8. **Bound caveat and obstruction.** Rendered primary pp.10–11 confirm Thm4's premises are middle→ideal up to `bound1`, real→middle up to `bound2`, with `bound2·max(boundS,1)≤bound1`, but its printed conclusion says up to `bound1`. This is an apparent indexing error, not just PDF extraction. **[INFERENCE]** The safe outer-use contract is a final b with `b≤bound2` and `b·max(boundS,1)≤bound1`; it must not be silently attributed as the printed theorem. The paper names formal theorem `composability` in its appendix; current AFP search exposed the outer-bound formulation, but direct AFP primary fetches returned403, so historical mechanized/version correspondence remains **E**. The unambiguous Thms5–6 are used as stated.
9. **Quantifiers/errors.** CC19 has a simulator before all bounded distinguishers; negligibility is a parameterized statement, not one concrete ε. In existing families, `∀context∃negligible δ` must not be changed into `∃negligible δ∀context`; neither permits choosing the simulator after δ/context. Security and availability errors add separately under compatible modes. No PPT theorem follows from the paper's information-theoretic model.
   A distinct, already implemented quantifier is `S/UC/Machine/EventBounds.agda:100–115`: `Saturated X ε = ∀ polynomial p. ∃ negligible ν. ∀n. X n (p n) (ε n (p n)+ν n)`. When `X=BoundedAt`, ν is chosen before **all contexts admitted by that cap** (`:90–93,117–121`), not inside their quantifier. Do not confuse this existing uniform-at-cap event theorem with either the canonical local-negligible U setup or its small-image reading.
10. **Obligations.** Give exact honest caps/embedding and experiment correspondence for SSP/W, extract native query costs, choose family/rate and error uniformity, prove both observation components and their composition. For a numerical use of CC19 Thm4, consult a versioned formal source rather than propagate the printed bound discrepancy.

## 4. Strongest reusable two-consumer target: adapters, not a new coupling core

All proposals in this section are **[INFERENCE]**. The parent candidate R2 should be read as the following **native adequacy/interface adapters around existing theorems**.

### 4.1 Actual signature mismatches

| Existing API | What it already proves | Mismatch that an adapter must handle |
|---|---|---|
| `GamePlaying.StepBisim⊥ / runWith⊥-bisim` | Homogeneous `Q,R`, partial rational kernels, fixed related states, equality for every finite adaptive `Strat Q R` and every final Bool→ℚ payoff | SSP/IPDL replies depend on channel; native outputs may be traces or arbitrary X, not Bool; typed flattening needs correct-tag support and legal tester correspondence. A Task precommitted word is a restricted native schedule, not a license to upgrade its scheduler class. |
| `GamePlaying.Defer.AvgBisim / runWith-avg` | Normalized rational seed, related family of early states and one late state, averaged Pr1 equality for finite adaptive strategies | Family relation is not merely a state map; result is not native open linking/UC. Total normalized kernels mean failure extensions must use the partial or machine layer rather than silently normalize. |
| `GamePlaying.Defer.Run.DeferStep / run-defer` | Machine `Dₚ`, closed machine, finite strategy, affine seed; whole run agrees in `≈ₚ` | Not `≈ᴹ`, not arbitrary open imported oracles, not already a two-sided U/quotient statement. It is an existing machine-run bridge, not a missing lazy-sampling theorem. |
| `SSP.Dist.Kernel.Samples / sample-⟦⟧` | Dependent channel queries/replies, arbitrary output X, partial kernels, joint output/final-oracle-state equality after stochastic state resampling | Requires normalized stochastic map ρ, not an arbitrary relation/coupling. Protocol `_⊑_` further quantifies every imported oracle state O and its frame. Native runners need constructor/experiment adequacy. |
| Task `Simulation` | Deterministic θ, same choices, matching all input/local kernels; same fixed schedule trace equality and independent wired-product closure | Does not already provide arbitrary distribution-state relation, stochastic resampling, or family averaging. Inputs/wiring/disabled stuttering must be preserved. |
| IPDL `Correspondence.execution/agreement` | Actual finite typed/private-coin adaptive native experiments equal compiled SSP resource/test runs | Native public result erases final residual state; persistent pending `Maybe` reply is **not** failure. This is not a UCSetupMorphism or general typed open-network linker theorem. |

### 4.2 Proposed adapter lemma, reusing `sample-⟦⟧`

Supply, for a native finite experiment syntax e:

- a typed interface A and response oracle `O_N : Oracle A S`;
- a constructor-faithful interpreter/translation `tree(e):Calls A X`;
- an exact native-run square, either retaining final state or projecting the final state out of `⟦tree(e)⟧ O_N s`;
- `Samples ρ O0 O1` and corresponding initial-state law;
- for composition, an independently proved frame/wiring law on the native model.

Then **reuse `SSP.Dist.Kernel.sample-⟦⟧:243–245`** and project state to obtain equality of every native outcome payoff, hence both verdict masses and failure. Since ρ is normalized, discarding its final state draw is justified; native failure is not discarded. The mathematical step is a corollary of the existing theorem. The reusable work is a small adapter/adequacy interface and concrete instances, **not another probability abstraction or finite-run induction**.

Concrete first instances:

1. **SSP + TaskPIOA.** SSP's `Calls` uses the identity adapter. For Task M use query=task and reply=`Maybe(Output)`, with `O_M(q,t)` obtained by swapping `tick M t q`'s `(reply,state)` pair and embedding the total rational distribution into `Dist⊥`. A disabled tick is `just(state,nothing)`—a successful no-output result—not outer `nothing`. A fixed schedule plus accumulated trace/payoff translates to a finite call tree. `Simulation.tick-match:47–48` yields the Dirac-ρ commuting square; `starts` handles initialization. Prove the native `runFrom`/compiled-tree projection square. Existing `product-simulation` and `Security.test-preserves/uniform⇒≈ᵁ` already handle deterministic witnesses; broader stochastic witnesses need their own input/receiving/product closure, not a new UC core.
2. **SSP + IPDL.** IPDL already provides `resource`, typed `turn`, `compileTree`, `execution`, `lift-run`, and `agreement` (`S/IPDL/SSP.agda:29–92`). Its `Input/Get/Step` response contract reads Get **before** the subsequent quiescence (`IPDL/Native.agda:109–119`). Reuse that existing native outcome square. `IPDL/Linker.agda:83–131` already proves a specific shared-hidden-bit square using canonical residual state. A general stochastic residual-state/linker relation and its contextual closure are separate work; the interface adapter alone must not invent an IPDL setup.

For **U** rather than native payoff equality, retain each model's own source bridge: SSP `_⊑_⇒≈ᵈ⇒≈ᵁ` includes all imported oracles and both ancillas; Task's syntax/wiring/tests have their own `Uniform` bridge; IPDL currently has only finite experiment correspondence and specific linker squares. For **compiler** transfer, use PK11–PK12, not the run lemma as a substitute for target-test or simulator coverage.

## 5. Synthesis and exact remaining work boundary

- The strongest genuinely reusable direction is **typed native-run adequacy plus locality adapters**, using existing `Samples/sample-⟦⟧`, `StepBisim⊥`, `AvgBisim`, `Defer.Run` and partial FLGP. A new generic coupling/deferred-sampling framework would duplicate present code.
- Source operational linking/renaming/separation is not discharged by generic UC composition. Conversely, already proved category/UC/hybrid/quotient/transfer instances are **A coverage**, not “new constructions.”
- Three crucial controls are concrete: illegal return separates raw compiler identity from PM copycat; equal one-call marginals do not preserve a repeated hidden bit's joint trace; shared-cell writes do not justify independent-state tensor. Also retain the source-specific correction that SSProve's **syntactic** interchange permits shared global state.
- SSPdist preserves and reflects SSP's UC order. Both direct SSP→PM[wb] and quotient-descended SSPdist→PM[wb] preserve UC; the component triangle is present. Neither path is ordinary-PM transfer or arbitrary target-security reflection.
- The missing stronger semantic squares are **hostile/full-PM compiler adequacy**, **single-simulator/grade lifting for reflection**, **fixed-error/query/rate transfer**, and **native stochastic relation/frame/wiring adapters** where not yet provided. Existing SSP padding/descent, both density directions, IPDL finite experiment agreement and Task deterministic simulation/test closure must not be listed as missing.
- Meaningful corpus exclusions: full SSProve language coverage, arbitrary infinite lazy tables/GPVs, general recursive/adaptive task schedulers, every protocol-specific KEM/DEM or sigma proof, unbounded session allocation and PPT/runtime semantics. None is inferred from the inspected finite package carrier. No new B theorem is asserted in this package slice; the parent's generic designated-mode packaging is kept separate.

# Machine/UC construction-generality evidence

Read-only research for `ConstructionGeneralityAstra`; no repository edits or verification commands. Exact integration root: `/Users/andre/IOHK/categorical-crypto/.worktrees/uc-setup-landscape`. Source boundary and docs revision are parent-supplied `9f9be2a89` and `b24ae5f92`; not independently checked with git. Code locations below are relative to that root, under `src/CategoricalCrypto/` unless stated otherwise. Source PDFs are under `docs/kb/sources/papers/`. Lines are the reader's extracted lines, not PDF page numbers. Known declarations were inspected at this root, not copied blindly from scout line ranges.

## Scope, vocabulary, and coverage

Owned constructed nodes: **ITM.Setup.StdSetup**, the initialized **PM interpretation** used by that setup, **ITM.Relational.Uninitialized.UC.StdSetup**, and **Machine.UC.StdSetup**. PM's other probabilistic constructions remain with their separate owner. IITM/GNUC/SUC/iUC/GUC are **source theories**, not additional implemented repository nodes. IPDL is an interpreter/correspondence consumer, not a current UCSetup. CSP is not counted as an integrated/checked consumer. Causal/RSIM setups are planned, not constructed; `Machines.Sim.Relational` is not an RSIM security definition.

Classification: **A** already supplied by an inspected generic API at the stated instance; **B** a bare-UC-axiom result not yet packaged; **C** a construction/generalization requiring specified extra structure; **D** unrestricted claim obstructed by an explicit control; **E** unresolved or source/access/representation evidence missing. A entries are coverage, **not new constructions**. No useful machine/session/PPT construction below was identified as B: the bare UCSetup signature has none of those data. Classification/generalization proposals and cross-model candidate interfaces are marked **[INFERENCE]**. Source theorem statements and code declarations are evidence, not proposals.

Keep four distinctions throughout:

* `≈ᵁ` universally closes an observation kernel under ancillary grades. `≤UC` additionally quantifies attackers/simulators. A state-map simulation is not that adversarial simulator.
* The relational machine setup compares **state-isomorphic test composites**, not initialized execution probabilities. Its verdict port does not turn its test equality into a bit-distribution observation.
* Existing finite-table ITM syntax has unbounded work tapes and an unbounded probabilistic-delay interpreter. Finite control is not finite state or PPT. Its monoidal compositions are free syntax, not a proved compiler into one finite transition table.
* Independent sessions, shared state, global access, instantaneous routing/iteration, and persistent initialized state are different operations. Parent-owned [construction-generality-structural-evidence.md](#appendix-d) gives the general state/trace/copying distinctions; this report specializes them to the machine corpus. In particular, do not replace the actual sum-interface machine trace with Cartesian stream feedback or copying of randomness.

### Explicit bounded source corpus and version control

Indexed primary passages were discoverable, but uninspected canonical theorem numbering is deliberately not asserted here. See M16. |

Cryptology 26(3), 375–441 (2013). Numbering below is **the inspected 2009 full report**: Definitions 7–8, 25–29; Lemmas 9–13; Theorems 14–16, 27. |

Definitions 4.1–4.3, 4.6; Lemmas 4.4–4.7; Theorems 4.8–4.9; §5. Some proofs explicitly deferred to full report. |
