Per-model corpus assignment: initialized ITM/PM uses SUC/IITM/RUN/GNUC/iUC for native machine/runtime contrasts and JUC/GUC/UCGS/RESP for candidate wrappers; UC0 is an explicit access-limited standard reference. Uninitialized ITM and relational machine-isomorphism use the inspected code and MEALY's initialization distinction, with the same UC papers only as **contrasts**, not evidence that their native semantics have been implemented. All requested source families thus have a disposition; the canonical and SUC-full access holes are not hidden behind nearby variants.

## Current code boundary (observed)

* `ITM/Core.agda:177–185` gives `run`, fair-choice instruction `flip`, `send`, and `TM` with finite `#Q`, selected `start`, transition table `δ`. `Mem` is control state plus tape (`:193–197`); `load :211–212` starts an activation with supplied persistent memory and fresh activation input/buffer. `𝕋ᴹ :226–230` is the free monoidal category. `ITM/Run.agda:34–47,62–64` interprets instructions, uses `iterₚ`, and initializes `runᴾ` at `blank`. `send` preserves next control/work memory. No security-parameter runtime certificate is in this construction.
* `ITM/Setup.agda:36–55` pulls PM tests back along the strong monoidal interpretation and constructs `itm⇒prot`; `νᵀ` is identity on those pulled-back tests and epi/mono. `ITM/Transfer.agda:63–105` proves both directions for `≈ᵁ`, forward `≤UC`, and **only explicitly simulator-represented** reverse `≤UC`.
* `Machine/Core.agda:17–33` gives arbitrary state and a relational transition `S → input → Maybe output → S → Type`; no initial state. Tensor has product state with one component stepping (`:68–84`). `Machine/Iso.agda:28–38` requires a bijection of **all** states preserving steps in both directions. `:42–54` proves a redundant-Boolean-state forwarder is not isomorphic to the one-state identity.
* `Machine/UC.agda:59–64,84–105,109–127` defines tests with an ancillary interface and a relational machine, equality of those tests by same ancillary interface plus state-isomorphism, reversed monoidal carrier, and `StdUC`. `:164–174` supplies its own grade-stability proof and `≈ᵁ ⇔ ≈ℰ`. This does not establish PM grade stability.
* `ITM/Relational.agda:49–52,112–145` uses terminating `Halts` derivations, retaining possible coin branches but not their weights, and does not select `start` in `Uninitialized.leaf`. Its pullback test setup and morphism are `:174–192`; image-isomorphism implies its `≈ᵁ` at `:201–210`. `initializedLeaf :212–213` is a helper, **not a second initialized relational UCSetup**.
* `Machine/Initialized.agda:20–43,65–69` adds an initial state and requires initial-state preservation in initialized isomorphism, yielding finite-trace equality. It supplies neither probability nor PPT semantics.

## Ten-field construction rows

### M01 — Existing generic UC, dummy, composition, and robust-property results

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M01; A coverage for ITM, uninitialized ITM, Machine.UC, and PM. Dummy/composition are explicitly present in multiple primary UC families; this is not a new machine construction.
2. **Precise primary contract.** IITM Definition 19/Theorem 7 and Theorem 8; RUN Theorems 14–16; GNUC Theorems 5–7, in the versions/URLs above. These include their respective admissibility/runtime hypotheses. UC0's canonical numbering is not used.
3. **Native signature/laws.** `∀ A admissible ∃ S admissible ∀ E admissible: real ≈ ideal`; dummy replacement concentrates the witness before the environments. Native contextual substitution additionally requires allowed communication/identity/runtime structure. Native indistinguishability is not automatically the repository's chosen kernel.
4. **Repository state/API.** `Abstract2.agda:45–46,131–137,156–176`: `≈ᵁ`, `≤UC`, dummy equivalence and `UC-compose`. `Standard2.agda:19–34` is instantiated at the four stated carriers/tests. `UC/Robust/Compilation.agda:82–96,111–124` already characterizes `≤UC` by robust hyperproperty preservation and explicit-simulator trace-property preservation. The file has **165**, not 215, lines.
5. **Class.** **A** at these exact instances. [INFERENCE] Native runtime-closed substitution is C, not a consequence of renaming this existing A theorem.
6. **Minimal data / A instance.** The existing `StdUC` arguments are the concrete monoidal carrier and presheaf listed above. No new “generic machine UC” record is needed to obtain these results. Native admission additionally needs the closure supplied in M12–M14.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: the inspected `dummy-complete` builds attacker-composed simulator `a ∘ s₀`. Control: an environment-dependent simulator cannot be supplied to that function; its argument already requires one simulator working for all ancillary tests.
8. **Obstruction vs unknown.** [INFERENCE] Bare axioms do not describe session creation or machine cost; that absence is a signature obstruction, not an unproved universal machine theorem. Source-specific closure results remain separate.
9. **Quantifiers/security/error.** Repository: `∀a ∃s ∀W ∀test`, and dummy `∃s₀ ∀W ∀test`. The exact kernel is state-iso or pulled-back perfect PM observation, depending on setup. No computational negligible advantage or PPT simulator is thereby created.
10. **Obligations.** Reuse A directly. A source-to-repo security comparison still needs operational representation, admissible attacker/environment translation, closure under the proof's context construction, appropriate ancillary coverage and (for reverse security) simulator representation. A robust-property characterization is not a compiler or a backtranslation algorithm.

### M02 — Existing machine state composition, simulation congruence, and sum-feedback

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M02; existing machine carrier APIs, with PM and symbolic initialized roles as actual consumers; SSP has a parallel pointed-state pattern. MEALY supports the initialized-state abstraction, not native UC security.
2. **Primary contract.** MEALY Definitions 3.2/3.5/3.6, Proposition 3.4: `(U,i,f)`; pure/cartesian state map `α` obeys `α i=j` and the transition square; composition tensors states/initializers; zig-zag closure handles lack of pullbacks. Archive extracted `:201–245`.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Pointed state plus transition, purity of state transport, initializer preservation and transition commutation. The repository's routed instantaneous feedback instead has signature `Machine(A+X,B+X) → Machine(A,B)` and requires Elgot iteration; this is not stored-stream feedback.
4. **Repository/API.** `Machines/Sim.agda:39–55` has `θ`, `θ-pure`, `θ-point`, `θ-step`, then equivalence closure. `Machines/Trace.agda:25–28,51–75,89–102` supplies the symmetric/distributive/PureSub/Elgot interface and yanking. `Machines/Base.agda:63–85` instantiates it with the commutative probabilistic-delay Kleisli base. `Symbolic/Semantics.agda:91–100` gives initialized role machines. `SSP/Protocol.agda:116–169` has pointed state, product-state composition/tensor and `θ-init`/`θ-step`.
5. **Class.** **A** for the inspected machine APIs; **C [INFERENCE]** for a shared initialized-state presentation bridging SSP and machine syntax; **D [INFERENCE]** for attributing arbitrary feedback/state/copying to bare UCSetup.
6. **Minimal data.** State tensor, initializer, effectful transition, chosen pure state maps, and context congruence. For the actual sum trace add symmetric base, distributivity and Elgot laws; no assumption of Cartesian copying of effects.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: PM base and symbolic roles supply points/pure maps; SSP explicitly pairs states and initializers. Control: `Machine.Iso.padded-not-iso` distinguishes redundant state even when forwarding behavior agrees; parent structural evidence gives the Boolean fixed-point obstruction to unrestricted total feedback.
8. **Obstruction vs unknown.** Exact carrier-level construction exists. [INFERENCE] Native dynamic population and runtime are not obtained from it. State-isomorphism, zig-zag pure simulation and initialized traces are different equivalences.
9. **Quantifiers/security/error.** State maps witness whole transition systems, including a point where present, before input traces. They are not `∃ adversarial simulator`. Exact congruence costs no error; arbitrary feedback of approximate components needs a query/iteration bound, not an automatic same-ε theorem.
10. **Obligations.** A new consumer must supply the exact base/purity/iteration fields and point square. To lift carrier congruence to a UC claim, prove that its tests respect this equivalence and all ancillary prefixes; to lift approximate security, prove the corresponding nonexpansiveness or explicit accumulation bound.

### M03 — Existing finite-table ITM interpretation and the exact transfer boundary

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M03; A coverage for ITM→PM, with a second free interpreted syntax consumer in symbolic machines. This is an implementation fact, not a native PPT UC implementation.
2. **Primary contract.** Native contrasts: IITM §3 (CheckAddress/Compute, systems and activation); SUC §§2.1–2.2 (lifetime runtime, router, initial `1^n`); MEALY initialized transitions. No source says this repository's finite-table/free syntax is already their native machine population.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Current literal leaf `TM` → initialized PM process; composition extends strongly monoidally from free syntax. Persistent control/work state and fresh input/buffer per activation; fair `flip`; potentially divergent `iterₚ`. Source IITM includes identity selection and dynamic `!M` population that are additional data.
4. **Repository/API.** `ITM/Core.agda:177–230`, `ITM/Run.agda:34–64`, `ITM/Setup.agda:36–69`; `ITM/Transfer.agda:63–83` proves `preserves-≈ᵁ`, `reflects-≈ᵁ`, `preserves-≤UC`. `:94–105` reverse security explicitly takes target `s′`, its target witness, source `s`, and behavioral equality of `F₁ s` with `s′`. The preservation proof uses image-domain dual ancillary coverage (`:70–75`), not target GradeStable.
5. **Class.** **A** exact transfer above; **E** unrestricted source-simulator completeness/PPT representation; **D [INFERENCE]** the claim that test surjectivity alone gives security reflection.
6. **Minimal data.** No extra assumptions for the existing A result. Stronger reverse theorem needs behavioral simulator fullness (or a compiler for the target simulators in scope), not merely monic/epic observations. A PPT reading also needs security parameters, operational costs and uniform resource-preserving compilation.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: explicit represented dummy simulator is already sufficient. [INFERENCE] General control: take base finite sets with tests separating bits, grade objects finite Boolean powers, source grade arrows identities only, target additionally bit flips, and tensor action `T_X B=X×B`. Constant-0 versus constant-1 source protocols are not source-UC-equivalent; target flip is a simulator. Identical tests do not create the missing source grade arrow.
8. **Obstruction vs unknown.** The control obstructs a universal tests-only reflection theorem. Whether every relevant PM simulator is implementable by literal/free ITM is **not established**; do not infer impossibility from its absence. Source image duals are enough for the observed forward U theorem, so an obsolete “bare kernel only” characterization would be false.
9. **Quantifiers/security/error.** Forward source dummy witness is translated once, independent of target tests. Reverse requires one source witness representing the target dummy witness before all tests. Current security is perfect pulled-back PM observation, not asymptotic PPT indistinguishability.
10. **Obligations.** For an enlarged transfer: prove code/activation representation, point preservation, target test backtranslation, ancillary coverage, and separately simulator fullness on the intended admissible class. Resource and approximation preservation are independent proof obligations. Do not invoke PM GradeStable as a shortcut.

### M04 — Initialization-sensitive state transport versus the uninitialized state-iso setup

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M04; uninitialized ITM and Machine.UC are actual nodes; initialized PM, symbolic roles and SSP are ≥2 pointed consumers for an initialization-sensitive generalization.
2. **Primary contract.** MEALY Definition 3.5 requires the initializer square. IITM §3.3 initializes fresh machine instances/coins; iUC §3.3/Fig.4 distinguishes once-per-instance `Initialization` from once-per-entity `EntityInitialization`.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Uninitialized relation `R:S×I×Maybe(O)×S→Type`; initialized version `(S,s₀,R)` with iso `h:S≅T`, step equivalence, and `h(s₀)=t₀`. Probabilistic initialization requires a distribution-preserving square instead, not just a relation of possible starts.
4. **Repository/API.** `ITM/Relational.agda:112–145` proves `reinitialize-iso` after changing `start` only; `:174–213` defines the uninitialized setup and separate initialized helper. `Machine/Initialized.agda:20–43,65–69` gives initialized iso→finite-trace equivalence. `ITM/Relational/Tests.agda:49–60,72–88,116–148` gives same-table iso, opposite initialized outputs, no initialized iso, and persistent-memory latch.
5. **Class.** **A** for current uninitialized iso and helper theorem; **C [INFERENCE]** shared pointed-state construction; **D** uninitialized-iso⇒initialized-behavior without an initializer condition.
6. **Minimal data.** Choose a point/distribution; preserve it under simulation; retain state across activations. Probability-sensitive semantics must retain transition weights and divergence mass; PPT requires additional operational costs.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: initialized iso proves finite traces equal; SSP `θ-init` is the matching shape. Hard code control: zeroStart and oneStart have identical transition table and isomorphic uninitialized images, but `initialized-traces-differ` and `no-initialized-iso` are proved. `initialized-memory-evolves` outputs false then true, excluding reinitialization at every activation.
8. **Obstruction vs unknown.** The initializer omission is a proved obstruction, not merely missing API convenience. Erasing coin weights into `Halts` also cannot in general reflect probability equality [INFERENCE]: fair versus biased choices can have the same possible terminating traces. No initialized relational UCSetup/PPT correspondence is claimed.
9. **Quantifiers/security/error.** Uninitialized iso quantifies all states; initialized traces quantify inputs from a chosen start. Neither quantifier implies the other without a point condition. No negligibility or probability-error parameter exists in the uninitialized setup.
10. **Obligations.** Preserve initialization, transition relation/probability, and composed state ownership. Prove observations and contexts are sound for the desired pointed equivalence; only then compare UC kernels. Reusing current uninitialized pullback tests without changing their meaning will not establish initialized cryptographic security.

### M05 — Native machine virtualization, renaming, dummy routing, and contextual reduction

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M05; a repeatedly used native proof construction in IITM/RUN/iUC, proposed for ITM and symbolic/IPDL interpreters (≥2 consumer families).
2. **Primary contract.** IITM Lemma 4: consistent external-tape renaming preserves execution/boundedness. Lemma 5: transparent two-way dummy/renaming preserves behavior and almost/strict boundedness. Lemma 6 (§4.5, extracted `:444–468`): for any system S there is one IITM M with the same external interface, accepting every CheckAddress message, such that `S|Q ≡₀ M|Q` for all connectable Q, with almost/strict boundedness equivalence. RUN Lemma 9 supplies polynomial-time approximation of closed executions when reactive boundedness is available.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Encode a finite system description and dynamically created persistent configurations within one machine; dispatch activations according to the source semantics; consistent tape renaming is equivariant; dummy forwarding is transparent. Preserve external outputs/probabilities and the stated resource notion, not necessarily step-for-step cost.
4. **Repository/API.** Literal ITM composition is free (`ITM/Core:226–230`), so not this finite-table virtualization theorem. PM `Machines.Sim:39–55` and relational spans `Machines/Sim/Relational.agda:255–299` support state-map evidence. `Symbolic/Machine.agda:169–196` interprets into relational machines and maps symbolic iso. `IPDL/Linker.agda:105–131,242–267` proves selected initialized-state/linker squares, not general IITM virtualization.
5. **Class.** **C [INFERENCE]** representation/context reduction; **A** for existing state-map/relational-span combinators only; **E** native IITM→literal repository ITM compiler.
6. **Minimal data.** Effective encodings of names/configurations, an explicit address-selection/activation discipline, persistent store, independent fresh randomness where source allocates it, interface renaming, and runtime overhead accounting. Preserve code/identity visibility where contexts can inspect it.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: IITM Lemma 6 supplies a real contextual theorem; IPDL selected paired/flat/canonical squares show a second useful proof pattern. Control: forgetting initialization fails M04; identifying multiple state instances fails M07. Relational state spans with pure legs do not themselves construct an adversarial simulator or prove runtime.
8. **Obstruction vs unknown.** General virtualization is not impossible; it is explicitly proved in IITM. Its realization by this repo's particular instruction set/ports is unresolved. [INFERENCE] A free extension law alone cannot supply configuration encodability or a polynomial overhead theorem.
9. **Quantifiers/security/error.** Native exact behavioral equivalence holds for every connectable Q with one compiled M. Approximate polynomial cutoff arguments may have environment-dependent bounds; do not move that dependence into a simulator chosen after E. Repository congruence is exact.
10. **Obligations.** Construct encoding/decoding, prove activation simulation and initial-state square, context renaming/backtranslation, same public observations, cost and admission preservation; then discharge test/ancillary coverage. Reverse security additionally needs simulator compilation, separate from protocol compilation.

### M06 — Namespaces, fresh sessions, and unbounded replication

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M06; native in JUC, IITM, GNUC, SUC and iUC. Candidate consumers are ITM/PM session wrappers and symbolic occurrence naming/SSP state maps; existing static names are not native dynamic sessions.
2. **Primary contract.** IITM §§5.2–5.3: session wrapper strips/prefixes SID and fixes an instance's first accepted SID; Definitions 13–16 generalize to polynomial SID function σ. Theorem 5: `!P̄` and Q environmentally bounded plus `P≈Q` imply `!P̄≈!Q̄` and `!Q̄` bounded. Theorems 9–10 give SS self-composition; Theorem 10 requires P,F σ-session versions, disjoint network tapes, `!P̄` bounded, and single-session realization. Lemma 14 supplies a **σ-complete single-machine simulator**. GNUC Theorems 2/6 lift poly-time/emulation to multi-instance within its structured IDs; JUC §2 fixes distinct SIDs.
3. **Native signature/laws.** `Sessionσ(P)`/`!Sessionσ(P)` route `(sid,m)` into per-SID state, reject malformed/cross-session inputs, and preserve the output SID. Nonreplicated accepting machines and connectability conditions matter. iUC local `(pid,sid,role)` addresses are not automatically global independent session partitions (§3.4).
4. **Repository/API.** `Symbolic/Semantics.agda:91–119` names fresh values `(κ,j)` and requires injective occurrence naming; this is a finite syntax-occurrence discipline. `SSP/Protocol:153–169` isolates product state. `ITM/Core` has ports/free tensor but no native IITM `!M` population/address dispatcher in the inspected construction.
5. **Class.** **C [INFERENCE]** namespace-and-session construction; **D [INFERENCE]** arbitrary replication of any UC arrow; **A** only existing finite tensor/composition.
6. **Minimal data.** Decidable efficient namespace encoding/renaming, unique state ownership, address validation, separation of per-session random state, environment closure under selecting/relabeling one session, runtime-bounded number of activated sessions, and one reusable simulator respecting σ. For arbitrary fresh-name allocation also prove freshness/equivariance under extension.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: IITM Lemma 14 gives the missing simulator discipline, and Lemma 17 derives replication boundedness for environmentally **strictly** bounded protocols. Control: reusing a mutable key/coin state is joint state, not independent copies; static injectivity is not a dynamic spawn theorem. RUN's varying-index example in M15 blocks a pointwise-negligible hybrid shortcut.
8. **Obstruction vs unknown.** [INFERENCE] Bare monoidal finite tensor cannot encode unbounded fresh populations without new object/state data. Dynamic session API in the repo is unknown/unimplemented, not disproved. IITM §8.2 explicitly warns strict-runtime closure statements fail for merely almost bounded systems (its counterexamples assume time-lock puzzles); no stronger unconditional claim is made here.
9. **Quantifiers/security/error.** Single simulator must handle all SIDs, not `∀sid ∃S_sid` with no uniform implementation. Each E activates polynomially many sessions; a **common** negligible bound controls all selected hybrid positions. Side conditions include boundedness of actual real replicated system.
10. **Obligations.** Build dispatcher and per-session initial state; prove renaming/freshness, no cross-session state access, simulator σ-completeness, environment backtranslation selecting arbitrary session, ancillary coverage, and polynomial aggregate cost/error. Finite QUC error addition is reusable only after those uniform bounds are supplied.

### M07 — Joint-state wrapper / multi-session idealization

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M07; named JUC construction, also expressed through IITM/iUC. Candidate consumers: ITM/PM long-lived resources and symbolic/SSP shared stores; ≥2 families, not current JUC implementations.
2. **Primary contract.** JUC §3, extracted `:84–112`, **Theorem 1**: if joint protocol ρ̂ securely realizes multi-session functionality F̂, then transformed `π^[ρ̂]` emulates π in the F-hybrid model. F̂ routes `(sid,ssid,v)` to an **independent** F instance at ssid; transformed π redirects all its F invocations into one ρ̂ instance, preserving original session as SSID. §4.2 `:176–178` states CRS lower bound `s≤t+u` when realizing s-bit CRS from t-bit CRS with ≤u sent bits per party.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Wrap a family of isolated logical interfaces behind one resource and a router; implementation may share state, specification separates logical instances. The source theorem's security premise is for the **constructed F̂ interface**, not a rule allowing arbitrary shared state.
4. **Repository/API.** `SSP/Protocol:116–159` and `Machines` allow persistent resources; symbolic names distinguish occurrences. Existing generic `UC-compose` can consume a proved wrapper realization, but no inspected repository operator constructs F̂ or proves generic shared-state isolation. `IPDL/Linker:105–131` is a selected shared-coin implementation correspondence, not a JUC theorem.
5. **Class.** **C [INFERENCE]** router/state-ownership construction followed by **A** composition; **D** blanket independent-copy realization from arbitrary shared state.
6. **Minimal data.** Namespace/domain separation, routing inverses, per-session logical state invariant, controlled shared representation, consistent corruption leakage, compatible simulator shared state and cost. These are non-circular structural obligations; the remaining cryptographic claim that a particular shared implementation realizes independent F̂ is a separate proof, not assumed as a generic law.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: JUC's wrapper and session-tagged-signature motivation; source §1 explicitly binds signed messages to the session. Hard source control: CRS lower bound rules out universal noninteractive expansion of short CRS into arbitrary independent longer CRS. [INFERENCE] Simple state control: one cached fair coin shared by two sessions gives equality with probability 1; independent fair coins give 1/2, distinguished by two queries.
8. **Obstruction vs unknown.** Arbitrary shared⇒independent is false. A reusable wrapper with explicit sharing policy is viable; its formal common interface is a proposal. Do not call tensor alone JUC or erase which state is deliberately shared.
9. **Quantifiers/security/error.** One simulator for the multi-session system must serve all sessions/contexts with consistent shared state. Individual standalone simulators need not combine. Quantitative sharing advantage has to be proved at the wrapper interface; it is not necessarily the sum of standalone margins.
10. **Obligations.** Give router operational correspondence, namespace injectivity, logical-state separation or a representation invariant explaining deliberate sharing, corruption/state exposure correspondence, simulator uniformity, and aggregate cost. Then prove F̂ realization and use existing A composition; tests must see all allowed cross-session observations.

### M08 — Global resource access and the management-envelope construction

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M08; GUC/UCGS distinguish global from protocol-local setup; candidate consumers ITM/PM and symbolic/SSP public resources. No new repo node is inferred from the UC variant names.

UCGS Definitions 1–3 define wrapper `M[π,γ]`, γ-subroutine-respecting and φ-regular γ. **Proposition 1**: γ respecting and π γ-respecting make M respecting; additionally γ π-regular and an α invoking at most one π give identical virtual/original transcripts with the same random tape. **Theorem 1** requires ρ,φ,π,γ subroutine exposing; γ φ-regular/respecting; φ,π γ-respecting; ρ `(π,φ,ξ)`-compliant and `(π,M[code,γ],ξ)`-compliant for both codes. Then wrapper-defined ξ security implies replacement security. UCGS `:141–181` is inspected.

Global resources are accessible to external protocols/environment, not privately re-created or chosen by simulator. Regularity forbids γ spawning fresh φ by subroutine-output or containing φ as subsidiary.

Standard A composition is exactly what UCGS seeks to reuse, not a replacement for the envelope proof.

UCGS explicitly budgets manager start at 3k and context at 4k; these are source operational costs, not generic grade facts.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: UCGS Proposition 1 is an exact same-random-tape transcript correspondence; on-demand CRS/clock/registration-based ledger are source regularity examples. Control: arbitrary-address global channel may spawn an unregistered recipient and violates regularity (`:149–155`). Transcript wrapper is exact; cryptographic realization and any approximation remain separate.

### M09 — SUC authenticated router and restricted translation to full UC

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M09; a precise source operational wrapper, not a new generic dummy theorem. Candidate consumers ITM message ports and symbolic/SSP channel interpreters.
2. **Primary contract.** SUC conference §§2.1–2.3, Definition 1; §1.3 states the restricted SUC↔UC translation relationship but defers proofs to full version. Exact full-report translation theorem numbering/side conditions are **not treated as inspected**.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Fixed known parties, Z and A, star router; router checks sender identity, stores messages as a multiset, lets A select a stored message, delivers at most once per stored copy, and erases it. Functionality calls have SID and public header/private body; headers distinguish messages, only header/identities leak. All party↔functionality delivery is A-scheduled; Z communicates directly with A and supplies party inputs/reads outputs. All machines receive `1^n`, Z starts. Subroutine code executes within local party machines.

6. **Minimal data.** Authenticated source identity, destination validation, unique public message handles, hidden private bodies, queue multiplicity/at-most-once removal, fixed role roster, explicit scheduling and corruption rules, plus a source-to-target operational encoding.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: inspected router gives an executable precise contract. Hard source control: SUC §2.2 cannot model immediate local ideal computation such as signatures nor guarantee fairness, because A can delay functionality delivery; §1.3 also notes the limitation. Queues are semantically consequential, not mere wiring aliases.

9. **Quantifiers/security/error.** Definition 1: `∀PPT A ∃PPT S ∀balanced PPT Z ∀constant d ∃negligible μ ∀n ∀|z|≤n^d`, bit-gap ≤ μ(n). Native lifetime time counts security parameter plus accumulated input lengths. Current repository perfect kernels do not instantiate this automatically.

### M12 — Runtime/complexity closure and admissible machine classes

1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M12; native central construction in RUN, IITM and GNUC; candidate consumers ITM interpreter plus SSP/symbolic compiled executions. Not current PPT implementation.
2. **Primary contract.** RUN Definition 7: reactive polynomial S means every a-priori polynomial Z yields w.o.p. polynomial closed runtime, with bound allowed to depend on Z; Definition 8 makes A/S validity relative to protocol. Theorem 16 additionally assumes **actual real composite** σ^π reactive. IITM Definitions 7/8/10 distinguish almost/strict/universal/environmental boundedness; Theorem 8 requires real composite bounded; Lemmas 15–17 give strict-runtime closure under disjoint tapes, controlled one-way message flow, and session replication. GNUC Definition 2 uses one polynomial independent of well-behaved Z in external uninvited flow; bounded adversaries additionally satisfy adversary→protocol flow bounds; Theorem 7 proves substituted protocol poly-time under its structured IDs/substitutability.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Costed execution, input/flow accounting, admission predicate indexed by protocol/context, cutoff approximation and closure under all contexts constructed by composition proofs. These three runtime disciplines are distinct; none is “each activation PPT” alone.
4. **Repository/API.** `ITM/Run:46–47` unbounded iteration; no native step-count/security-parameter certificate in `ITM.Setup`. `UC/QueryBound.agda:100–112,131–138` has an actual potential certificate controlling routed queries/traces, **not TM step complexity/PPT**. `UCSetup/Regrade`/wide-subcategory APIs catalogued by parent are possible reuse sites only once closure is supplied; context-relative admissibility may need more than an arrow predicate.
5. **Class.** **C [INFERENCE]** cost/admission enrichment; **D** automatic closure of component-wise polytime under arbitrary wiring, and **D** RUN's strong-reactive security composition variant.
6. **Minimal data.** Operational cost model, encodings/input-size assumptions, security parameter, resource flow or potential, admission of actual closed executions, and closure under renaming/virtualization/simulator absorption/replication. State exactly whether bounds are `∀E∃p_E` or `∃p∀E`; account for simulator as composed with F, not necessarily in isolation.
7. **Positive/control.** Positive: IITM Lemma 16's polynomial cross-flow bound, GNUC Theorem 7, and RUN's secure-message example (`:692`) where S alone expands a binary length but F+S is valid because F previously received that many bits. Hard controls: IITM §8 `:1297–1305` ping-pong of two individually environmentally bounded machines can run forever. RUN **Theorem 27** (`:702–722`) gives one-subroutine counterexample: π,ρ,σ and both composites strongly reactively polynomial, π emulates ρ in that notion, but replacement fails because no strongly valid simulator exists.
8. **Obstruction vs unknown.** These controls disprove naive runtime closure and an overly strict validity/security combination, not all strict-time source models. IITM/GNUC's positive laws have different hypotheses. No proof or disproof of a concrete native PPT layer for this repository was found; it remains E beyond these specified candidate interfaces.
9. **Quantifiers/security/error.** RUN `∀Z∃p_Z` and GNUC `∃p∀Z` are not silently interchanged. Almost-bounded cutoff introduces negligible failure; it must be uniform enough for the intended hybrid. Simulator validity can be protocol-relative. Strict probability-1 validity cannot automatically be transported across negligible changes (Theorem 27).
10. **Obligations.** Define counted operational executions and all admissibility classes; prove cost of free interpretation/virtualization; closure of identities, composition, tensor and every auxiliary context; show simulator resource validity and cutoff error; only then restrict/regrade the generic UC interface. 1. **ID / consumers / standardness.** M15; central to IITM/RUN/RESP replication proofs, with machine and symbolic/SSP quantitative semantics as ≥2 candidate consumers. Existing finite QUC composition is A coverage, not a native computational theorem.

## Cross-model candidate shortlist and negative controls

[INFERENCE] These are the reusable candidates supported by at least two mapped consumer families, in priority order; none needs a new name for the already-existing generic UC/dummy/composition API.

1. **Pointed-state interpretation/context correspondence** (M04/M05): literal ITM→PM, symbolic initialized roles, SSP pointed protocols and selected IPDL linker squares. Required point/transition/representation squares are concrete and independently testable mathematical obligations; no security premise is substituted for them.
2. **Namespace + state-allocation/session wrapper** (M06/M10): machine session populations and symbolic injective occurrence/SSP per-component state. Add actual dynamic allocation and uniform session contexts; static free tensor is insufficient.
3. **Shared/global resource envelope** (M07/M08): machine resources and symbolic/SSP stores. Separate logical session isolation, deliberate sharing and direct global access. JUC CRS/coin controls and GUC CRS commitment no-go bound the universal claims.
4. **Leakage-equipped state/corruption interface** (M11): machine explicit memory plus symbolic/SSP state. Must respect representation and public status; unqualified leak-all-state is not quotient-safe.
5. **Costed/admissible context closure** (M12/M13/M15): ITM steps and SSP/symbolic interpreted costs/queries. Closure proof includes simulator absorption, responsive routing and uniform hybrids, not a bare arrow predicate called PPT.

Existing A coverage remains: generic UC/dummy/composition/robust characterization; strong monoidal free interpretations and pullback tests; machine pointed-state simulation/sum-trace; exact ITM U preservation/reflection, UC preservation and **simulator-conditioned** UC reflection; uninitialized state-iso test setup. No new B construction identified. Main D witnesses: reinitialized same table with differing initialized outputs; redundant-state non-isomorphism; shared versus independent coin; JUC CRS lower bound; globally fixed CRS commitment impossibility; ping-pong runtime divergence; RUN Theorem 27 strong-validity failure; nonuniformity caveat for reactive-simulator exchange; varying-index negligible hybrid failure; responsive silence/no liveness.

## Evidence and bounded-completeness statement

All named constructed machine nodes have a code boundary, applicable A coverage, structured C candidates, and explicit negative controls. All source families requested by the assignment are assigned to rows. The report does **not** claim an exhaustive catalogue of every UC variant or of every theorem in those papers: bounded standardness means repeated use in the enumerated primary corpus, with exact selected contracts. Uninspected deferred full proofs and the canonical-full-paper access limitation remain explicit E items, not invented theorem numbers. The current project has no demonstrated native PPT machine implementation, generic dynamic session allocator, generic corruption wrapper, or unconditional target-simulator backtranslation in the inspected evidence.

No builds, typechecks, tests, linters, formatters, git validation, repository source/docs writes, or performance measurements were run. This is source/code inspection and a durable local research artifact only.

# Appendix I — ND/TaskPIOA source inventory

# Construction generality: NDA/NDB over PM and SSP, and actual TaskPIOA

## Evidence boundary and result

Read-only primary-literature research; no repository writes, Git validation, builds, typechecks, tests, linters, or formatters. Code cited below was inspected in `/Users/andre/IOHK/categorical-crypto/.worktrees/uc-setup-landscape`. Parent reports docs-only head `b24ae5f92` and unchanged source proof boundary `9f9be2a89`; those hashes were not independently checked. `src/` paths below are relative to that integration root. Paper paths are relative to `docs/kb/sources/papers/`. Paper numbering is from the specifically inspected versions, not silently from later journal versions.

The five assigned setup nodes are real source declarations: `ND.PM.Setup.NDA`, `NDB`; `ND.SSP.Setup.NDA`, `NDB`; `TaskPIOA.Setup.UC.StdSetup`. Their existence does **not** make their native operational structures interchangeable. The ND nodes retain their base model's computations/tests and change grade categories. TaskPIOA instead has finite rational automata, actual task execution and synchronizing product, and a free installation/wiring category with a native evaluation presheaf.

Classification: **A** already parameterized repository API with identified actual instance; **B** a new theorem following from arbitrary setup axioms alone; **C** structured generalization requiring explicit additional operations/laws; **D** a specified extension refuted by a cited theorem/counterexample; **E** an identified unresolved correspondence/proof, not an impossibility claim. No genuinely new B construction was identified in this bounded corpus: the relevant setup-universal composition/dummy results are already A, while the native constructions require C structure. An A component of a larger C construction is subtracted rather than counted as a new construction.

The evidence uses the common brief, coverage map, both scouts, the corrected [construction-generality-generic-apis.md](#appendix-b), and the parent's [construction-generality-structural-evidence.md](#appendix-d). In particular: a bare `UCSetup` requires only `C`, monoidal grades `I`, a graded monad, and a Setoid-valued presheaf (`src/CategoricalCrypto/UCSetup.agda:21–26`). It does not require a tensor on C, probabilities, state, choice, copy, a scheduler, or a trace. `QUCSetup` enriches observations, not automatically carrier arrows, by an error algebra.

**Important mapping correction:** `TaskPIOA/Setup.agda:23` imports `CategoricalCrypto.Standard2 as Standard`; line 95 instantiates `Standard.StdUC Wiringᴹ ℰ-native`. It is **Standard2 under a local alias**, contrary to an earlier scout's attribution to the older Standard module.

## Bounded primary-source corpus

“Standard” here means an actual definition/construction/theorem in the following foundational primary texts, not every construction ever given a similar name. All six core sources were inspected. Public links identify primary texts; the archived texts, rather than search summaries, supply the contracts.

| ID | Inspected version and public citation | Sections/contracts examined | Meaningful exclusions |
|---|---|---|---|
| T | Canetti, Cheung, Kaynar, Liskov, Lynch, Pereira, Segala, *Task-Structured Probabilistic I/O Automata*, preliminary technical-report text dated **6 March 2006**, archived `canetti2006taskpioa.pdf`; [MIT record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/35df713f-23aa-4d27-9ef6-41ff7326a917), [public report copy](https://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/document?doi=e39a815951d25be5a974a40b14a661654a478c21&repid=rep1&type=pdf) | §2 Definitions 1–2, Proposition 1, Theorem 1; §3 Definitions 3–5, Theorem 2, Corollary 1; §5 local schedulers, Definition 6, Theorems 3–5; Appendix A.2 lifting/expansion; Appendix B.3 Definition 15 hiding; Appendix D Examples 1–9; §4 and Appendix E's OT motivation | Not silently the 2018 journal version. Countable/infinite execution and local-scheduler extensions exceed the implemented finite fragment. §4 explicitly delegates computational time bounds, negligible approximation and hardness to the separate OT work; those are not theorems supplied by this paper's exact ≤₀ relation. |
| S | Segala, *Modeling and Verification of Randomized Distributed Real-Time Systems*, MIT PhD thesis **June 1995** (title page `segala1995.txt:1–28`), MIT/LCS/TR-676; [author landing](https://profs.sci.univr.it/~segala/www/phd.html), [MIT record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/0ed3ec02-b703-44eb-8e90-d5d4da218b24) | §4.2 automata, combined transitions/executions; §4.3.1 synchronizing simple-automata product and §4.3.3 limits of generalization; §4.4 renaming/hiding; §§5.2,5.6,5.7 scheduler/adversary schemas; §§7.4–7.5 trace precongruence/principal context; §§8.2,8.5–8.7 simulations and execution correspondence | Not a claim about all timed/liveness results in chapters 9 onward. The archive's June 1995 title page, not conflicting catalogue metadata, determines this version. General probabilistic transitions over actions and states are distinguished from the simple fixed-action transitions used by the product. |
| V | Varacca–Winskel, *Distributing probability over non-determinism*, MSCS 16(1), 2006, archived author copy `varacca2006.txt`; [author PDF](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~gw104/distprobnd_journal.pdf), [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0960129505005074) | §§2–5: real cones, finite nonempty powerset, Proposition 3.2, indexed valuations and the `IV P → P IV` law, convex powerset; §6 Theorems 6.2–6.3 adequacy and §6.5 nonassociativity example | The finite Set construction is not all domain-theoretic variants sketched in §7. “Deterministic scheduler” means nonrandomized history-dependent scheduler, **not** oblivious scheduler. |
| B | Bonchi–Sokolova–Vignudelli, *The Theory of Traces for Systems with Nondeterminism, Probability, and Termination*, LMCS 18(2):21, 2022; archived `bonchi2022.txt`, [arXiv:1808.00923v7](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.00923v7) | §4 convex-set monad C, Lemma 4.2, Theorem 4.7; §5 three termination/observation algebras; §6 generalized determinization, Theorem 6.4 and Theorem 6.8; §7 may/must/may-must, Theorems 7.7–7.11, Definitions 7.12/7.14; §8 randomized resolutions and Theorem 8.4, Proposition 8.13, Remark 8.14 | Finitely branching NPLTS and finite-word tests. Not an identification with all interactive cryptographic environments or with TaskPIOA's static task schedules. Real convex parameters require a separately justified rational restriction for this repository. |
| Z | Zwart–Marsden, *Don't Try This at Home: No-Go Theorems for Distributive Laws*, **arXiv:1811.06460v2, 13 December 2018**, long preprint associated with the 2019 LICS work; archived `zwart2019nogo.txt`; [primary version](https://arxiv.org/abs/1811.06460v2) | Definition 2.10 Beck laws, Definitions 2.28/3.2, Counterexample 3.1, Theorem 3.5, Corollary 3.7, Examples 3.8–3.9 | Numbering is not claimed for the short LICS paper or the later journal article. Conclusions concern specified Set-monads and genuine distributive laws, not all mixed semantics or ND grade functors. |
| F | Forster et al., *Quantitative Graded Semantics and Spectra of Behavioural Metrics*, **arXiv:2306.01487v4, 27 January 2025**, archived `forster2023.txt`; [primary version](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.01487v4), [CSL 2025](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CSL.2025.33) | Definitions 2.1/2.5, 3.1, Theorem 3.2; Definitions 4.1/4.4, Theorem 5.5; Definition 5.7, Example 5.8, Lemmas 5.9–5.10; Definitions 6.1/6.4, Theorem 6.6; Definitions 7.3/7.5, Theorem 7.6 | Depth-indexed metric semantics is not the repository's interface/resource grade category. No generic characterization of a cryptographic U-kernel by unary modal tests is inferred. |

Archive provenance/version list: `INDEX.md:28–29,38,54–55,106–107,111–115`. Direct web reads of MIT/author landing pages returned HTTP 403; this did not prevent inspection of the archived primary texts. arXiv version metadata was also read directly. No missing semantic claim is filled in from a web summary.

It does not make the environment itself oblivious. |

Only this SSP instance has the inspected B-to-A nonreflection theorem; no PM nonreflection theorem is asserted. |

Rows N01,N05–N16 | Finite state/action/task codes, normalized finite rational kernels, one initial state, finite precommitted task lists, disabled-task stutter.
