There is no independently named ND, SSP, or TaskPIOA QUC node in the census. SSP does have a quantitative readout precursor (`SSP/Observation.agda:69–89`); PM has actual `QSetupᵒ` (`UC/Model/Quantitative.agda:19–25`). Do not count a potential generic factory instantiation as a constructed node. IPDL is an interpreter, CSP unintegrated/in progress, and causal boxes/RSIM planned: none is counted as a consumer setup here.

## Minimal reusable signatures, kept separate

These are proposed structured interfaces or already-existing APIs, not additional axioms smuggled into `UCSetup`.

1. **Choice-factor/relabeling interface — already A.** Symmetric monoidal carrier M; `C : Set → Obj M`; list fold `C⟦S⃗⟧`; partial-injective site relabelings with fibre maps; contravariant realizations `⌈ρ⌉ : C⟦T⃗⟧ → C⟦S⃗⟧` preserving identity, composition, concatenation and unit/associator regroupings. Actual consumers: ND.PM and ND.SSP. No semilattice or convex law is part of this signature.
2. **Mixed-choice algebra/effect — C.** A selected distribution/subdistribution monad D, barycentric operations, a semilattice operation, an explicitly selected interaction law, and a termination convention. For the convex-set solution, `C X = nonempty finitely generated convex subsets of D X`, `S ⊔ T = conv(S∪T)`, and `S +p T = {pμ+(1−p)ν}`. For indexed histories choose IV instead; do not assert ordinary P∘D is its composite monad. Rational kernels in SSP and TaskPIOA are two actual probabilistic ingredients, but neither current setup is thereby a C-algebra-of-protocols instance.
3. **Execution with information-disciplined scheduling — C.** States/initialization; actions/tasks and kernel; an explicitly declared scheduler view/admissibility relation; run/trace pushforward; composition of schedules or a prefix-dependent translator that cannot inspect forbidden branch state; product projection/extension laws. TaskPIOA supplies a static task word; ND.PM supplies request/reply lifetime selection; ND.SSP supplies pick-ahead queues. These are three distinct concrete consumers, not interchangeable instantiations without representation theorems.
4. **State/coupling simulation to observations — C.** Initial-state relation, lawful distribution relation lifting with specified marginals, step/input/output compatibility, fixed admissible schedule translation, tensor closure for separated states, and a run/readout lemma. Two actual state-map special cases: TaskPIOA `Simulation` and SSP `_≲ᵖ_`; their probability/equality and scheduling layers differ. The more general task distribution simulation additionally needs convex expansion on **execution distributions**.
5. **Determinized finite-word semantics — C.** Monad M on Set, Moore observation object O with an M-algebra `a:M O→O`, system `c:X→O×(M X)^A`, and the induced monad-over-Moore-functor law; for up-to reasoning, compatible algebraic context closure. This is not a monad-over-monad P/D law. Any TaskPIOA or ND consumer needs a scheduler-respecting transition encoding and test correspondence first.
6. **Metric/depth semantics — C.** Met-enriched graded monad `(M_n,η,μ)`, system functor G, nonexpansive `α:G→M_1`, selected trace tensor, and nonexpansive/readout-density laws. A later QUC observation presheaf and one fixed simulator are additional data. A generic Approx-valued presheaf alone supplies none of this structure.

## Full construction rows

### N01 — Generic setup construction, U closure, dummy simulation and serial UC composition (A)

1. **Consumers/status.** All five assigned setup nodes are actual source-level consumers of the generic UC core; TaskPIOA additionally constructs its native-tested wiring setup. This row is coverage, **not a newly discovered construction**.
2. **Primary contract.** Inspected repository declarations are the primary evidence for this generic API. Native comparison: T §2 Theorem 1 ([report](https://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/document?doi=e39a815951d25be5a974a40b14a661654a478c21&repid=rep1&type=pdf), PDF lines 69–77) is a different contextual-product theorem, not an automatic instance merely because both are called composition.
3. **Signature/laws.** `StdUC M E` constructs a setup from a monoidal carrier and test presheaf. `f≈ᵁg` means `∀W. μ W X ∘ T₁ W f ≈ℰ μ W X ∘ T₁ W g`. `f≤UCg` is `∀a:X→X′. ∃s:Y→X′. sub(a)f≈ᵁsub(s)g`. Dummy completeness is equivalent to `∃s₀:Y→X. f≈ᵁsub(s₀)g`; `UC-compose` maps `f≤g,h≤k` to `h∙f≤k∙g`.
4. **Repository.** `Abstract2.agda:45–46,131–137,156–176`; TaskPIOA `Setup.agda:53–95`, especially the real `evaluate`, `pull`, `plugging`, and `ℰ-native`; concrete ND setup declarations in the table above.
5. **Class.** A. The universal core already exists; native synchronization and schedule translation remain separately classified C.
6. **Exact instances.** TaskPIOA uses `Standard2.StdUC Wiringᴹ ℰ-native`. ND uses generic regrading of `StdUC 𝔾ᵒ ℰᵒ` / `StdUC Protᴹ ℰᵖ` through its symmetric bundles and choice factors; see N02.
7. **Positive/control.** ND.PM and ND.SSP are two actual consumers; TaskPIOA is a third. Free wiring interpretation is positive algebraic structure. Bare automaton trace inclusion is a nonexample of a sufficient UC witness (N11).
8. **Obstruction scope.** No negative result about these implemented generic theorems. The obstruction is to relabeling native product/scheduler theorems as already proved by them: a monoidal syntax category does not establish every native network equation.
9. **Security/error.** Simulator precedes all ancilla/test quantification; it may depend on attack a, and the dummy formulation supplies a single test-independent s₀. This is exact security, not an availability/liveness assertion and not native `∀Eρ∃ρ′`.
10. **Obligations.** Reuse, do not reimplement, this API. For new native interpretations prove category/coherence and test-pullback equations, then ancilla coverage and simulator representability for any requested reflection. No new B theorem is needed for ordinary serial composition.

### N02 — Grade extension/restriction and site relabeling (A)

1. **Consumers/status.** NDA/NDB over both PM and SSP; actual generic structure with concrete realizations, not intrinsic nondeterminism.
2. **Primary contract.** Repository `ND.Grade`, `ND.A`, and `ND.Relabel` are the defining primary source. Comparison contracts are V §§2–4 ([PDF](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~gw104/distprobnd_journal.pdf)) and B §4 ([v7](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.00923v7)): their semilattice/convex structures contain laws absent from these grade records.
3. **Signature/laws.** Grade `(X,S⃗)` realizes as `X⊗C⟦S⃗⟧`; tensor concatenates sites. B permits every base arrow between realized grades. A requires `s≈u⊗⌈ρ⌉`; `ρ:S⃗⇝T⃗` routes each source site to at most one target with a pick map and injectivity. Realization is a contravariant monoidal functor on these routings with explicit regrouping laws.
4. **Repository.** `ND/Grade.agda:42–107` constructs B and proves inclusion full/essentially surjective; `ND/Relabel.agda:60–62` is the exact routing record; `ND/A.agda:59–70,131–184,189–205` gives `Relabelling`, `Relabels`, NDA, A⇒B and the characterization by a relabel-shaped dummy simulator. Concrete instantiations: PM `Setup:25–58`; SSP `Setup:35–37,102–154`.
5. **Class.** A; D only for the unrestricted reflection extension in N03.
6. **Exact instance.** `(𝔾ˢ,C,relabelling,ℰᵒ)` for PM and `(Protˢ,Cᴵ,relabelling,ℰᵖ)` for SSP. This is generic `Abstract2.Regrade.regraded`, not a new powerset or scheduler implementation.
7. **Positive/control.** Both PM and SSP satisfy the same abstract relabeling laws, despite different operational pick protocols. Copying one real pick to two ideal sites is allowed in SSP B but excluded in A.
8. **Obstruction scope.** [INFERENCE] The record fields alone cannot establish `x⊔x=x`, convex closure, demonic infima, or a Segala transition relation: no such operations occur in the signature. A new choice site changes the grade; an unqualified same-hom idempotence claim may even be ill-typed without a specified grade comparison.
9. **Security/error.** B UC is equivalent to base-model UC at the realized grades, by fullness/essential surjectivity. A⇒B⇒base uses the same available simulator and observations. A is a restriction of **grade converters**, not proof that residual scheduling or every environment is static. An arbitrary PM B converter can respond online through its interface; this does not grant access to another machine's hidden state.
10. **Obligations.** Any literature-identification theorem needs an operational scheduler translation, admissibility/information law, observation correspondence and grade/simulator fullness. None follows from naming the categories A/B.

### N03 — B-to-A reflection fails at SSP, with exact pick-copying scope (D)

1. **Consumers/status.** `ND.SSP.Setup.NDA/NDB`; inspected source proof, not a new check. No analogous PM theorem is asserted.
2. **Primary contract.** `ND/SSP/Reflect.agda:50–151` is primary counterexample evidence. V §3 ([PDF](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~gw104/distprobnd_journal.pdf)) is a separate algebraic no-go, not the explanation/proof of this result.
3. **Signature/laws.** `once` consumes one Boolean site's pick and returns it. `twice` consumes one pick from each of two Boolean sites and returns the first. The theorem exhibits `f≤UC_B g` and `¬(f≤UC_A g)` at grades `(unit,[Bool])` and `(unit,[Bool,Bool])`, source interface unit and public Boolean export Ω.
4. **Repository.** `once/twice/f/g:50–62`; B simulator `copy:68–73`, state-map `copy-ᵖ:75–83`, `B-half:85–92`; pinning/routing and test `:98–127`; `refute:129–133`, `A-half:135–144`, existential `¬reflect:147–151`.
5. **Class.** D for unrestricted reflection of A→B on SSP UC order; A preservation from N02 remains valid.
6. **Missing data.** Reverse transport would need each relevant B simulator represented, up to the required U agreement, by an A-grade morphism. This counterexample denies that fullness/representability here. It cannot be repaired merely by using identical tests.
7. **Positive/control.** B's concrete `copy` forwards each incoming pick to both ideal queues, keeping them equal. The A case allows an arbitrary ordinary component u and any allowed relabeling; the counterexample does not just test identity relabelings.
8. **Counterexample.** The tester queues `true` once at the real site, then asks the Boolean export. `once` returns true. Any A relabeling forwards this one pick to zero or one ideal sites, so `twice` reaches an empty queue and dies. u never runs on this test. Thus true-verdict mass is 1 versus 0; the proof refutes the ≤1/2 observation bound. This is duplication/resource-routing failure, **not** a secret-coin peeking example, not a distributive-law counterexample, and not all-model nonreflection.
9. **Security/error.** Failure already occurs for exact UC despite the same base tests; the explicit distinguishing advantage exceeds 1/2. This does not automatically establish a separately defined quantitative ND theorem.
10. **Obligations.** A restricted reflection theorem must identify a genuinely relabel-representable simulator class or a different allowed routing discipline. Merely assuming the desired reflection would be circular; changing A to allow copying changes the model rather than proving the existing extension.

### N04 — Designated choice execution: lifetime selectors versus queued picks (C; already source-specific)

1. **Consumers/status.** Actual PM and SSP choice interpreters; common grade API exists, common native choice algebra has not thereby been implemented.
2. **Primary contract.** S §§4.2/5.2 and V §6 ([thesis](https://profs.sci.univr.it/~segala/www/phd.html), [V PDF](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~gw104/distprobnd_journal.pdf)) define native transition/scheduler choice. Code provides the distinct local contracts below.
3. **Signature/laws.** PM `choose:(S→Proc⁺ S⃗ A B)→Proc⁺(S∷S⃗) A B` requests a choice at first activation, saves that activation, initializes only the selected branch after a response, and keeps that branch for the resource lifetime. SSP `choose i k` is an instruction inside a call tree; `resolve` pops a site's oldest queued value on **each occurrence**, with empty pop interpreted as dead.
4. **Repository.** PM `ND/PM/Choice.agda:48–93` (`fresh/wait/active`, `choose-request`, `choose-resume`), graded composition/tensor `:99–121`. SSP `ND/SSP/Choice.agda:112–155,168–218,228–278`; FIFO `push/pop`, `resolve`, compilation `⌈_⌉`, and simulation preservation. SSP source explicitly explains that compilation lands in graded homs, not a `StdUCMorphism` (`:16–22`).
5. **Class.** C for a reusable interpreter/adequacy construction. Its site-relabel algebra is already A (N02).
6. **Minimal extras.** A choice request protocol, allocation/lifetime semantics, state/initialization, a persistent store or queue discipline, failure/suspension behavior, and naturality of interpretation under grade concatenation. To add internal nondeterministic choice, also specify a hiding/resolution semantics rather than treating the public pick interface as already hidden.
7. **Positive/control.** PM and SSP are two actual interpreter consumers of choice grades. They are not equivalent consumers of one lifetime convention: repeated SSP `choose` consumes fresh queued entries while PM's new outer choice site selects a single branch once.
8. **Concrete obstruction.** [INFERENCE from `resolve` and `evalC`] In SSP, at a fixed one-site grade, `choose i (λ_→ret true)` dies on an export activation with no queued pick, whereas `plainᶜ(ret true)` returns true. Thus ordinary internal-choice idempotence cannot be imported just by erasing the designated-choice interface. PM permits suspension awaiting a reply; SSP call trees cannot suspend that activation, as its actual interpreter shows. Neither difference is a theorem of bare UCSetup.
9. **Security/error.** Retained base observations distinguish failure and verdict mass. Picks may be supplied adaptively between SSP calls; “pick-ahead” does not mean a globally fixed schedule. Fixed A routing does not remove the choosing environment's adaptivity. Interpreter simulation is a proof witness, not an adversarial simulator converter.
10. **Obligations.** Formulate lifetime-indexed choice interfaces; prove graded identity/composition/tensor interpretation equations and test backtranslation. Any semantic equivalence to a standard NPLTS requires a precisely chosen resolution class and a theorem respecting pick consumption, timing and dead behavior.

### N05 — Native probabilistic combination, combined transitions and convex semilattices (C, with existing probability infrastructure subtracted)

1. **Consumers/status.** SSP and TaskPIOA already consume normalized finite rational distributions; PM has its separate partial-distribution carrier. The mixed powerset/convex transition construction is not supplied by NDA/NDB.
2. **Primary contract.** S §4.2.2 and Proposition 4.2.1 (`segala1995.txt:1973–2011`); B §4.1–4.2, Lemma 4.2, Theorem 4.7 (`bonchi2022.txt:1555–1599,1707–1745,1941–1960`), [B v7](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.00923v7).
3. **Signature/laws.** Segala combines finite/countable outgoing transitions from the same source with nonnegative weights summing at most 1; residual weight is the stopping outcome. Combined-combined transitions flatten to combined transitions. For normalized mixed choice, B's `C X` consists of nonempty finitely generated convex subsets of finitely supported distributions; Dirac singleton is unit, multiplication chooses a distribution from each inner convex set and flattens with the outer weights. `⊔` is associative/commutative/idempotent; `+p` satisfies barycentric associativity, symmetry `x+py=y+1−p x`, idempotence, and `(x⊔y)+p z=(x+p z)⊔(y+p z)`. Convex absorption `x⊔y⊔(x+p y)=x⊔y` follows. C is not the ordinary finite-powerset-of-distributions composite monad.
4. **Repository.** `ProbabilisticLogic/Distribution/RationalDist.agda:40–76,176–203,351–367` already supplies normalized finite rational distributions, return/bind, equality by all rational rewards, and map laws. SSP `Protocol.agda:37–41` / ND `Choice:112–117` has rational Boolean coins. TaskPIOA `Core:49–55,94–106` uses rational kernels and bind. No C-algebra of protocol homs, powerset transition carrier, or arbitrary combined transition closure occurs in these inspected ND/Task modules.
5. **Class.** C for mixed-choice structure/representation. Existing rational distribution operations are already reusable carrier infrastructure, not a new setup-universal construction.
6. **Minimal extras.** Choose normalization versus subdistribution/cemetery semantics, rational versus real coefficients, and a lawful mixed effect such as C. Give hom/transition operations and prove the selected algebraic equations survive interpretation and observation. Neither QUC error enrichment nor a list of choice sites supplies these laws.
7. **Positive/control.** Actual probabilistic consumers: SSP coins and TaskPIOA kernels. Mathematical mixed-choice examples: B's C and the max/min/interval observation algebras. Plain finite sets of distributions fail the required convex closure: `{δ0,δ1}` omits `½δ0+½δ1`.
8. **Obstruction scope.** [INFERENCE] Under pointwise Minkowski combination, the preceding two-element set violates probabilistic idempotence, because `S+½S` contains the mixed distribution absent from S. This does not refute convex semilattices; their convex closure is exactly the repair. D for a genuine P/D distributive law is N06, not a ban on mixed systems.
9. **Security/error.** A randomized resolver can realize convex mixtures; a deterministic **oblivious** task word need not realize them. An existential or adversarial choice of grade converter is not an infimum/max observation. Probabilistic algebra equations concern a chosen native equality, not automatically all joint/ancilla tests. Missing verdict mass must not be normalized away.
10. **Obligations.** Prove a finite rational presentation/adequacy theorem; establish closure under substitution/product with the exact scheduler class; prove observation/test factorization and ancilla compatibility. For errors, prove mixture/transition nonexpansiveness in the chosen metric before reusing QUC arithmetic.

### N06 — Ordinary probability/powerset distributive laws: precise no-go (D)

1. **Consumers/status.** Negative control for attempts to obtain Segala/demonic semantics from PM/SSP choice grades or a generic setup; no current ND API is accused of violating this theorem.
2. **Primary contract.** V Proposition 3.2 (`varacca2006.txt:435–485`, appendix `:1314–1463`), finite valuations V and **finite nonempty** powerset P, no `V P⇒P V`; Z Definition 2.10, Counterexample 3.1, Theorem 3.5/Corollary 3.7/Examples 3.8–3.9 (`zwart2019nogo.txt:306–307,799–898,919–995,1067–1099`), [Z v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1811.06460v2).
3. **Signature/laws.** A Beck law is a natural transformation `λ:S T⇒T S` compatible with **both** units and **both** multiplications of the stated Set-monads. Z's algebraic theorem assumes stable universal representative term sets TP/TV; binary p in P and v in V; p commutative and idempotent; every TP term equal to p(1,2) uses only variables 1,2; v idempotent; a TV term equal to variable x uses only x; a TV term equal to v(1,2) cannot use only variable 1 or only variable 2. Then no composite theory of P and V exists, hence no `V P⇒P V`. These variable-support assumptions are essential, not just the word “idempotent”.
4. **Repository.** `ND/Grade:42–107` and `ND/A:59–70` have none of these P/D monad fields. The theorem does not apply to `ιB`, `incl`, or site-list concatenation as though they were distributive laws.
5. **Class.** D for the specified unrestricted ordinary-monad combination. Z Example 3.8 states no `D P⇒P D`, `P D⇒D P`, `P P⇒P P`, or `D D⇒D D` for its finite powerset P and finite probability-distribution D on Set.
6. **Minimal alternatives.** Change the carrier/equational theory (convex sets C or indexed valuations IV), or weaken the requested notion and reprove its actual laws. Replacing a genuine Beck law by a functor-over-Moore law is a different construction, not a solution to the same no-go target.
7. **Positive/control.** B's convex-set monad and V's indexed-valuation construction exist. Z Counterexample 3.10 notes that Reader can distribute over itself: it lacks the commutative term required by this argument, so the theorem is not “idempotence always forbids distribution”.
8. **Concrete contradiction.** Z uses `X={a,b,c,d}` and `Ξ=½{a,b}+½{c,d}`. Naturality under maps `f1:(a,b,c,d)↦(a,b,a,b)` and `f2↦(a,b,b,a)` plus a unit law forces λ(Ξ) to contain only Dirac distributions. Under `f3↦(a,a,c,c)`, the other unit law forces its image to be the singleton mixed distribution `½δa+½δc`. A set of Diracs cannot have that image. This is a four-element, unit/naturality obstruction. [INFERENCE] The same explicit calculation uses rational ½, so merely restricting the probability coefficients in that calculation to rationals does not remove it.
9. **Security/error.** This is an algebraic existence result, not a UC insecurity theorem, a quantitative lower bound, or a claim that restricted simulators fail. It prevents invoking the nonexistent monad structure as the justification for composition.
10. **Obligations.** State exact monads, direction and law set before applying a no-go. For alternatives prove their own representation and scheduler adequacy. Do not overstate the result as no monad structure whatsoever on every mixed carrier; that stronger assertion needs its own precise theorem.

### N07 — Indexed computations versus convex closure: two different scheduler adequacies (C)

1. **Consumers/status.** V's alternative mixed-effect construction is native literature structure, not an implemented interpretation of either ND node. PM lifetime states and SSP queues are potential operational data to compare, not already IV indices.
2. **Primary contract.** V Definitions 4.1–4.5, Proposition 4.6, §4.3, Proposition 5.7, Theorems 6.2–6.3, §6.5 (`varacca2006.txt:492–529,536–627,688–692,816–837,1150–1161,1230–1275`), [author PDF](https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/~gw104/distprobnd_journal.pdf).
3. **Signature/laws.** An indexed valuation is a finite family `(x_i,p_i)` modulo reindexing of positive-weight entries and removal of zeros; equal outcomes may retain distinct computation indices. The quasi-cone theory drops `(p+q)x=px+qx`. The law `IV(PX)→P(IVX)` sends `(S_i,p_i)` to all `(h(i),p_i)` with `h(i)∈S_i`. This composes to P∘IV. V's small imperative language then has `[[c]]:Σ→P(IVΣ)` adequate for deterministic history-dependent schedulers; its convex valuation semantics `Σ→P_TM(VΣ)` is adequate for randomized schedulers.
4. **Repository.** ND choice interpreter states/queues are at N04's paths; no valuation-index quotient, P∘IV monad, or V-language adequacy theorem is present in those inspected modules. Rational distribution bind exists, but merges observations extensionally, not as this indexed semantics.
5. **Class.** C. This is a positive alternative to N06, not an arbitrary-UCSetup result.
6. **Minimal extras.** Explicit computation indices/history, reindexing equivalence, positive weights, quasi-cone operations, choice-function distribution, and a finite operational language/run relation. For the convex alternative replace histories by appropriate randomized resolution closure and prove adequacy again.
7. **Positive/control.** The two denotational constructions in V are positive native examples. Repo candidate consumers are ND.PM and ND.SSP after an explicit encoding; their existing semantics must not be advertised as such an encoding. Deterministic/nonrandomized and oblivious/static are independent restrictions.
8. **Counterexample.** V §6.5: `c1` samples X uniformly from {0,1}; `c2` overwrites X with 0; `c3` nondeterministically sets X to 0 or 1. Naively binding sets of ordinary distributions by a selector depending only on the current state gives `{δ0,½δ0+½δ1,δ1}` for `c1;(c2;c3)` but only `{δ0,δ1}` for `(c1;c2);c3`. Merging histories erased the information used by later deterministic scheduling; naive composition is nonassociative.
9. **Security/error.** Indexed paths can reveal more information than permitted to a cryptographic scheduler. Adequacy for perfect-history deterministic schedulers is not soundness for T's oblivious residual scheduler. Conversely randomized convex closure changes attainable behavior. Neither adequacy theorem quantifies over UC simulator converters.
10. **Obligations.** Give a history/view-erasure representation respecting the selected scheduler discipline, then prove interpretation compositional and observation adequate. Do not assume index visibility, randomized schedulers, or convex closure are harmless abstraction steps.

### N08 — Task execution lifting and scheduler information restrictions (C; state-peeking extension D)

1. **Consumers/status.** TaskPIOA's finite execution is actual. ND.PM/ND.SSP are comparison consumers with distinct request/queue scheduling, not instances obtained simply by renaming their grades.
2. **Primary contract.** T §1 scheduling separation, §2 Definition 1 and Proposition 1 (`canetti2006taskpioa.pdf:21,46–63`), §5 local scheduler extension (`:125–149`); S §§5.2,5.6 (`segala1995.txt:3937–3965,4542–4610`), [T report](https://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/document?doi=e39a815951d25be5a974a40b14a661654a478c21&repid=rep1&type=pdf).
3. **Signature/laws.** T: input-enabled, transition-deterministic PIOA; partition of locally controlled output/internal actions; at most one enabled action per task per state. A fixed task word ρ acts on execution distributions: execute the unique enabled action, or retain the fragment unchanged if disabled. `apply(μ,ρτ)=apply(apply(μ,ρ),τ)`; native infinite schedules use limits. Proposition 1 realizes the result by an underlying PIOA scheduler. S's partial-information scheduler is not just a state predicate: equivalent histories must make correlated choices through specified transition maps preserving mixtures.
4. **Repository.** TaskPIOA `Core.agda:36–81` supplies signatures/input/transition/action determinism; `Schedule=List Task:83–84`; `step/tick/runFrom/traceDist:94–106`; `disabled-stutters:108–112`. `Product.agda:97–112` explicitly represents an adversarial automaton separately from the residual task word. Only finite lists, finite state/action codes and finite rational kernels are implemented here.
5. **Class.** C for structured run/scheduler lifting. D for allowing the residual scheduler to inspect arbitrary hidden state while claiming unchanged security. The native local-scheduler extension is C but not this code's current automaton representation.
6. **Minimal extras.** State/kernel and a scheduler-view interface; a finite-word action and execution pushforward; enabled/disabled semantics; admissible schedule translations closed under product. For local scheduling remove action determinism only with component-local policies, composed from local policies rather than arbitrary joint-state controllers. Fairness/liveness are separate assumptions, not implied by finite task words.
7. **Positive/control.** TaskPIOA itself is the direct positive consumer; SSP/PM finite run models can consume a general information-disciplined run interface only with their own activation rules. T's explicit adaptive adversary/environment can use its own history/state while the residual task scheduler stays oblivious: these are compatible, not contradictory requirements.
8. **Coin-guess control.** [INFERENCE, elementary finite automaton] A hidden fair toss stores b; two enabled one-shot tasks guess0/guess1 output whether their fixed guess equals b and then terminate. Every precommitted word making a guess has success 1/2, and words making no guess have success 0. A state-peeking scheduler choosing guess_b after the toss succeeds with probability 1. Randomizing an independent precommitted word does not improve 1/2. An explicit environment cannot implement the peeker unless an interface exposes b. This refutes the scheduler-class enlargement, not TaskPIOA's own static semantics and not all B-grade simulators. T Appendix D Example 3 gives the related primary Early/Late/Toss correlation comparison (`PDF:656–658`).
9. **Security/error.** Exact execution lifting fixes ρ before coins; an admissible translated word may depend on the source task prefix, not branch state. A bound proved only for static ρ is not a bound for all state-aware policies. Disabled stutter preserves total trace probability but says nothing about starvation/availability. S's deterministic-versus-randomized reduction in Proposition 5.7.1 has execution-based adversary and finitely-satisfiable execution-event premises; it is not a universal scheduler-elimination theorem.
10. **Obligations.** Prove run concatenation/linearity and scheduler admissibility under compiler/product; define any fair/infinite extension separately; prove view-respecting rather than full-state Skolemization; for local policies maintain component locality under composition. No native task-PIOA equivalence follows from N02's relabeling names.

### N09 — Scheduling/interleaving and synchronizing product, versus free wiring tensor (C; algebraic syntax A)

1. **Consumers/status.** TaskPIOA has the actual wired product; SSP and PM have their own separated-state tensor and graded composition. Common state product is insufficient to identify their synchronization semantics.
2. **Primary contract.** T §2 product and Theorem 1 (`PDF:63–77`); S §4.3.1 and §4.3.3 (`segala1995.txt:2885–2901,3497–3664`), [thesis](https://profs.sci.univr.it/~segala/www/phd.html).
3. **Signature/laws.** Compatible PIOAs have disjoint output owners and no hidden-action collision with the other alphabet. Product states are pairs. A shared action executes both kernels independently; a nonparticipating component stutters. Task partition is the disjoint union. Product schedule is a **joint word** in the sum of task sets, not merely two projected words. Native associativity/reassociation and schedule correspondence justify contextual composition. S's safe simple-automata product fixes one action per transition; §4.3.3 explains obstacles to naively synchronizing general probabilistic choices over actions.
4. **Repository.** `TaskPIOA/Core.agda:117–128` split input-name embeddings/wiring, `:139–164` product signature; `Product.agda:18–73` receive/product kernel/product/synchronization equations; `:75–84` schedule projections; `Setup.agda:28–54` free generators install/connect and their genuine set interpretation. SSP `Protocol.agda:122–159` uses call-tree inlining and channel-routed independent state pairs instead, and ND graded operations concatenate sites.
5. **Class.** C for native product/run correspondence. Free-monoidal syntax/coherence and the resulting standard setup are already A; do not list them as new native constructions.
6. **Minimal extras.** Typed port ownership, collision-free renaming/embeddings, input enabledness, local kernel product, retained joint scheduling, and reassociation/projection/extension equations. A reusable theorem needs a native-state interpretation and observable-trace naturality, not just a monoidal object tensor.
7. **Positive/control.** TaskPIOA is an actual synchronization consumer; SSP is an actual separated-state composition consumer of the smaller state-map pattern. Two independent installed TaskPIOAs inhabit the wiring tensor but are connected only by a `connect` generator; tensor alone does not execute or choose an interleaving.
8. **Obstruction.** [INFERENCE] Joint words L;R and R;L have the same separate projections but can emit visible traces `[left,right]` and `[right,left]`. Erasing their order is not a harmless representation quotient. General shared hidden state also invalidates independent product kernels; independent state pairs must not be conflated with copied/shared randomness (parent structural evidence X2/X3).
9. **Security/error.** `product-simulation` preserves the existing same-task simulation (N12), which then yields the actual security bridge. Native contextual product closure uses E composed with the third component and preserves `∀Eρ∃ρ′`; it does not manufacture a test-independent simulator. Approximate product replacement needs an error bound for both component uses and no implicit commutativity of a generic error algebra.
10. **Obligations.** Prove the native associativity/wiring invariance at the desired automaton equivalence, track schedule reorderings rather than erase them, establish all-context closure/test transport, and preserve independent-state assumptions. A free wiring category does not claim arbitrary nested products are equal as literal automaton records.

### N10 — Action renaming and operational hiding, distinct from restricting tests (C)

1. **Consumers/status.** Native T/S operations; TaskPIOA has hidden actions and output-erasure during execution but no hiding generator in its inspected setup syntax. ND relabeling is a different already-A operation on choice sites.
2. **Primary contract.** T Appendix B.3 **Definition 15** (`PDF:399`), `hide(P,S)` for `S⊆O`; S §4.4.1–4.4.2 (`segala1995.txt:3673–3693`), [T report](https://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/document?doi=e39a815951d25be5a974a40b14a661654a478c21&repid=rep1&type=pdf).
3. **Signature/laws.** Hiding leaves states/start/transitions intact and changes output O to O\S and hidden H to H∪S; actions are suppressed in public traces, not deleted from execution. Renaming pushes action labels through an injective map (S discusses relaxing injectivity without mixing external/internal actions). [INFERENCE] A task-preserving finite implementation should retain the tasks and reinterpret the local-action tags, so hiding must not turn a formerly adaptive scheduling choice into a new scheduler privilege.
4. **Repository.** TaskPIOA `Core:86–99` erases a hidden action's emission while executing its kernel. `Setup:28–31` has only `install` and `connect`; `productSignature` retains both components' outputs. Therefore wired synchronization does **not automatically hide** the wired output. No action-hiding constructor/theorem is present in the inspected Core/Product/Simulation/Setup/Security modules. SSP choice interfaces are external protocol factors, not automatically hidden nondeterministic sites.
5. **Class.** C. Generic reobservation is A only after one has a lawful new test presheaf/kernel implication; it is not an implementation of this operational operation.
6. **Minimal extras.** A finite public/private action split, typed inclusion/erasure and task transport; input ownership constraints; run pushforward equation `traceDist(hide M,ρ)=D(erase_S)(traceDist(M,ρ))`; test-closing/backtranslation for hidden interfaces. Private linking additionally needs a wiring policy, not just trace erasure.
7. **Positive/control.** TPIOA hidden transitions are an actual run-level consumer. ND.PM and ND.SSP can hide a choice channel only after specifying a concrete chooser/closure; their relabel maps alone are not that chooser. Native Segala automata are the primary positive renaming/hiding example.
8. **Obstruction.** [INFERENCE] A hidden action can alter the next visible reply, so removing it from the transition system is not hiding. A hidden action that changes scheduler observations can invalidate a previously allowed policy. Forgetting all wired outputs also changes the actual TaskPIOA test traces, since `observe` returns both component output alphabets.
9. **Security/error.** Deterministic trace erasure preserves equality of distributions and is nonexpansive for appropriate bounded-observation metrics, but reflection loses information. A native UC preservation theorem needs context/simulator translation; raw erasure is not enough. Hidden failures cannot be normalized away.
10. **Obligations.** Define and prove native hide/rename on the finite syntax and schedules; prove compatibility with synchronization/ownership; then build the observation comparison. Keep private port hiding, test restriction and grade relabeling as distinct signatures.

### N11 — Trace inclusion, contextual closure and native compositional implementation (C; bare-trace shortcut D)

1. **Consumers/status.** TaskPIOA's finite contextual relation is actual. Segala's contextual/principal construction is primary comparison theory, not an existing ND model identification.
2. **Primary contract.** T Definition 2/Theorem 1 (`PDF:69–77`), Appendix D Example 6 (`:668–674`); S Example 7.4.1, Theorem 7.5.1/Corollary 7.5.2 (`segala1995.txt:7731–7747,7840–7881`), [S](https://profs.sci.univr.it/~segala/www/phd.html), [T](https://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/document?doi=e39a815951d25be5a974a40b14a661654a478c21&repid=rep1&type=pdf).
3. **Signature/laws.** T comparable automata share public inputs/outputs, not private states/tasks. `M≤₀N` iff for every compatible closing E, every task-scheduled trace distribution of M∥E occurs for N∥E. Quantifiers: `∀E ∀ρ ∃ρ′. Dist(M,E,ρ)=Dist(N,E,ρ′)`. Product closure reassociates the third component into E. S refines raw trace-distribution inclusion to the coarsest contained precongruence; its principal context characterizes that precongruence by one specially expressive context.
4. **Repository.** `TaskPIOA/Security.agda:23–37` implements comparability and the exact finite `Contextual` quantifiers. `Product:86–95` supplies Environment/observe; `Setup:100–110` constructs native tests with definitional readout correspondence. A general native `Contextual`-product theorem or principal-context theorem is not supplied by the inspected declarations; actual closure for the stronger state-map relation is N12.
5. **Class.** C for native contextual closure/context-density. D for replacing it by bare trace inclusion. Generic U closure itself is already A, but these native contexts are not automatically its entire test/ancilla class.
6. **Minimal extras.** Comparable public signatures, compatible closing contexts, native composition/reassociation and schedule transport. A principal-context theorem additionally needs a concrete expressive context encoding arbitrary environmental randomization/interleaving; “choose one universal tester” is not an assumption justified by UCSetup.
7. **Positive/control.** T Theorem 1 gives native product compositionality; S's principal context has one state, fresh deterministic left/right loops, and a fair probabilistic pleft/pright action (or a simple-automata replacement). These are different context classes; the principal context cannot be imported into TaskPIOA without preserving its scheduler restriction.
8. **Counterexamples.** S Example 7.4.1: raw-equal systems become distinct in a context that correlates d with f and e with g; one system must decide f/g before the random d/e result. T's **oblivious** scheduler still does not make bare traces sufficient: Appendix D Example 6 has `tdists(T1)⊆tdists(T2)`, but a reporting environment distinguishes the ordering of visible b/c relative to report tasks. T1's schedule A,R,C,R,B,R yields two reporting traces that no schedule of T2∥E can match. Thus these are two precise native obstructions, not just absent proofs.
9. **Security/error.** Matching ρ′ is chosen after the environment and source word, but before all rewards used to test that matched distribution. This differs even from per-reward matching `∀EρP∃ρ′`. It is weaker in quantifier structure than a single uniform simulator before all contexts. Exact product compositionality does not provide computational resource bounds or separate availability.
10. **Obligations.** Establish finite native reassociation/context closure, then characterize the relationship between single-automaton environments and arbitrary wiring contexts/ancillas. Any quantitative variant must state whether one matching schedule works for all tests and retain that quantifier during composition. Native context-density and simulator fullness are separate proof tasks.

### N12 — Simulation, distribution lifting/expansion and execution correspondence (C; completeness shortcut D)

1. **Consumers/status.** Actual TaskPIOA deterministic state-map/lumping simulation and SSP state-map simulation. Full T distribution-of-executions simulation is a more general native proof principle, not the same record.
2. **Primary contract.** T §3 Definitions 3–5, Theorem 2/Corollary 1 and Appendix A.2 Lemma 3 (`PDF:81–115,263–278`); S §§8.2,8.5, Theorem 8.6.1, Proposition 8.7.1 (`segala1995.txt:9357–9388,9967–9980,10003–10008,10314–10317,11644–11654`), [T](https://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/document?doi=e39a815951d25be5a974a40b14a661654a478c21&repid=rep1&type=pdf).
3. **Signature/laws.** Lift a relation R via a nonnegative coupling w with both prescribed marginals and support in R. T expansion E(R), for R between distributions, relates mixtures admitting equally weighted decompositions into R-related distributions. T's simulation relates **distributions on finite execution fragments**, requires equal trace pushforwards, relates the initial Diracs, and has a **single** `c:(Tasks1*×Tasks1)→Tasks2*`. For R-related distributions consistent with ρ and full(c)(ρ), stepping task t matches execution of c(ρ,t) up to E(R). Soundness gives closed trace-distribution inclusion; a simulation for each E gives contextual inclusion. S's forward simulation instead has type `State1→relation→Dist(State2)`, matches by weak combined transitions and lifted distributions, is product-closed and sound for trace precongruence.
4. **Repository.** `TaskPIOA/Simulation.agda:19–24`: `stateMap`, initial-state equality, exactly equal `choose q t`, pushforward equality for every input and local kernel. `:50–59` run/trace match for the **same** fixed task word; `:61–97` product closure using independent kernels. `Security:39–41,43–79` gives contextual matching ρ′=ρ, wiring/test preservation, `uniform⇒≈ᵁ`, `simulation⇒≤UC`. SSP `Protocol:166–185`, `Observation:60–67` has initializer/step-preserving state maps and run equality; ND.SSP `Choice:261–278` lifts those through queued-choice interpretation.
5. **Class.** C for a reusable state/coupling/execution proof interface. D for claiming T Definition 5 is complete for contextual inclusion. The actual exact security bridge is already source-specific, not absent.
6. **Minimal extras.** Finite distributions or appropriately measurable kernels; lawful lifting/flattening and convex closure; initializer/step/label conditions; prefix-consistent, view-respecting schedule translation; test readout factorization and independent-state product laws. For TaskPIOA's present record the translation is identity and the relation is the graph of a deterministic map. Generalization must not silently replace it by state-peeking matching.
7. **Positive/control.** Two actual constructed-model consumers of the smaller pattern are TaskPIOA and SSP. T Appendix E motivates distribution-to-distribution relations by sample-then-permute versus sample-uniformly. More general couplings are not supplied merely by a QUC setup's error type.
8. **Native negative witness.** T Appendix D Examples 5–8 (`PDF:666–679`) states Early and Late implement one another, but no Definition-5 simulation from Late to Early exists. Early must choose branch before a, Late chooses afterward. Prefix consistency of full(c) forces its image of prefix a to commit simultaneously to two incompatible Early choices to match later ab and ac. This refutes completeness of **that** native simulation format; it is not a counterexample to the repository's soundness theorem. The repository record is still more restrictive (same signature, deterministic map, identity task translation).
9. **Security/error.** State/coupling simulation is not an existential UC adversary simulator. In this particular implementation, fixed state maps plus product closure prove every wiring test and ancillary slot preserves agreement, hence U and identity-simulator UC. The paper's `∀E∃R,c` soundness route only gives `∀Eρ∃ρ′`; it does not exchange quantifiers. Approximate coupling needs a bounded-horizon or usage bound before total error claims.
10. **Obligations.** Factor the shared lifting/run proof without erasing differences in return/abort and schedule semantics; implement the distribution-level native relation only with its consistency and Skolem conditions; prove closure and readout soundness; prove ancilla compatibility for any U bridge. Completeness or simulator extraction requires additional theory, not “simulation exists by trace inclusion”.

### N13 — Generalized determinization and may/must/may-must finite-trace semantics (C)

1. **Consumers/status.** B supplies a standard native mixed-choice construction. Current ND grades and TaskPIOA are not an inspected instantiation. Finite probabilistic behavior in SSP/TaskPIOA is a concrete ingredient for a prospective encoding.
2. **Primary contract.** B Proposition 6.2, Definition 6.3, Theorem 6.8; §5 Propositions 5.1–5.3; §7 Definitions 7.1–7.2; §8 Definition 8.1, Theorem 8.4, Proposition 8.13/Remark 8.14 (`bonchi2022.txt:2402–2438,2650–2676,2777–2780,3039 onward,4239–4240,4365–4375,5146–5153,5269–5288,6003–6014`), [v7](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.00923v7).
3. **Signature/laws.** For monad M, Moore functor `F=O×(-)^A`, and M-algebra `a:M O→O`, the canonical law `M F⇒F M` yields `c#:M X→F M X` and semantics in `O^(A*)`. For NPLTS `t:X→(P D X)^A`, convexify and include termination, using pointed convex semilattices C(-+1) or quotients with bottom/top. Free singleton observation algebras are intervals `[l,u]` with convex interval hull/Minkowski operations; `[0,1]` with max and convex combination; `[0,1]` with min and convex combination. The latter's distinguished 0 is algebraic top for the induced semilattice order. Native trace outputs are respectively lower/upper attainable probability interval, supremum, infimum.
4. **Repository.** N02's grade records have no transition system `X→(PDX)^A`, C monad or O algebra. TaskPIOA tests are expected arbitrary rational rewards of **closed trace distributions at fixed schedules**, `Setup:56–69`, not one of these extremal finite-word observations. No such determinizer or identification appears in inspected ND/Task modules.
5. **Class.** C. Neither B's functor law nor algebraic distributivity contradicts N06's monad-over-monad no-go.
6. **Minimal extras.** Specify the transition functor, effect monad, output algebra, termination embedding and determinized state space; prove native encoding/scheduler correspondence and homomorphic observations. For a rational specialization prove finite rational closure and the chosen extremal semantics; do not silently use all real combinations in a rational implementation.
7. **Positive/control.** B Theorems 7.8–7.9 recover reactive probabilistic/LTS semantics. SSP/TaskPIOA are potential finite-distribution consumers **after** representation, not existing full instances. The may/max algebra and must/min algebra are distinct even on the same transition graph.
8. **Obstruction/control.** B's randomized resolution has `r:Y→(D Y+1)^A`, corr:Y→X, exact enabledness per label, and `Dcorr(r(y)(a))∈conv(t(corr y)(a))`. It resolves internal nondeterminism but still permits several labels. A fully probabilistic resolution also chooses a label and may stop while a transition is enabled. B Remark 8.14 shows unrestricted infima over the latter are zero for every nonempty word, since immediate stop is allowed. Thus “demonic trace semantics” is under-specified unless stopping/resolution policy is fixed.
9. **Security/error.** The theorem computes extrema **for each finite word**, allowing the optimizing resolution to vary with the word. It is not equality/inclusion of entire trace-distribution sets under a single matching schedule, and not adaptive-environment UC. B's invariance under monad maps/algebra morphisms preserves this chosen language semantics, not automatically the repository observation presheaf.
10. **Obligations.** Give the finite native encoding and prove which scheduler class it captures; construct the O algebra and determinizer; prove trace-test correspondence. Only then ask whether a context lemma upgrades finite words to all repository tests, and separately whether one uniform simulator is representable. None of these latter claims is settled by the representation theorem for C alone.

### N14 — Bisimulation up to algebraic context (C)

1. **Consumers/status.** Standard proof principle on B's determinized mixed-effect systems; not a proof rule for arbitrary ND grade diagrams or arbitrary UC emulation.
2. **Primary contract.** B Theorem 6.4, Theorems 7.7/7.10/7.11, Definitions 7.12/7.14 (`bonchi2022.txt:2794–2807,4528–4546,4799–4821,5010–5032`), [v7](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.00923v7).
3. **Signature/laws.** On determinized states Ti X, bisimulation R requires equal observations and R-related successors for each label. Up-to context replaces successor R by its closure under the distinguished termination element, semilattice operation and every barycentric operation. The algebraic context closure is compatible, hence sound and composable with other compatible up-to techniques. Probabilistic and convex probabilistic branching bisimilarity imply the three trace equivalences; converse is not claimed.
4. **Repository.** TaskPIOA `Simulation` is a state-map proof and SSP `_≈ᵖ_` is the equivalence closure of state maps; neither is this determinized up-to relation. No compatible-context closure for these mixed trace semantics is defined by the inspected ND/Task files.
5. **Class.** C; A only for reusing already-present category equality/UC congruence once a sound native theorem reaches them.
6. **Minimal extras.** N13's determinization and algebraic presentation, relation progression, and proof that the chosen context operation is compatible with progression. An arbitrary closure on relations is not sound merely because it is called “contextual”.
7. **Positive/control.** B's may/must/may-must semantics are positive consumers of one proof principle. A proposed two-model implementation could encode finite SSP and TaskPIOA transition fragments, but that encoding and its scheduling laws remain obligations; no actual such consumer is claimed.
8. **Obstruction/control.** [INFERENCE] If one allows indiscriminate closure relating all successor states, equal immediate outputs no longer imply equal later outputs; compatibility, not closure alone, is the substantive soundness condition. N08's hidden-coin control also prevents importing state-aware convex transition proofs into an oblivious-schedule observation without a bridge.
9. **Security/error.** Result is native trace equivalence, not UC order. Up-to ⊔/+p does not generate a test-independent simulator and does not prove equality under interactive ancillas. No approximate up-to theorem is supplied by these exact results.
10. **Obligations.** Prove representation and compatible progression on each encoded source, then native run soundness and all-admitted-test/ancilla coverage. Quantitative up-to reasoning requires nonexpansive closure and explicit error accumulation in addition to exact compatibility.

### N15 — Native contextual inclusion versus uniform wiring security (E for the unrestricted converse; positive source bridge explicit)

1. **Consumers/status.** Actual TaskPIOA; ND reflection provides an independent warning about simulator classes, not a counterexample to this particular missing TaskPIOA converse.
2. **Primary contract.** T Definition 2, Corollary 1, §5 Theorems 3–4 (`PDF:71,115,133–145`), [report](https://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/document?doi=e39a815951d25be5a974a40b14a661654a478c21&repid=rep1&type=pdf); inspected repository `Security.agda:33–88` and `Abstract2:131–172`.
3. **Signature/laws.** Native `Contextual c M N=∀Eρ∃ρ′.observe M E ρ≈Mℚ observe N E ρ′`, including comparability with differing private tasks. Repository `Uniform f g=∀x.Related(⟦f⟧x,⟦g⟧x)` uses a fixed same-signature simulation relation, then implies U agreement for every ancillary resource tuple. UC's dummy form is one grade arrow s before all tests. T's local-system theorem uses a fixed map from low-level local schedulers to high-level local schedulers as a sufficient condition, not a consequence of arbitrary pointwise implementation.
4. **Repository.** `uniform⇒contextual:39–41`, `wiring-preserves:53–66`, `test-preserves:68–69`, `Uniform/uniform⇒≈ᵁ:71–76`, `simulation⇒≤UC:78–79`; `resource-≤UC⇒contextual:81–88` extracts the dummy simulator in the **unit leakage, same-signature resource slice** and matches the **same** precommitted ρ. `native-correspondence` is definitional readout equality for the displayed test, not full abstraction.
5. **Class.** E for a general native-contextual-to-wiring-UC correspondence/extraction theorem. The actual simulation⇒UC and unit-resource UC⇒contextual statements are positive source-specific C bridges. D applies only to unjustified logical quantifier exchange or to the narrower incomplete native simulation format in N12, not an unproved all-TaskPIOA insecurity claim.
6. **Minimal extras.** Constructive scheduler matching with one causal/view-respecting strategy; representation of that strategy by the permitted grade/wiring arrows; all-context-to-wiring test backtranslation and ancillary coverage; handling of differing private task/signature types. These are representation/fullness/closure tasks, not the tautological assumption “contextual inclusion implies UC”.
7. **Positive/control.** Same-task deterministic Simulation is a real sufficient witness working uniformly through every wiring generator. N03 gives an actual two-grade-model example where observational power is the same yet simulator representability fails, showing why a separate fullness obligation is necessary.
8. **Negative versus open.** [INFERENCE] Logical `∀E∃s.R(E,s)` need not imply `∃s∀E.R(E,s)` (take R(E,s) iff E=s for two E). This diagnoses why a proof cannot just reorder quantifiers; it is **not** a realized TaskPIOA counterexample. T's Early/Late pair refutes completeness of its prefix-consistent Def5 simulations, not an independently characterized wiring-UC converse. The exact stronger converse remains unestablished in the inspected code/corpus.
9. **Security/error.** Native ∃ρ′ follows E and ρ but precedes all rewards for distribution equality; UC ∃s follows attack but precedes ancillary grades, tests and their supplied resource values. A uniform-simulation witness is stronger still in operational shape. For quantitative variants, one ε and one s before all tests differ from ε(E,ρ), schedule-by-schedule matching, or local negligible bounds. Finite TaskPIOA currently has no security-parameter/PPT/negligible-family structure.
10. **Obligations.** First characterize the exact finite wiring test class and same-resource slice; then either build a representable uniform scheduler/simulator or exhibit a concrete counterexample. Keep native observation adequacy, ancilla density, schedule causality, grade fullness and computational resource restrictions as distinct obligations. Current source bridges must be preserved rather than advertised as a full equivalence.

### N16 — Quantitative graded semantics, metric trace laws and QUC error arithmetic (C/D; existing arithmetic A)

1. **Consumers/status.** F supplies native metric/depth semantics. PM already has `QSetupᵒ`; SSP has a quantitative readout precursor. No named ND/TaskPIOA QUC node or F-style graded transition interpretation is currently established by the inspected code.
2. **Primary contract.** F Definitions 3.1/4.1/6.1, Theorem 5.5, Lemmas 5.9–5.10, Theorems 6.6/7.6 and the negative Theorem 3.2 (`forster2023.txt:357–437,502–512,685–736,771–793,842–867,883–925`), [v4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.01487v4).
3. **Signature/laws.** Met has bounded pseudometrics/nonexpansive maps. A depth-graded monad has `M_n`, `η:Id→M_0`, `μ^{n,k}:M_nM_k→M_{n+k}` with unit/associativity; nonexpansive `α:G→M_1` gives depth behavior maps and `dα(x,y)=sup_n d_{M_n1}(γ^(n)x,γ^(n)y)`. Probabilistic metric trace distance uses Kantorovich distance on length-n trace distributions under **Manhattan** label distance. Depth-1 quantitative presentations give depth-1 monads. A graded logic needs an M0 algebra of truth values, homomorphic propositional operators, and modalities forming M1 algebras; uniform-depth formula evaluation is nonexpansive. Expressivity additionally requires a specified upward-closed initiality invariant Φ and depth-0/depth-1 separation on canonical algebras M_n1.
4. **Repository.** Existing A arithmetic: `UC/Quantitative/Witness.agda:41–61,66–80` gives `At`, fixed-simulator `Witness`, attack-universal `Witness⁺`, dummy equivalence, transitivity error ε⊕δ and serial error εu⊕εf. `UC/Model/Quantitative:19–25` is the actual PM instance. SSP `Observation:69–89` measures both Boolean verdict masses including absence of a verdict. TaskPIOA `Setup:56–69` instead admits **arbitrary ℚ-valued rewards** with exact expectation equality; no bounded-error normalization appears there.
5. **Class.** C for metric transition/trace semantics and a quantitative bridge; D for the specified universal unary-modal characterization and for assuming every metric tensor yields a nonexpansive law. A for already-existing QUC witness/error composition, not a new construction.
6. **Minimal extras.** Metric state/label objects, chosen tensor, nonexpansive kernels/graded operations, bounded or Lipschitz-controlled rewards, scheduler-preserving test pullback, quantitative observation presheaf, and representable fixed simulators. Depth n, interface choice-site grade, resource/query allowance and numerical error are separate indices. [INFERENCE] With unrestricted rational rewards, any nonzero expectation discrepancy can be scaled past every fixed finite ε, so a useful TaskPIOA QUC extension must bound rewards or account for their norm rather than reuse the exact test set with a uniform absolute-error bound.
7. **Positive/control.** F gives Hausdorff trace monads for metric LTS and Kantorovich probabilistic trace semantics. Actual finite-rational source ingredients occur in TaskPIOA and SSP; PM supplies a real quantitative UC consumer. These are promising two-model consumers of a bounded finite-kernel estimate, not already a proved F-to-UC instantiation.
8. **Exact negatives.** F Example 5.8 (`:721–730`): d(x,y)=1, μ=½x+½y, ν=δx, d(a,b)=½. In the supremum product `A×D X`, input distance is ½, while their mapped distributions in `D(A×X)` have Kantorovich distance ¾; the candidate law is not nonexpansive. Manhattan tensor restores the required nonexpansiveness (Lemma 5.9). F Theorem 3.2: for generative `Gprob=Dω(A⊞−)` over a **non-discrete** metric label space, no compositionally interpreted coalgebraic logic with **unary next-step modalities** has logical distance equal to probabilistic metric trace distance. It does not refute all test languages, discrete-label claims, or arbitrary QUC constructions.
9. **Security/error.** A finite trace metric is not UC security until all admitted interactive contexts and ancillas are controlled with one simulator. Repeated use can amplify local ε; [INFERENCE] n independent Bernoulli(ε) opportunities versus none give success gap `1−(1−ε)^n≤nε`, so no length-independent ε guarantee follows. Use a usage/query bound, summable errors or contraction. Generic error ⊕ need not commute. For security-parameter families distinguish one negligible bound before all contexts from context-dependent negligible witnesses; no such asymptotic TaskPIOA extension is supplied by finite rational automata. Availability must be stated separately from indistinguishability.
10. **Obligations.** Construct a bounded-reward/native quantitative evaluator, prove nonexpansive schedule/product/hiding maps and a usage-aware finite-run bound, then instantiate the already-A QUC factory/witness composition. A characteristic-test theorem needs F's separation hypotheses or another proved test-density argument; the no-go blocks the stated unary-modal shortcut. Establish simulator uniformity and computational admissibility independently of metric soundness.

## Conclusions and research obligations, without overclaiming

- **Already A and not new:** generic Standard2 setup/UC core; ND grade/relabel factories and their two concrete instances; existing distribution bind/map infrastructure; fixed-simulator quantitative arithmetic once a quantitative instance exists.
- **Best two-consumer C target:** a small initialized-state/distribution-lifting/run-to-observation interface, consumed by the existing TaskPIOA and SSP state-map results, then cautiously generalized to couplings. Keep TaskPIOA's fixed task word and SSP's adaptive call/queued-pick activation as parameters, not hidden assumptions. The parent package/structural research owns the broader SSP distributional and stored-state interfaces.
- **Independent C target:** native task product/renaming/hiding plus schedule transport and the exact finite contextual correspondence. The free wiring setup already exists; the missing work is native representation/closure, not another category syntax.
- **Native mixed-choice alternative:** either convex sets with barycentric/semilattice laws or indexed histories with a chosen scheduler adequacy. Neither is obtained from A/B grade names, and neither should be silently substituted for the actual PM/SSP choice interpreters.
- **Proved D boundaries:** SSP B→A reflection at the exhibited grades; ordinary P/D Beck distributive laws under the stated premises; loss of associativity for V's naive set-of-valuations bind; scheduler peeking enlargement (explicit elementary control); bare trace inclusion as contextual implementation (both S and T); completeness of T's Definition-5 simulation; F's specified metric/nonexpansiveness and unary-modal claims.
- **Genuinely E here:** unrestricted finite native Contextual→wiring-UC correspondence, with explicit simulator/schedule representability and ancillary test coverage. No false impossibility is inferred just because its proof is absent.

No source/check results were fabricated or exercised. This report records inspected declarations and primary-text contracts, and marks new deductions `[INFERENCE]`; it does not claim these proposed generalizations have been implemented or typechecked.

## Scope and reading convention

Read-only research by `ConstructionGeneralityAstra.SymbolicRelLiterature`. Parent-confirmed source proof boundary: `9f9be2a89`; current docs-only commit: `b24ae5f92`. These are supplied provenance, not a new Git audit. No repository edits, builds, typechecks, tests, linters or formatters were performed.

Path aliases: short symbolic names `Term.agda`, `Role.agda`, `Fine.agda`, `Setup.agda`, `Semantics.agda`, `Deduction.agda`, `Static.agda`, `Computational.agda`, `Soundness.agda`, `Machine.agda`, and their `Static/`, `Computational/`, `Soundness/`, `RunFollowing/` subpaths are relative to **CC/Symbolic/**. `RelSetup.agda` abbreviates **CC/Examples/RelSetup.agda**. Generic `UCSetup`, `Abstract2`, `Standard2`, `Machines/`, `UC/` and `IPDL/` paths are relative to CC/. Installed dependency roots AC/ and SL/ are stated explicitly in R01/R03.

A = already an existing generic API, with its actual instance; B = follows from arbitrary setup axioms but not already packaged; C = valid only for an explicitly structured class; D = a proposed unrestricted extension fails; E = the stated correspondence/proof is unresolved. Mixed labels always distinguish the valid restricted part from the false or unresolved extension. **No new B construction is established by this bounded corpus.** A rows are coverage, not proposed new constructions.

“Standard” means a named definition/theorem in the inspected primary sources below. This is not a claim to exhaust symbolic verification, relation algebra, or all protocol constructions. New deductions and proposed abstractions are marked **[INFERENCE]**. Source statements, existing code declarations and new deductions are kept separate.

### Levels of equality and security

The generic core has `C : Category`, a **monoidal grade category** `I`, a graded monad and a Setoid-valued test presheaf; it does not give a monoidal structure on C, symmetry, state, trace, names, probability or copying. `Standard2.StdUC` is an additional monoidal-carrier factory. The symbolic free carrier in the inspected code is `v = Mon`, **not a free symmetric monoidal category** (`Fine.agda:36–42`; `Computational.agda:109–116`).

Write `≈hom` for carrier equality, `≈ℰ` for the bare observation kernel, and `≈ᵁ` for its ancillary-grade closure. They are not definitions of one another. The local generic security order is

`f ≤UC g  ≡  ∀ attacked-grade map a:X→X′. ∃ s:Y→X′. sub(a)∘f ≈ᵁ sub(s)∘g`.

`≈ᵁ` already quantifies every admitted ancillary grade and test. The simulator cannot depend on that test. Dummy completeness equivalently supplies one `s₀:Y→X` before those universal tests; attack `a` uses `a∘s₀`. Source bisimulations, symbolic recipes and apex-machine projections are not such adversarial simulators just because they are called “simulations.” Generic references and exact hypotheses are in [the owner-reviewed API catalogue](#appendix-b), especially G1–G5/G10; structural nonimplications are in [the structural evidence](#appendix-d).
