### Corpus mapped to each model/proof layer

| Consumer | Core examined corpus | Actual boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Fine symbolic setup | S1, S3, S4, S5 | Free **monoidal** finite-circuit syntax of roles, syntactic test equality. Native applied-pi binding, mobile channel substitution and replication do not follow from that syntax. |
| Coarse symbolic setup | S1–S5 | Same carrier, verdict-context test equality, canonical term theory. Static and run-following results are evidence for this observation relation, not an identification with all native applied-pi contexts. |
| Certified symbolic setup/interpreter | S1, S6, S7; S4/S5 for simulator quantifier comparison | Query-admissible roles and a formal fine-source interpretation. S6/S7 motivate additional cryptographic conditions; they are not automatically instantiated by `Scheme` or `Certified`. |
| Deduction/static/emitter layer | S1 §§4, S2 §§2–4, S3 §§II–III | Recipes, static equality, context lemmas; generic multi-sorted converse is not established. |
| Relational/run-following proof layer | S1 Def4.4/Thm4.1; S7 §5 adaptive trace obstruction; R1/R2 for distinguishing algebraic relations/feedback | Existing state/apex proofs and world-indexed marginal laws. No claim that their API is literally the applied-pi labeled bisimulation relation, or a new setup. |
| One-shot Rel setup | R1–R3; S6 as a contrast with computational encryption | Possibilistic binary relations with all subset tests. Product tensor is not a categorical product in Rel. No probability-sensitive security or error metric is reconstructed from support. |

Meaningful exclusions: no claim of a general ProVerif-style decision algorithm, active computational soundness for arbitrary equational theories/primitives, probabilistic soundness from the 1983 perfect-cryptography model, or source-to-code full abstraction for unrestricted applied pi. Original Kozen/JSV PDF endpoints attempted were unavailable; R2/R3 are directly inspected primary sources for the precise trace/Kleene claims made here, not invented theorem numbers from inaccessible sources. Quantitative-family and shared-state theory beyond these instantiations is owned by the other reports.

## Native operator inventory versus the implemented source

| Native construction | Implemented local part | Boundary that remains |
|---|---|---|
| Term/name substitution | `Term.Substitution`, `substᵗ`, `ren`; recipe binding/evaluation | These are not capture-avoiding substitution through native process binders/active substitutions. |
| Restriction and fresh allocation | `React.new`; `(occurrence-supply,counter)` names; injective occurrence naming | No native `νn.P`/`νx.A` constructor or α-quotient of processes in the inspected role syntax. Byte nonces are sampled, not guaranteed nominally distinct. |
| Scope extrusion | Freshness/foreign-name invariants support contextual proofs | No direct native New-Par theorem; its capture/scope side condition must survive any representation theorem. |
| Parallel composition | Finite circuit tensor; Role interpretations route a **sum** of port letters | Native shared-channel `P|Q`, its commutative structural congruence, and product-value copying are not the local free-Mon tensor by definition. |
| Replication | Repeated activations/control loops of a fixed role | No `!P`, unbounded generator spawning or session-indexing construction in those modules. |
| Conditionals | `React.test` on same-sort recipes, decidable E-equality; exact guard agreement in static proofs | Native arbitrary E need not be decidable; computational byte guards need a separate correctness/soundness argument. |
| Input/output and mobile channels | Fixed typed ports, per-input reactions and emitted payload/next control | Terms can be transmitted, but the inspected syntax does not turn arbitrary received channel names into dynamically allocated port interfaces. |
| Frames/static observations | Finite sorted frames, owned/foreign recipe names, deduction/static equality, emitter/context proofs | No automatic equivalence between this representation and all native restricted substitutions/extended processes or labeled transitions. |

## Full construction rows

### A01 — Standard wiring, dummy completeness and serial UC composition

1. **Consumers/status.** All four constructed setups above. Existing generic coverage, not a new symbolic or relational construction.
2. **Primary contract.** Native composition/security comparisons: S4 Def4.3/Lem4.4/Thm5.37, S5 Def9/Thm16; categorical relational composition: R3 §4. The exact repository UC contract is the inspected `UCSetup.agda:21–26,55–57` and `Abstract2.agda:45–78,131–172,174–221` (code is primary evidence for this formalisation, not attributed to those papers).
3. **Native signature/laws.** A monoidal carrier M and lawful test presheaf E give `StdUC M E`; a protocol is `A→X⊗B`. Composition combines grades tensorially. The abstract core derives dummy completeness and serial composition from the graded-monad/presheaf laws. Applied-pi parallel/context composition is a different native operation whose interpretation needs proof.
4. **Actual API.** `Standard2.agda:19–34`; `Fine.agda:36–42,71–80`; `Setup.agda:102–105`; `Computational.agda:109–143`; `Examples/RelSetup.agda:31–38,124–147`. Fine even includes a direct `UC-compose` instantiation at lines76–80; Rel composes its concrete encryption/relay witnesses at143–147.
5. **Class.** **A** for these categorical instances. Not A for all native applied-pi parallel/replication laws.
6. **Exact instantiation.** M is `𝔻𝕐` with `ℰ≅` or `ℰˢʸᵐ`, `𝔻𝕐ᶜ` with `ℰ≅ᶜ`, or `Rels` with product monoidal structure and `Hom[-,⊤]`. No symmetry requirement is inserted into `StdUC`.
7. **Positives/controls.** Symbolic and Rel are two constructed-theory consumers. Control: a symbolic `Role` contains an internal control loop, but this does not give a generic replication operation on an arbitrary protocol; source `P|Q` with shared free names is not justified merely by categorical tensor.
8. **Obstruction scope.** Existing categorical laws are proved declarations. A missing native-process representation theorem is a correspondence gap, not a counterexample to generic `UC-compose`. Bare setup axioms do not even provide a base tensor; see structural evidence.
9. **Security/errors.** Exactly `∀a ∃s ∀ancilla,test`; equivalently one dummy simulator before tests. Serial composition retains this order and has no numerical error. It must not be paraphrased as `∀test ∃simulator`.
10. **Obligations.** For a native-language composition claim: interpret objects/processes, show identity/composition/tensor and observation correspondence, then use this API. Do not reimplement dummy completeness or claim a second universal composition theorem merely by renaming it.

### A02 — Observation coarsening and conditional setup transport

1. **Consumers/status.** Fine→coarse symbolic is implemented; the same generic family applies to reobservation of Rel. Certified→computational and fine→machine-iso are formal morphisms with separate coverage obligations.
2. **Primary contract.** S1 Defs4.1–4.4 and Lem4.3 distinguish static/observational information; the exact local theorem is `Abstract2/Morphism.agda:51–108,114–143`, `UCSetup/Morphism.agda:31–39,48–68`. S1 does not prove this categorical setup-morphism theorem.
3. **Signature/laws.** Same computational fields plus a natural map of test presheaves gives `changeKernel`. A bare-kernel implication on the same carrier gives `Refine.≈ᵁ-refine`/`≤UC-refine`. General transport needs U-preservation; one sufficient route is test-map epi plus **target** GradeStable. Reflection requires grade fullness, κ monicity, test-map mono and **source** GradeStable.
4. **Actual API.** `Setup.agda:58–65` compares tests by all closures/verdicts; `grade-stable` and `≈ᵁ⇔≈ℰ` at123–130; `coarse`, `coarse-epi`, `fine→sym` at154–163. `Symbolic/Machine.agda:169–196` maps fine syntax to machine-iso; `Computational.agda:209–215` supplies another formal morphism. No epi/fullness statement for the latter is supplied there.
5. **Class.** **A** for implemented fine→coarse and existing generic transport. **D** for unconditional reflection from a coarser observer; **E** for any additional computational coverage/full-abstraction claim not proved by the mapped modules.
6. **Exact instance/minimal additions.** Fine/coarse has identity carrier, `coarse` and `coarse-epi`, and the proved target `grade-stable`. Thus it meets the generic preservation route. Other morphisms need their own target-test/ancilla coverage or direct U-preservation and simulator fullness for reflection; a formal arrow alone is insufficient.
7. **Positives/controls.** Fine→coarse is positive. **[INFERENCE] Rel observation-coarsening control:** replace `Hom[-,⊤]` by a constant one-point test presheaf. All parallel protocols become U-equal; the same-grade identity simulator therefore makes even a leaking and a nonleaking protocol UC-related. Full Rel tests distinguish them (`leaky-not-secure`, `RelSetup.agda:164–172`). This is a second carrier instantiation of reobservation, not an already declared new setup node.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The control proves observation loss can destroy reflection, not that the actual fine→coarse map lacks every conceivable restricted reflection theorem. S1's static-vs-dynamic counterexample also does not itself establish a local fine/coarse separation. Local `emit-≈E` proves coarse equality of E-related reactions, not automatically their fine inequivalence.
9. **Security/errors.** Same-carrier coarsening preserves the **same** dummy simulator; does not produce a stronger adversary or reflect security. No error appears. Computational test translation using only translated/dead-closed contexts is not coverage of all computational contexts.
10. **Obligations.** Separate naturality, bare-kernel preservation, ancilla coverage, simulator/grade fullness and observation faithfulness. Use existing `Refine`/`Transfer`; provide an actual counterexample or representation theorem before asserting strictness/equivalence of particular symbolic kernels.

### N01 — Substitution, restriction, fresh names, scope extrusion, parallel and conditionals

1. **Consumers/status.** Native applied pi; local fine/coarse roles and their proof layer; certified byte execution has a different randomness contract. Nominal abstraction across symbolic and IPDL is a candidate, not implemented nominal process syntax.
2. **Primary contract.** S1 §§2.1–2.3, structural rules New-Par/Alias/Subst/Rewrite and communication/Then/Else. S2 §2.1–2.3 for term substitution and restricted frames. S3 §III name-stamping is a separate, older string operation, not nominal binding.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Plain processes `0`, `P|Q`, `!P`, `νn.P`, `if M=N then P else Q`, input and output; extended processes additionally active substitutions and variable restriction. Binding is modulo α-renaming; active substitutions obey domain/cycle conditions. `A | νu.B ≡ νu.(A|B)` requires `u ∉ fv(A)∪fn(A)`. Conditionals test equality modulo E, and the Else rule has its ground-disequality condition. Parallel is commutative/associative at the source structural-equivalence level, not a property to silently impose on local Mon syntax.
4. **Actual API.** `Term.agda:36–81` has sorted terms, `Name=ℕ×ℕ`, `Substitution`, `substᵗ`, `ren`; `:198–242` recipes, evaluation and recipe substitution; `:266–280` decidable/canonical EqTheory. `Role.agda:184–211` has `test`, `new`, `emit` and finite control/frame declarations; `:285–338` run configurations/counters and `(κ,j)` generation. `Semantics.agda:114–119` requires an **injective occurrence naming** for a closed verdict observation. There is no native applied-pi process binder/active-substitution/scope-extrusion constructor in these inspected role/free-category modules.
5. **Class.** Existing term/recipe and role operations are source-specific. General nominal reuse is **C**. **D** for equating name-supply freshness with port-tag disjointness or for unrestricted/capturing scope laws. Full applied-pi representation is **E** here, not asserted impossible.
6. **Minimal added structure.** **[INFERENCE]** For a reusable nominal layer: atoms/permutations with finite support or an equivalent binding representation; equivariant term/process interpretation; capture-avoiding substitution with identity/composition/fresh-renaming laws; fresh allocation extending an existing support; restriction and its freshness-scoped extrusion law; tests respecting α-equivalence and hiding. If parallel shares channels, explicit ownership/connection semantics is also needed. Port disjointness alone proves none of these.
7. **Positives/controls.** Local `new` uses disjoint occurrence supplies and monotone per-role counters, while term substitution is already present. A possible second consumer is native IPDL `new`/α-renaming, but current typed `Ren`/finite hidden-wire positions are not nominal atoms (see opportunities below). **[INFERENCE] capture control:** naive `[b/x](νb. out(c,x))` binds the substituted b; capture-avoiding substitution first renames the binder to d, giving `νd.out(c,b)`. A context already knowing free b distinguishes the two by equality of the received value with b. Scope extrusion over an A that uses free u similarly changes its free communication scope.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The capture counterexample refutes the law without its side condition. No counterexample is claimed to the actual `(κ,j)` implementation, which enforces supply separation. Finite circuit syntax and finite control do **not** mean finite runs or finite state: counters and term values can grow, and control can loop.
9. **Security/errors.** Native freshness is qualitative and quantifies avoidance of the whole relevant support/context. Local `eval` existentially chooses an injective naming and proves a deterministic verdict observation; this existential is not a probabilistic name sample or a UC simulator. Byte-level independent nonce draws can collide; their bad-event probabilities need a separate soundness theorem.
10. **Obligations.** A representation theorem must relate source binders/α-classes to supplies, preserve substitution and restriction, prove scoped extrusion and context closure, and cover all admitted fresh-name contexts. Then establish the observation bridge and relevant simulator quantifiers. Do not substitute a renaming of finite ports for this proof.

### N02 — Dynamic replication, session indexing and joint-state wrappers

1. **Consumers/status.** Native symbolic UC/SS, with no `!` constructor or dynamic role-spawn operator in the mapped local symbolic source. Local circuit tensor/composition and a Role's control loop already exist but are not replication.
2. **Primary contract.** S1 Repl `!P ≡ P|!P`; S4 Defs4.1–4.3, §5 replication counterexample, Defs5.1–5.3 and Thm5.37; S5 Lem5, Props13–14, Def15/Thm16 and §4.1 tagged joint-state encryption.
3. **Native signature/laws.** S4's process security is `P≤Q` iff **one** simulator/adaptor `(S,ϕ,n)` yields observational equivalence `P ~ νn(Qϕ|S)`, with disjoint IO/NET disciplines and NET stability. Parallel composition permits only IO sharing with the extra component. Indexed replication uses fresh/capture-avoiding SID substitution/tagging, distinct ground SID terms modulo E and a suitable indexing context; Q must be NET-stable. S5 instead uses a one-way observational preorder and proves parallel/replication closure for its SS relation. Its simulator may add nondeterministic behavior; this is not the same order as S4's equivalence-based one.
4. **Actual API.** `Role.agda:200–211` permits new names and jumps via emitted next control, not creation of new source generators. `Fine.agda:36–42` is finite free Mon syntax; `RunFollowing/Supply.agda:19–59` extends a finite occurrence naming. No native session/indexing/replication theorem is exported by these mapped modules. Generic `UC-compose` covers serial categorical composition only as stated in A01.
5. **Class.** Tagged/session-disciplined replication and joint-state wrappers are **C**; unconditional replication or “shared state is free copying” is **D**. Translation of either paper's native security into local `≤UC` is **E**.
6. **Minimal added structure.** **[INFERENCE]** A session constructor with fresh SID generation, capture avoidance, disjointness/equivariance and dispatch semantics; separate per-session state from deliberately shared state; a wrapper exposing exactly the allowed shared resource interface; a single simulator compatible with all sessions and the scheduler/context discipline. Dynamic unbounded spawning also needs a semantic carrier/representation not supplied by finite syntax alone.
7. **Positives/controls.** S4's indexed `!!x` construction and S5's tagged joint-state encryption are positive native examples. S4 gives `P≤Q` but `!P≰!Q`: a per-session secret links outputs on two NET channels, while untagged sessions prevent a simulator from determining whether requests belong to the same session. S5's concrete shared-key and independent-key systems can expose one key versus many; their tagged ideal-functionality theorem is not equality of those raw resources.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The S4 counterexample is a genuine failure of unindexed replication for that security definition, not a contradiction of S5's preorder theorem. The two use different observations/simulation relations. A finite register loop can service repeated requests, but it does not allocate independent protocol instances or prove a replication law.
9. **Security/errors.** S4 Lem4.4 aligns its `∃simulator` formulation with strong/black-box/universal alternatives under its process hypotheses. S5 Def15 has black-box `∃S∀A`, UC `∀A∃S`, dummy `∃S`; Thm16 equates them **for that preorder framework**. Local dummy completeness has its own typed grade maps. None of these symbolic results supplies computational error, resource growth or a common PPT simulator for unlimited sessions.
10. **Obligations.** Fix which native security relation is represented; implement session interfaces and prove capture/ownership/dispatch invariants; prove wrapper semantics and one simulator's closure under all permitted sessions. For computational use additionally derive query/session cost and accumulated error, rather than counting replicas as free.

### K01 — Deduction, static tests and restricted decision procedures

1. **Consumers/status.** Local sorted knowledge/recipe layer supports coarse symbolic proofs. Dolev–Yao and Abadi–Cortier procedures have restricted native domains; no universal local deduction/static-equivalence decision procedure is established.
2. **Primary contract.** S2 Prop1 p.5: `νñσ ⊢ M` iff some recipe ζ avoids ñ and `ζσ =E M`; §2.4 static equivalence; Props3–5 and Def1/Thm1. S3 §I perfect-cryptography assumptions; cascade Thm1 and name-stamp Def7/Thm3.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Frames are finite substitutions with restricted names; deduction closes public operations and equations. Static equivalence compares **all equality tests between recipes**, at the same domain, not merely membership in a set of deducible ground messages. S2's polynomial algorithms require finite **convergent subterm** rewriting (right side a proper subterm or constant, with convergence), with DAG-size conventions. S3 works with its two-party uniform cascade/name-stamp operator languages and perfect public-key cancellation, not arbitrary applied-pi programs.
4. **Actual API.** `Deduction.agda:37–51,71–73` defines Knowledge, recipe-witness deduction, inductive rules and equivalence; cut/weakening/ownership at110–117. `Foreign` and `≈ₛ` at124–129 use names foreign to both frames and all recipe equalities. `Term.agda:266–280` supplies decidable **term equality**, canon soundness/completeness and no-new-names; no saturation/decision algorithm for existential deduction or universally quantified static equivalence follows as an exported declaration.
5. **Class.** Deduction/static proof reuse is **C** over a recipe/equational structure; algorithmic reuse is **C** under the stronger finite convergent-subterm hypothesis. **D** for decidability over arbitrary equational theories. Whether particular broader local EqTheory instances admit such algorithms is **E**.
6. **Minimal added structure.** Sorted public signature, admissible-name predicate, substitution/equation congruence and recipe evaluation suffice for the basic rules. A terminating/confluent finite rewrite presentation plus the subterm property and finite representations is additional algorithmic data; `DecidableEquality` alone is insufficient. To share an algorithm with typed code, prove its treatment of sorts and canonicalization.
7. **Positives/controls.** Pairing/encryption destructor equations are primary positive subterm examples. S2 §2.4 gives frames with `enc(0,k)` versus `enc(1,k)` and the exposed k: both 0 and 1 are already deducible, yet the decryption equality test distinguishes the frames. S3's active saboteur has all intercepted messages and its own decryption operations, not possession of every honest private key.
8. **Obstruction scope.** S2 Prop3 gives decidable E-equality with undecidable deduction; Prop5 states deduction can be decidable while static equivalence is undecidable. These refute unrestricted inference from a decidable theory/deduction relation. No claim is made that those examples satisfy **every** extra field of this repository's canonical/no-new-names EqTheory record without a separate instance proof.
9. **Security/errors.** Deduction is `∃recipe`; static equivalence is `∀recipe pairs`, with both-direction equality. Neither has an adversarial UC simulator or computational error. Secrecy-as-nondeducibility, static indistinguishability, process equivalence and UC emulation remain separate properties.
10. **Obligations.** For a shared decision procedure: represent the finite theory, prove termination/confluence/subterm closure, soundness/completeness of saturation and complexity relative to actual term representation, then connect returned witnesses to local recipes/static tests. For security use, add the contextual observation bridge; do not identify nondeducibility with all-context security.

### K02 — Frame/static equivalence, contexts and labeled bisimulation

1. **Consumers/status.** Existing local forward static-to-observation theorem; converse for the single-sort PairEnc emitter instance. Native applied-pi context/bisimulation principles have not been identified with the entire finite-role source calculus.
2. **Primary contract.** S1 Def4.1 observational equivalence, Defs4.2–4.3 frame equality/static equivalence, Lemmas4.1–4.3, Def4.4 labeled bisimilarity, Thm4.1 equivalence of labeled and observational equivalence. S5 Def3/Prop4 uses one-way labeled similarity; equality with observational preorder is **conjectured**, not proved there.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Observational equivalence is symmetric, same-domain, barb-preserving, weak-reduction closed, and preserved by every closing evaluation context. Labeled bisimulation requires static equivalence plus matching internal/visible weak transitions, with label-domain/freshness conditions. Static equivalence equals observational equivalence **on frames**, but is strictly coarser for arbitrary dynamic processes. No general “up-to context” theorem is extracted from S1's discussion, which does not develop it.
4. **Actual API.** `Static.agda:156–177` constructs emitter evidence and `≈ₛ⇒≈ℰ`. `Static/Distinguish.agda:77–84,146–149` constructs foreign-name testers and the converse. `Static/PairEnc.agda:3–30` explicitly states multi-sorted converse not established and exports `≈ℰ⇔≈ₛ` only for `Σ₀,E₀`/lists of unit sort. `Setup.agda:123–130` separately promotes this coarse bare kernel to U via grade stability.
5. **Class.** Existing emitter theorem is source-specific **C**, not a new generic UC construction. Generic multi-sorted converse and whole applied-pi full abstraction are **E**. **D** for applying “static=observational” from frames to arbitrary processes.
6. **Minimal added structure.** Recipe closure and guard agreement; context closure with fresh-name extension; observations that can express the distinguishing recipe equality test. For completeness, a tester representation for every admissible native equality test, its correct sort/channel access and fresh-name allocation. Arbitrary UCSetup provides none of these.
7. **Positives/controls.** The single-sort PairEnc emitter theorem is a precise positive; the generic forward theorem is another scope of use. S1 Lem4.3's dynamic processes output on different public channels but have the same empty frame: static equivalence cannot distinguish their different barbs. The multi-sorted local completeness gap is not promoted to a counterexample.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The primary dynamic-process control genuinely refutes unrestricted frame reduction. The code comment “multi-sorted converse not established” is an unknown-proof boundary, not evidence it is false. A syntactic or machine-state bisimulation certificate is not necessary for every contextual equality without a completeness theorem.
9. **Security/errors.** `∀κ` frame static equivalence yields coarse emitter `≈ℰ`; coarse grade stability then yields `≈ᵁ`. An equal-grade UC witness may use identity through the generic core, but static equivalence itself supplies no simulator for differently graded resources. All these local results are exact, not negligible statements.
10. **Obligations.** Generalise tester expressiveness across sorts or exhibit a typed counterexample; prove native source-context representation/adequacy before claiming full abstraction; distinguish a sound relational proof method from completeness; use the existing grade-stability bridge rather than suppressing the ancillary quantifier.

### P01 — Relational apex/lockstep proofs through arbitrary admitted contexts

1. **Consumers/status.** Symbolic static frame/role contexts consume already generic machine relational-simulation machinery. This is a proof layer, not `RelSetup` itself or an additional setup.
2. **Primary contract.** S1 Def4.4 supplies the native motivation for related-state/transition proof principles, but the precise apex-machine contract is primary code: `Machines/Sim/Relational.agda:24–27,255–299`; `UC/Model/Relational.agda:35–114`. R1's binary-relation category is a different categorical object and does not by itself prove this machine theorem.
3. **Signature/laws.** A common apex machine with pure projections to two machines, commuting initialization/step squares, gives `RelSim`. Identity boundary projections give machine equality in the quotient semantics; composition, event-tensor and compatible feedback preserve spans. Base hypotheses are a symmetric monoidal V, monoidal distributivity, PureSub and Elgot iteration. In the rational-distribution instance the commuting equations preserve full marginals, not just support.
4. **Actual API.** `Machines/Sim/Relational.agda:255–299` exports `RelSim`, graph-to-span, `relsim⇒≈ᴹ`, composition/tensor/trace. Symbolic `Static/Same.agda:70–119` defines related terms as the **same recipe**, evaluated in the two frames, and their projected alphabets. `Static/Relational.agda:129–154` closes context apexes under Mon structure with `∀o,j Foreign φ ψ (ι o,j)`; `Emits` at161–172 demands related frames, ownership and both legs **for every injective occurrence naming**; `relsim⇒≈ℰ` at250–265 quantifies every admitted test, closure and verdict.
5. **Class.** **A** for reuse of existing generic carrier-level `RelSim` operations with the actual PM/role consumer. **C** for a proposed shared observation-compatible relational interface across other setups. Not an arbitrary-UCSetup construction.
6. **Exact instance/minimal additions.** Symbolic letters are paired recipe evaluations; the apex runs the same role, guard outcomes agree by static equivalence and context fresh draws are foreign. Sealed-model `HSimᵒ` lifts the underlying spans. Another model needs an actual state/step or relation-lifting interface, compatible initialization, composition/feedback and observationally adequate projections, not merely a relation on executions.
7. **Positives/controls.** Existing consumers are generic machine semantics and its symbolic static specialization. One-shot Rel offers a second constructed-theory **opportunity** via extensional relational proofs, not an existing `RelSim` instance asserted here. Control: matching only supports of two randomized steps gives no equality of marginal distributions (biased pad/coin control in R04).
8. **Obstruction scope.** **[INFERENCE]** An arbitrary set relation R on hidden states without initialization/step squares can relate machines that immediately output distinct verdicts; hence the bare relation is not a sound observation proof. Dropping freshness can make one context recipe access a supposedly hidden name and invalidate guard agreement. This does not challenge the checked-in signatures, which retain those hypotheses.
9. **Security/errors.** The current theorem yields exact coarse `≈ℰ`, followed separately by grade-stability/U. There is no adversary-dependent simulator extraction from the apex; its projections are semantic proof maps. Approximate coupling would additionally need a quantitative relation and nonexpansive observation/usage laws; exact spans do not imply an error budget.
10. **Obligations.** Reuse the existing span algebra first. A cross-model abstraction must distinguish one-shot relation composition from initialized state transitions, prove both marginal/step laws and observation compatibility, and then establish ancillary closure or an explicit UC witness. Do not introduce a second “generic bisimulation” record with weaker undocumented laws.

### P02 — Run-following worlds, adaptive state changes and context stability

1. **Consumers/status.** Existing generic rational-machine world-indexed proof layer, consumed by Symbolic.RunFollowing PairEnc/Role/Circuit/Supply. Not a setup node, not computational encryption soundness by itself.
2. **Primary contract.** S7 §5 explains why per-trace static soundness is insufficient when an attacker adaptively selects a trace from random outcomes; its tree argument provides a primary motivation, not a theorem identifying the local API. Exact native code contract: `Machines/Sim/RunFollowing.agda:7–10,65–120,269–456`, `.../RunFollowing/Process.agda:56–90,127–165`.
3. **Signature/laws.** Worlds with initial world and a preorder; related letters and two projections. `Stable` has world-indexed state, point/step marginal laws and a promotion map preserving projections. `Following` can move to a later world during a probabilistic step while preserving both projected step distributions. One Following component composes/tensors with Stable contexts; trace carries worlds through internal iterations. Boundary input sections and identical output projections convert a Following witness to an ordinary relational span.
4. **Actual API.** Core `Stable`/`Following` at92–112; mixed event composition301–347, `trace-following`408–418, `following-relsim`423–456. Process composition/tensor and mixed following closure at127–159. `Symbolic/RunFollowing/PairEnc.agda:46–76,105–133` has normalized key-free plaintext, before/after worlds and legal-message expansion; `Role.agda:105–125,339–349,393–400` requires other occurrence κ′≠κ. `Circuit.agda:191–211,267–295` records stable contexts/correct interpretation and mixes them with the changing hole. `Supply.agda:19–59` picks a new supply above the finite maximum and preserves injectivity.
5. **Class.** **A** for the existing generic **machine-level** world/Stable/Following API and its symbolic instance. **C** for extension to another structured semantics, including a proposed two-Following construction supplied with common-world synchronization/update structure. **E** for unrestricted two-Following closure: no concrete counterexample to that exact well-typed claim is supplied here. **D** remains for the per-trace⇒adaptive-soundness shortcut and for discarding required point/step or legality premises where the cited obstruction applies; it is not inferred from absence of a closure theorem.
6. **Exact instance/minimal additions.** PairEnc world changes once a legal plaintext is accepted; expansion substitutes a protected abstract handle by encryption of that plaintext versus zero. Before-world legality excludes premature handle/key access; role contexts preserve worlds and transport their states when the hole advances. A broader model must supply world promotion, joint step distributions with both marginals, and boundary agreement; arbitrary UCSetup does not expose these.
7. **Positives/controls.** Symbolic PairEnc with role contexts and the separately parameterized machine/Process algebra are actual consumers. S7's adaptive trace-selection discussion is the primary negative control. **[INFERENCE]** A common-world synchronization/update law is a candidate structured route for two advancing components; the current separate witnesses do not themselves provide such a law. This identifies a proposed proof obligation, not a counterexample to unrestricted two-Following closure.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The mixed closure theorem has precisely one Following side and one Stable side. Absence of a general two-Following theorem establishes neither failure nor impossibility, so that exact closure claim remains **E**. Dropping PairEnc's legality before acceptance would allow the context to test a handle whose meaning is subsequently changed, defeating the stated expansion invariant; that is a distinct obstruction to removing the existing proof's legality premise.
9. **Security/errors.** Current point/step equalities are exact distributional marginal laws; `following-relsim` requires input sections for **every world** and both sides plus output projection agreement for every world. The resulting ordinary span can use existing equality/observation bridges. There is no negligible term or UC simulator supplied merely by changing worlds.
10. **Obligations.** For another consumer prove world formation/promotion coherence, legality invariants, joint-step marginals, compatible feedback and the boundary section/output laws; establish a test/context bridge. If allowing two changing components, specify and prove a common-world synchronization law rather than assuming it. Computational cryptographic reductions remain separate.

### C01 — Certified byte interpretation is query admissibility, not crypto soundness

1. **Consumers/status.** Constructed certified fine symbolic setup and its implemented morphism into the negligible computational family setup. This is separate from the coarse symbolic source and from supplied-backtranslation soundness.
2. **Primary contract.** Exact code contract `Symbolic/Computational.agda:3–6,97–143,158–176,209–215`; S6 Def1/Thm1 and S7 Thm12 specify extra assumptions required for actual computational soundness, detailed in C02/C03.
3. **Signature/laws.** `Scheme` supplies a Bits set, symbol implementations indexed by η, nonce distribution, decidable bit equality and sort tag. A certified Generator contains a Role, unit-free input/output, positive rate r and `∀η. Certified(value r)(runᶜ η role)`. Free monoidal interpretation then yields a strong monoidal functor to admissible machine families. The certification is query/resource admissibility of this machine interpretation, not the cryptographic assumptions of either paper.
4. **Actual API.** `Computational/Run.agda:47–54,78–84` exposes the fields and byte recipe evaluation. It has no encryption-security, E-soundness, tag-injectivity, collision or PPT fields. `Computational.agda:100–116` declares certified source; `leafᶠ`158–161 uses constant polynomial rate; `Fᶜ`166–167 is `freeStrongMonoidal`; `toᶜ`174–176 closes translated test ancilla with `deadᶠ`; `νᶜ`209–212 is natural and `fine→comp`214–215 is a `UCSetupMorphism`.
5. **Class.** **A** for existing FreeMonoidal/StdUCMorphism/family-query machinery with this actual instance. **D** for “Certified implies cryptographically sound/PPT” or unconditional coarse-source security preservation. **E** for missing target-test/ancilla/fullness claims.
6. **Exact instance/minimal additions.** The free functor consumes `certData` and the certified leaf family, so its functor laws follow from the existing API. To lift **coarse** equations/security, add an implementation relation for E and primitives, representation/type/parsing assumptions, collision/security bounds and a context/backtranslation theorem with the required quantifiers. Full target computational UC additionally needs feasibility/adversary closure and ancillary coverage, not just translated tests.
7. **Positives/controls.** Constant-rate certified role families are a real positive; symbolic source→machine-iso is another fine-syntax interpretation (`Machine.agda:169–196`). **[INFERENCE]** A scheme can return constant nonces or implement an “encryption” symbol by revealing its argument while still having finite per-query work/bounds. Nothing in the Scheme fields excludes that. Thus query certification cannot establish secrecy or symbolic E-soundness.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The control is a deficiency of the claimed implication, not an implementation bug. No source coarse→computational soundness is inferred from the certified **fine** arrow. Closing each translated ancillary test with dead input is not a density proof for arbitrary target tests/closures.
9. **Security/errors.** The formal morphism carries no cryptographic reduction/error. Generic security transfer still needs U-preservation, obtained through a proven coverage route, not assumed. A polynomial query bound is not a common negligible error over all programs or an execution-time bound on arbitrary Bits/impl values.
10. **Obligations.** Instantiate actual primitive assumptions and representation correctness; prove or supply context extraction and uniformity over the compared honest programs; show admissibility of source-derived simulators and closure under computational adversaries if claiming canonical target `≤UC`. Keep source setup construction, interpreter functoriality and crypto soundness as separate deliverables.

### C02 — Passive formal-encryption computational soundness

1. **Consumers/status.** S6 provides a restricted target theorem relevant to symbolic term/frame interpretation, not a theorem already instantiated by local `Scheme` and not active UC soundness.
2. **Primary contract.** S6 Def1/type-0 security, Fig1 and Thm1; acyclic-key definition and key-cycle discussion (`P/abadirogaway2002.txt:255–321,539–611,757–811,1089–1112`).
3. **Native signature/laws.** Expressions consist of bits, pairs, keys and symmetric encryptions under key names. Keys receive independent generated values; ciphertexts use randomized encryption and tagged encodings. Formal expression equivalence implies computational indistinguishability of interpretation ensembles for **acyclic** expressions under the paper's **type-0** encryption-security assumption, which hides more than ordinary type-3/CPA leakage (including key/repetition/length distinctions as specified there). It is a passive fixed-expression theorem.
4. **Actual API.** `Term`/`Recipe`/PairEnc provide syntax and equations; `Scheme`/`evalᶜ` provide an arbitrary computational interpretation; `Soundness` is conditional on separate extraction. No S6 key-acyclicity/type-0 instance theorem is declared in these mapped modules.
5. **Class.** **C** for this restricted computational interpretation; **D** for arbitrary implementations, self-key encryption or replacing the theorem by general active UC soundness. Local instantiation is **E**.
6. **Minimal additions.** An expression-fragment embedding and formal-equivalence correspondence; acyclic key dependency; independently generated honest keys and encryption randomness; type-0 security and representation/length/tag conditions. An active theorem additionally needs an adaptive-context argument rather than this passive premise.
7. **Positives/controls.** Fixed acyclic paired/encrypted messages are positive. S6 explicitly constructs secure-scheme variants that fail on self-encryption `E_k(k)`; the paper does not license a blanket assertion that every longer key cycle has the same known counterexample. Constant/insecure byte implementations in C01 are the simpler local-assumption control.
8. **Obstruction scope.** Key-dependent messages can fall outside the security game's promise; acyclicity is not cosmetic. **[INFERENCE]** A theorem quantified over each fixed pair of expressions cannot be substituted for one simultaneous adaptive context extractor merely by treating an interaction trace as an expression after it has been chosen.
9. **Security/errors.** For each fixed eligible M,N and PPT distinguisher, the distinguishing advantage is negligible under the encryption assumption; reduction cost depends on the expressions/encryption occurrences (the proof discusses a loss involving m+n). No UC simulator or universal all-program error is produced; the implication is soundness, not general reflection/completeness of computational equivalence.
10. **Obligations.** Establish an exact fragment interpretation, formal-equivalence relation and primitive game reduction; preserve key/rand discipline and account for the occurrence-dependent loss. Do not extend to interactive, malformed-input, key-revealing or session-sharing contexts without a theorem covering those cases.

### C03 — Interactive soundness, supplied backtranslation and ground-guarded quantifiers

1. **Consumers/status.** S7 proves an interactive restricted-process theorem. Local `Symbolic.Soundness` derives conditional feasible-context agreement from an explicitly **supplied** Backtranslation; it does not construct that prerequisite or claim canonical target adversary-quantified UC.
2. **Primary contract.** S7 §3.3 simple-process grammar, Def11 key hierarchy, Thm12, Lemmas24–25 and §5 adaptive trace discussion; actual local contracts `Soundness.agda:45–72,97–128,147–161` and `Soundness/Errors.agda:21–45`.
3. **Native signature/laws.** S7 simple processes have disciplined received-message checks, failure behavior, tagged formats, fresh encryption randomness and the required key hierarchy in reachable executions; key correctness includes the stipulated certification/checking of adversarial keys. Under **joint IND-CPA and INT-CTXT**, observationally equivalent eligible processes have computationally indistinguishable interactions. This is not an arbitrary symbol implementation result. Locally `GroundGuarded` requires a distinguished ground-control state, a ground jump at that state and ground jumps on failing test branches; ground output alone is explicitly insufficient (`Role.agda:218–238`).
4. **Actual API.** `GroundGuardedC` closes the per-role predicate structurally (`Soundness.agda:45–49`). Feasible Context contains an ancilla, test, closure and separate feasibility proofs at62–68. `Backtranslation.extract`70–72/120–121 supplies an Extraction for every W and feasible context c. Extraction106–118 has η-indexed samples, symbolic ancilla/test/closure, a result for every honest f, classification for each `GroundGuardedC f`, and a protocol-local approximate realization. `sound⁺`126–128 derives all-W feasible-context agreement. `sound-≤UC`156–161 additionally requires GroundGuardedC of the **dummy simulator extracted from the source UC proof** and returns `DummyWitness`.
5. **Class.** **C** for the restricted soundness pattern and S7 hypotheses. **D** for treating the Backtranslation record as a proved extractor, ground payload as full guarding, or the output as canonical target `≤UC`. Deriving a concrete cryptographic extractor/target-UC bridge is **E** in the inspected modules.
6. **Minimal additions.** An actual context extractor satisfying the record, backed by primitive security, parsing/type validity and key/randomness restrictions; an appropriate feasible-context class with its operational justification. To obtain full computational UC, prove feasible tests/closures absorb every allowed computational adversary and preserve relevant resource bounds, and prove the retained simulator admissible. No such closure is a field of the current arbitrary Feasible predicate.
7. **Positives/controls.** S7's restricted simple processes are a primary positive; the local `sound⁺`/`sound-≤UC` are positive conditional derivations. S7 stresses that high-probability soundness for each fixed trace is insufficient when the attacker selects traces adaptively; a simultaneous tree/extraction argument is needed. Local comments explicitly reject “ground output implies termination/input independence” and “this is the target canonical UC order.”
8. **Obstruction scope.** The paper's adaptive issue and missing feasibility closure identify exact failed inferences, not a proof that no suitable extractor or closure theorem exists. The local ground-guarded condition is not asserted identical to S7's simple-process grammar; correspondence remains a proof obligation.
9. **Quantifiers/errors.** Precisely, the supplied prerequisite is `∀A,B,W,c. ∃Extraction(W,c)`; its sampling distribution and symbolic contexts are chosen **before the honest f**. That extraction supplies `error(f,η)` with `∀f. GroundGuardedC f → Negligible(error f)` and `∀f,η` realization bounds. There is no one common negligible function for all contexts/programs. For fixed W,c,f,g the derived bound is `error f η + error g η`; the classification quantifies every η/sample/verdict and preserves both verdict masses, with divergence represented by `nothing`. `sound-≤UC` retains one source dummy simulator s first, requires `GG(f), GG(g), GG(s)`, then yields `∀W ∀feasible c. ∃negligible δ(W,c,f,g,s). ∀η` agreement. It does not reorder to `∃δ ∀W,c,f` or to `∀target adversary ∃target simulator` without extra closure. S7 likewise fixes eligible processes and attacker before its negligible errors; Lem25 adds the two lift-failure errors to the tree comparison.
10. **Obligations.** Construct, rather than assume, the extractor for a concrete scheme and feasible class; prove its shared-before-f quantifier, handling of divergence/malformed inputs and all-world ancilla; relate source guarding to the concrete simple-process discipline; prove source-simulator guarding/admissibility; derive adversary absorption and query-sensitive error scaling if upgrading to target UC. The existing conditional theorem should then be reused unchanged where its contract fits.

### R01 — One-shot relational composition, product tensor and separating predicate tests

1. **Consumers/status.** The actual `Examples.RelSetup.StdSetup`; generic StdUC wiring is already covered by A01. This row records the specific carrier/readout rather than inventing a “relational UC” proof layer from its name.
2. **Primary contract.** R3 §4 defines `R;S = {(x,z) | ∃y. xRy ∧ ySz}` and identity; R1 §§3.1–3.2 uses product tensor on Rel. R2 §1 explicitly distinguishes that monoidal product from categorical products/coproducts. Local readout faithfulness is primary code, `RelSetup.agda:31–68`.
3. **Signature/laws.** Objects are sets, arrows binary relations, equality mutual inclusion, composition existential middle-state elimination, tensor componentwise relation on Cartesian products, tensor unit a singleton. All predicates `B→⊤` are tests. Singleton predicates distinguish any unequal relation; thus this particular observation is not a coarsened support quotient of some already probabilistic source.
4. **Actual API.** `RelSetup.agda:19–38` imports `Categories.Category.Instance.Rels` and `Categories.Category.Monoidal.Instance.Rels`, forms `Rel` with `Rels-Monoidal`, and instantiates `StdUC Rel (Hom[-,⊤])`. The installed dependency was directly inspected at `/nix/store/damk5ndj6x64821i4v858pc7rfbaz6z6-agda-categories-0.3.0/src/Categories/` (abbreviated **AC/** below): `Category/Instance/Rels.agda:14–36` gives relation equality/composition; `Category/Monoidal/Instance/Rels.agda:87–107` gives product tensor and singleton unit. `ℰ-faithful`50–65 uses point predicates; `≈ᵁ⇒≈`67–68 reflects U-equality to relation equality. `encChan`85–86, `otp-perfect`94–95 and the dummy simulators/`UC-compose`124–147 are concrete carrier calculations and security consumers.
5. **Class.** **A** for the existing relation carrier/monoidal factory/StdUC instance and ordinary exact relational equational reasoning. **C** for imposing a functional/cartesian substructure on another model. **D** for identifying product tensor with unrestricted natural copying of nondeterministic resources.
6. **Exact instance/minimal additions.** Category is `Rels 0ℓ 0ℓ`, tensor is `Rels-Monoidal`, observation is the representable `Hom[-,⊤]`. The same dependency also already exports `Rels-Cartesian` and `Rels-Cocartesian` (`AC/Category/Monoidal/Instance/Rels.agda:30–85`), whose categorical product/coproduct is **disjoint union**, with empty terminal/initial object; these are not the product-tensored instance chosen by RelSetup. Bare UCSetup has no relational representation or subset tests. **[INFERENCE]** The generic equality→U direction together with the local reflection proves `≈hom ⇔ ≈ᵁ` here; bare kernel is also faithful by `ℰ-faithful`. That does not make `≤UC` equality: the simulator still changes the leakage interface.
7. **Positives/controls.** `otp-perfect` proves every ciphertext is possible for each message by choosing a key; `send≤ideal` reuses the generic dummy theorem. `leaky-not-secure`164–172 proves an input-leaking relay cannot emulate the no-leak ideal. **[INFERENCE] value-copy control:** the diagonal copies an already chosen Bool, but for `R:1→Bool` relating the point to both bits, `Δ∘R` permits only `(0,0),(1,1)`, whereas `(R×R)∘Δ` also permits `(0,1),(1,0)`. Thus a relation that copies a value does not duplicate an independent resource choice, and diagonal naturality fails on arbitrary relations.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The copy calculation is a concrete failure, not merely lack of a Cartesian API. The source's `copy` at180–185 reads the **honest output** to reconstruct a leaked bit; a UC simulator restricted to the ideal leakage grade cannot do that. `relay-outcomes`187–195 and `blind-incomplete`199–201 are an existing counterexample to environment-blind robustness characterising UC.
9. **Security/errors.** Predicate observations are possibilistic/exact. The simulator precedes all predicate tests and input/ancilla contexts; it cannot be replaced by an environment postprocessor with access to honest output. No distribution, probability advantage or error algebra is supplied by RelSetup.
10. **Obligations.** When using a relational algebra equation, prove it in the actual product carrier and then reuse equality→U/dummy completeness. For a new relational representation of a model, prove composition/tensor/readout preservation, and separately faithfulness or simulator fullness if claiming reflection. Do not infer probability-sensitive security from this exact relational calculation.

### R02 — Converse/dagger, compact product trace and sum feedback are distinct

1. **Consumers/status.** One-shot Rel has an existing dependency-level dagger on its underlying category. Its product-compact trace is a primary mathematical structure, not an operation exported by the actual RelSetup file. Generic machine feedback already exists separately and is consumed by Symbolic proofs.
2. **Primary contract.** R1 Def3.1 (unit/counit and snake identities), §3.2 (Rel dual is converse), Def7.2/Prop7.3 (involutive contravariant dagger). R2 §1 pp.2–4 explicitly gives the two different relational traces and the GoI construction.
3. **Native signature/laws.** Converse is `R†:B→A`, `b R† a ⇔ a R b`, with `(S∘R)†=R†∘S†`, `id†=id`, `R††=R`. For Rel with **product** tensor, X is self-dual; cup/cap relate precisely equal pairs. Its compact trace is `Tr×_Z(R)(x,y) ⇔ ∃z. R((x,z),(y,z))` for `R:X×Z→Y×Z`. For Rel with **sum** tensor, `Tr+_Z(R)` instead follows a finite path from X through zero or more Z states to Y. R2's GoI objects are pairs `(A+,A−)` with arrows `A+⊗B−→A−⊗B+`, composition via the chosen trace; this changes the carrier and is not automatically a UC setup.
4. **Actual API.** `AC/Category/Dagger/Instance/Rels.agda:12–25` exports `RelsHasDagger` (`_†=flip`) and `RelsDagger`, including functoriality/involution. `AC/Category/Monoidal/Instance/Rels.agda:172–204` separately exports `Rels-Symmetric` for the product tensor. These are available carrier structures; `RelSetup.agda:19–38` itself selects only Rels/product monoidality and tests. No Rel compact/trace instance was found among the three installed files named `Rels.agda` or declared in RelSetup; that is a bounded export observation, not an exhaustive absence claim about every differently named dependency. Existing `CC/Machines/Trace.agda:25–28,51–75` and `Machines/Sim/Relational.agda:290–299` implement **stateful coproduct-interface** iteration/trace, with the actual Symbolic consumers in P01/P02.
5. **Class.** **A** for the existing relational dagger carrier instance and for the separately existing machine trace API. **C** for packaging compact/product trace or GoI with the required carrier/test data. **D** for assuming a dagger is an inverse, that product trace is a state/iteration constructor, or that arbitrary UCSetup has these structures. General UC-security closure under a proposed trace/reversal is **E**, not established merely by the carrier equations.
6. **Exact instance/minimal additions.** `RelsHasDagger` applies to exactly `Rels 0ℓ 0ℓ`. A compact construction additionally supplies symmetric monoidality, object duals, cup/cap and snake laws; a sum-feedback construction instead needs the appropriate finite-path/iteration structure. For a UC closure theorem, type the traced leakage interfaces, specify what loop information the simulator sees, and prove test/ancilla compatibility. Bare UCSetup contains none of these data.
7. **Positives/controls.** R1's equality cup/cap is the primary Rel positive; Symbolic's existing machine trace context closure is a different implemented positive. **[INFERENCE] dagger control:** for discard `d:Bool→1`, `d†∘d` is the full relation on Bool, not identity; `d†` chooses a possible value, it does not recover a discarded one. **[INFERENCE] compact-trace control:** tracing the Bool-negation permutation gives the empty scalar because no z equals not z, while tracing identity gives the true scalar. This is a one-shot consistency constraint, not a persistent initialized store.
8. **Obstruction scope.** Different domains `X×Z→Y×Z` and `X+Z→Y+Z` already preclude identifying the two trace operations without a representation/translation. Neither compact cups nor relational converse supply an initial state, probabilistic law or resource-independent duplicate. The bounded missing Rel compact instance is not evidence the mathematical construction fails.
9. **Security/errors.** Dagger and trace respect the relevant **carrier equality**; for the separating Rel tests that can be converted to U-equality. However, transposing `f:A→X×B` changes it to `X×B→A`, not another protocol of the same real/ideal security type. A simulator factoring leakage before transpose need not remain a valid simulator after it. No quantitative error or generic UC feedback closure follows from these equations.
10. **Obligations.** Reuse the existing dagger and machine trace where their signatures fit. For a Rel compact instance prove the equality cup/cap snake and trace laws; for a GoI category prove its composition/embedding and then supply a separate observation presheaf/grades. For security closure prove a simulator- and context-respecting loop theorem, including ancillary coverage; do not mistake carrier equality congruence for UC-order monotonicity.

### R03 — Union, finite Kleene iteration and relational proof principles

1. **Consumers/status.** Binary union and finite-path reflexive/transitive closure are existing generic relation operators in the installed standard library. They apply literally to the one-shot Rel carrier, but RelSetup does not export a choice/star security theorem or a KAT proof package. Native symbolic replication remains N02, not this endorelation star.
2. **Primary contract.** R3 §2 defines the regular/KAT signature; §4 p.9 gives union, composition, reflexive-transitive closure, empty/identity/full relations and test atoms as partition-selected subidentities. Thm4.21 proves completeness for **KAT with full-relation top**, adding `x≤top` and `x≤x·top·x`. R3 explicitly distinguishes other signatures involving converse/intersection; no completeness theorem for a freely combined “all relational operations” signature is inferred.
3. **Native signature/laws.** For endorelations R,S on X, union is disjunction, zero is empty, one is equality, and `R*` holds precisely along a finite path of length n≥0. Composition distributes over union; star is the least reflexive/transitive closure. Tests are subidentity relations from an explicit finite partition/Boolean test algebra. **[INFERENCE]** Path induction yields the usual invariant rule: if P holds initially and each R edge preserves P, every R* path preserves P; guarded relational composition gives partial-correctness tests, not a liveness or termination guarantee.
4. **Actual API.** Directly inspected installed standard library 2.3 at `/nix/store/npghc9r78hv41my5xgdbv50wwg0q65f2-standard-library-2.3/src/` (abbreviated **SL/**): `Relation/Binary/Construct/Union.agda:31–32` defines `_∪_ : REL A B ℓ₁ → REL A B ℓ₂ → REL A B (ℓ₁⊔ℓ₂)`. `.../Closure/ReflexiveTransitive.agda:21–34` defines `Star`/ε/cons/transitive append; `:57–88` gives gmap/map and fold/foldl. In particular `fold P : Trans R P P → Reflexive P → Star R ⇒ P` is an actual reusable induction principle. These apply to Rels at level0 without implementing duplicate operators. No KAT completeness/choice-security/iteration-security theorem is declared in the inspected `RelSetup.agda:1–201`.
5. **Class.** **A** for the existing generic **carrier operators** with the literal `REL A B 0ℓ`/`Rel A 0ℓ` instances used by `Rels 0ℓ 0ℓ`; not an assertion that RelSetup currently invokes or exports them. **C** for an ordered/Kleene-enriched categorical proof package and for security closure under additional uniform-simulator assumptions. **D** for union or iteration preserving arbitrary UC witnesses merely because each branch/step is secure.
6. **Exact instance/minimal additions.** At carrier level use SL union/Star/fold directly. A general algebraic package needs join-enriched homs, composition preserving joins, identity/zero and a lawful least-iteration operation; KAT additionally needs the chosen test subalgebra. Classical arbitrary complement is not an automatic Boolean algebra of constructive Agda predicates; use decidable tests or explicit Boolean-partition data. Security additionally needs a common simulator or an observable selector/dispatch structure, and an iteration contract for leakage grades and resource use.
7. **Positives/controls.** **[INFERENCE] common-simulator positive:** if every `fᵢ=(s×id)∘gᵢ` uses the same s, distributivity gives `⋃fᵢ=(s×id)∘⋃gᵢ`; equality→U and dummy completeness then apply. **[INFERENCE] nonuniform-simulator counterexample:** input A=Bool, honest output B=1, real leakage X=Bool, ideal leakage Y=1. For i∈Bool let `fᵢ={(i,(i,*))}` and `gᵢ={(i,(*,*))}`. Each fᵢ emulates gᵢ with the constant-i simulator. Their unions are input-leak versus no-leak, and no one `1→Bool` relation can match both inputs. An ideal observable branch tag would supply the missing distinction.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The counterexample is an actual failure of `∀i.∃sᵢ` implying `∃s.∀i`, even in the constructed one-shot carrier with separating tests. It does not refute union congruence for carrier equality or the common-simulator restricted theorem. Star relates only finite successful paths; no infinite behavior, fairness, fresh process creation or universal session simulator is encoded merely by `Star R`.
9. **Security/errors.** Native algebraic laws have no simulator or error. Exact carrier equality is preserved by union/star and can feed this Rel U-kernel; UC security with changing leakage grades needs more than order monotonicity of relations. Finite repeated quantitative uses would require the appropriate accumulated error and grade/resource accounting; unbounded star does not supply a polynomial usage bound or keep negligible error uniformly across arbitrary path lengths.
10. **Obligations.** Reuse SL operators, package only the missing algebraic laws/typed test interface, and prove any chosen KAT expression interpretation before importing the primary completeness theorem. For union/iteration security specify and prove the single-simulator/dispatch invariant, grade aggregation and admitted-context closure. For quantitative versions prove usage-dependent error composition and termination/availability separately; retain the two-branch counterexample as a non-negotiable control.

### R04 — Forgetting probabilities to support cannot reflect probabilistic security

1. **Consumers/status.** One-shot Rel is a constructed possibilistic setup. Separately, finite rational/partial-distribution support and its strong-monoidal **SFun** transport are already implemented carrier constructions, not missing abstractions. SFun is a pure-initial-state, finite-word stateful-function category (`CC/SFunM.agda:21–28,56–69`), distinct from one-shot Rel and the sealed PM model; no SFun UC instance was found in the owner/scout census. No setup/test/grade bridge is inferred merely from this carrier functor.
2. **Primary contract.** R1 §6 identifies Rel scalars as Boolean truth values; R3 §4 gives existential relation composition. S6 Fig1/Thm1 and S7 Thm12 instead concern probability ensembles and computational distinguishing advantages. Primary code contracts are `src/ProbabilisticLogic/Distribution/RationalDist/Support.agda:90–95,151–174,196–211` (abbreviated **Support** below), `CC/SFunM/Morphism.agda:22–28,49–104,113–139`, and `CC/SFunPossibility.agda:28–57`. These declarations were read, not newly typechecked.
3. **Signature/laws.** `supp : Dist-ℚ A → List A` keeps **strictly positive** entries and is compared by possibility equality, not raw-list equality; zero-weight representation entries must be discarded for congruence. `supp⊥ : Dist⊥ A → List A` additionally removes `nothing`, taking certain divergence to the empty list. Congruence, return and bind laws make these setoid monad morphisms. Generic `SFunM.Morphism` postcomposes each step kernel with the supplied monad morphism while keeping state/initial state; `θ-trace`/`θ-eval` preserve finite-word execution and the resulting functor is strong monoidal for the SFun event-routed tensor. This is not a general measure-theoretic singleton-support theorem, a compact trace, or automatically a one-shot-Rel interpretation.
4. **Actual API.** **Support** exports `supp-cong`151–163, `supp-return`169–170, `supp-bind`172–174, and `supp⊥-cong`199–202/`supp⊥-return`204–205/`supp⊥-nothing`207–208/`supp⊥-bind`210–211. `CC/SFunPossibility.agda:28–41` defines the possibility `Listᴰ`; lines46–47 instantiate `SFunMorphism Dist⊥ᴰ Listᴰ supp⊥` with those laws; `suppᵉ`, `SFun-supp`, `SFun-supp-monoidal` are exported at49–57. Existing negative controls are **Support** `pad-supp-perfect`267–273, `pad-not-perfect`277–279 and `close-supp-differs`298–333. RelSetup's `encChan`/`otp-perfect`85–95 still concern existential keys, while symbolic `Scheme.nonce` and `Soundness/Errors` retain actual probability/error data.
5. **Class.** **A** for the existing finite rational/partial-distribution support maps and the exact `SFunM.Morphism` strong-monoidal instantiation above; they must be subtracted from a new-construction list. **C** only for extending that abstraction to another explicitly structured carrier/readout. **D** for reflecting probability-sensitive equality/security from support, and for turning arbitrarily small distributional error into exact support equality. A specified UC setup bridge, including tests, grades and their coverage, remains **E** unless separately constructed; carrier functoriality alone is not that bridge.
6. **Exact instance/minimal additions.** Reuse `Mo=Dist⊥ᴰ`, `No=Listᴰ`, `θ=supp⊥`, `θ-cong=supp⊥-cong`, `θ-return=supp⊥-return`, `θ-bind=supp⊥-bind`; do not repropose positivity/return/bind proofs or SFun monoidal transport as missing work. A UC comparison additionally needs the chosen source/target setups, test/readout translation, grade/effect compatibility and ancillary/simulator coverage. A relation between SFun finite-word semantics and one-shot Rel also needs an explicit representation. To retain probabilistic security use weights/quantitative observations; no theorem about support alone can reconstruct information it discards.
7. **Positives/controls.** Existing `SFun-supp-monoidal` is the positive algebraic transport; **Support** proves full ciphertext support for its biased pad and unequal ciphertext distributions for the two messages. **[INFERENCE] stronger fixed-simulator control, using the code's bias convention:** for Bool XOR with `Pr[K=1]=3/4`, `Pr[K=0]=1/4`, both inputs have full ciphertext support, but `Pr[C=0|M=0]=1/4` and `Pr[C=0|M=1]=3/4`. A no-leak ideal simulator has one input-independent probability q of outputting ciphertext0, so `max(|q−1/4|,|q−3/4|) ≥ 1/4`. Thus no such simulator matches both input contexts with negligible error. This security-quantifier lower bound is a deduction beyond the existing distribution-inequality declaration, not a claim of a newly checked theorem.
8. **Obstruction scope.** The controls are finite rational examples, not pathological measures. **Support.close-supp-differs** supplies, for every rational `0<ε≤1`, ε-close distributions with unequal support; exact carrier-equality preservation therefore must not be confused with quantitative/negligible-kernel preservation. **[INFERENCE]** The biased-pad constant lower bound remains nonnegligible for a constant-bias family. Likewise, possible success cannot yield quantitative availability: partial success masses `2^(−η)` and1 have the same nonempty success support, while `supp⊥` forgets the difference in divergence probability.
9. **Security/errors.** The existing map preserves exact distribution equality and exact finite-word carrier equality, but is neither probability-reflecting nor a supplied UC/security transport theorem. The two biased-pad input contexts force the **same** simulator q to meet incompatible distributional requirements; letting q depend on the input/test would exchange the security quantifiers. Support carries no numerical advantage/error; `close-supp-differs` also obstructs an unconditional passage from small probabilistic error to exact possibilistic equality. RelSetup's `otp-perfect` is perfect **relational** equality, not an assertion that an arbitrary pad distribution is uniformly secret.
10. **Obligations.** Start from the existing support/strong-monoidal SFun APIs. Only the additional representation, observation-presheaf, grade/effect and context-coverage bridge remains to be supplied for a claimed setup comparison; specify its limited possibilistic adequacy and retain both code controls. Any probability-sensitive transfer must carry weights and quantitative observation laws, fixed-simulator quantifiers, resource bounds and the intended local/uniform error scope. Do not label a lossy algebraic functor or existential-key equality a cryptographic soundness theorem.

## Two-consumer opportunities, with concrete acceptance controls

These are proposed structured generalisations, not additional completed source constructions. Their exact ten-field obligations are N01/K02/P01/P02 and the Rel rows above.

### O-N — A nominal allocation/renaming/context layer, not a port-renaming layer

- **First actual consumer:** symbolic roles and the coarse contextual proof layer. Reuse `Term` substitution, `Supply` injective fresh extension, and the ownership/Foreign invariants; do not replace their theorem-specific conditions with a generic “fresh” Boolean.
- **Second source-theory opportunity:** IPDL. Targeted primary addendum: Gancher et al., *A core calculus for equational proofs of cryptographic protocols*, POPL2023, [DOI10.1145/3571223](https://doi.org/10.1145/3571223), `P/gancher2023ipdl.txt:535–568,594–605,875–883,1007–1012`: Fig4 generates private channels modulo α-equivalence; Fig6 typing enforces scope/single writer; context embeddings are **injective**; Fig11's comp-new law is typed scope extrusion. These are the native contracts a shared layer could target.
- **Current IPDL code control:** directly inspected `CC/IPDL/Syntax.agda:47–55` has a typed map `Ren` with no injectivity field; `:61–64` has finite hidden context H and definitions over `I ++ (O ++ H)`. Hidden positions and a type-preserving map are not a proof of fresh allocation, α-invariance or injective context embedding. Current IPDL is an interpreter/resource interpretation, not a setup or a completed nominal-instance theorem. Fine/coarse sharing one carrier is not counted as two independent nominal theories.
- **[INFERENCE] Minimal shared contract:** distinguish arbitrary value substitution (which may alias) from injective fresh renaming; give a finite-support/typed-binding representation, equivariance, capture-avoiding substitution and composition laws, fresh extension, and restriction/extrusion compatible with interpreted contexts. Prove source-specific representation into supplies for Symbolic and into typed hidden positions for IPDL.
- **Concrete controls:** the N01 `[b/x](νb.out(c,x))` capture example; illegal extrusion across a free use of the restricted name; two same-typed distinct channels collapsed by a noninjective Ren. The latter can turn reads of two independently assigned values into reads of one value, so “typed renaming” alone cannot instantiate the native injection law. Port-tag disjointness tests cannot replace any of these controls.
- **Security deliverable:** an α-/scope-respecting interpretation and a context coverage theorem, followed by existing kernel/U transport. Native channel linking, state persistence and probabilistic observations remain model-specific. No dynamic replication, general scheduling or cryptographic soundness follows from the nominal interface alone.

### O-R — Shared relational proof infrastructure with an explicit observation bridge

- **First actual constructed-theory consumer:** one-shot Rel, with relation composition/product and all subset tests. Extensional relation equations immediately feed existing equality→U and dummy completeness.
- **Second actual constructed-theory consumer:** coarse Symbolic interpreted in PM. Its Same/Emits apex proofs and RunFollowing already consume `Machines.Sim.Relational`, `UC.Model.Relational` and the world-indexed Process algebra. Reuse these existing generic carrier operators; do not rename them as new setup factories.
- **[INFERENCE] Useful common boundary:** package the part of an algebraic/relational proof that is stable under the admitted contexts and adequate for the chosen observation. For one-shot Rel it is extensional relational equality with separating predicates; for machines it additionally requires initialized state, both step marginals, pure projections and possibly feedback iteration; Symbolic additionally requires recipe equality/freshness and its verdict tester bridge. The common part cannot erase those differences.
- **Concrete controls:** biased pad (same support, different marginals); copying one already chosen value versus independently duplicating a choice; observation coarsening to constant tests (loss of reflection); and `RelSetup.blind-incomplete` (environment reconstructs the leak from honest output, while a simulator cannot). A proposed relation-lifting abstraction must distinguish all four.
- **Security deliverable:** prove context preservation and a bare-/U-kernel bridge at the intended observation, then separately construct or transport one simulator before all tests. Algebraic converse, compact trace, union and star do not themselves produce such a simulator. Approximate generalisation must retain actual distribution weights, errors and resource-sensitive usage, not only a may-reachability relation.

## Coverage and conclusion

The bounded corpus covers process substitution/restriction/freshness/scope extrusion, parallel/replication/conditionals; recipe deduction/static equivalence and context/labeled-bisimulation principles; symbolic simulators/session separation; passive and interactive computational interpretation contracts; and the requested one-shot relational algebra families. It covers all four named constructed nodes and the three auxiliary proof-layer families without relabeling the latter as setups.

The strongest implemented generality is already **A**: Standard2/dummy/serial composition, same-carrier observation coarsening and conditional transfer, generic machine spans, and world-indexed machine following. The meaningful **C** opportunities are nominal representation with real binding/scope laws, typed tester completeness, observation-compatible relational proof reuse, and concrete cryptographic backtranslation. The principal **D** controls are unindexed equivalence-based replication, capture/illegal scope extrusion, arbitrary-theory decidability, static equivalence of frames misapplied to active processes, certification mistaken for crypto soundness, probabilistic security inferred from support, and simulator/test quantifier exchange. **E** boundaries are explicitly correspondence/coverage/extractor proofs, not asserted impossibility.

No repository changes or verification runs accompany this research artifact.

# Appendix K — IPDL/CSP source inventory

Original durable entry point: [construction-generality-ipdl-csp.md](#appendix-k)

# IPDL and CSP: native constructions, generality, and assumption boundaries

## Scope and evidence boundary

Read-only primary-source research for the construction-generality census. No repository source/docs were changed and no build, typecheck, test, lint, formatter, or Git validation was run. Local code references below are relative to `/Users/andre/IOHK/categorical-crypto/.worktrees/uc-setup-landscape/src/CategoricalCrypto/`, unless a paper-archive path is specified. The source proof boundary `9f9be2a89` and docs-only head `b24ae5f92` are parent-reported, not independently validated here.

**IPDL is an implemented finite interpreter and SSP-distribution resource interpretation, not an IPDL `UCSetup` or `UCSetupMorphism`.** It already has the concrete hidden-channel, identity, flattening, and seeded-feedback squares listed below. “A general typed linker is absent” must not be shortened to “linking is absent.” **CSP is in progress, with no checked/integrated setup at this boundary.** Proposed or concurrently implemented CSP names are not treated as stable APIs. The external ITree-CSP source is a distinct, precisely pinned evidence corpus, not a checked landing in categorical-crypto.

Read and used: [construction-generality-common-brief.md](#appendix-a-common-research-contract), [construction-generality-coverage.md](#appendix-a), both completed scouts, owner-reviewed [construction-generality-generic-apis.md](#appendix-b), and owner structural synthesis [construction-generality-structural-evidence.md](#appendix-d). The last supplies the Matt connection-algebra, guarded-trace, initialized-state and copying distinctions; it does not replace either theory's native semantics.

Classification: **A** = already-generic construction with an actual instance/consumer; **B** = follows from arbitrary setup axioms but not packaged; **C** = structured class requiring explicit extra data; **D** = the stated unrestricted extension is false, with a scoped counterexample; **E** = exact claim unestablished in the inspected evidence. C/D pairings distinguish the valid restricted construction from its invalid extension. New mathematical comparisons and proposed interfaces are marked **[INFERENCE]**.

## 1. Explicit primary corpus and completeness boundary
