This is a complete classification of the requested **reaction/linking/name/sampling/exchange/context/security** IPDL families and **ret/bind/sequential/parallel/interleaving/choice/hiding/renaming/recursion/iteration/bisimulation/FD/FSim/up-to** CSP families within this explicit corpus. It is not a claim of exhaustive worldwide literature, all CSP dialects, or all ITree-CSP applications.

## 2. Already-generic coverage — excluded from the new-construction list

### A0 — Existing SSP-distribution target and standard UC infrastructure

1. **Construction/status/models.** Existing monoidal quotient/test descent plus `Standard2.StdUC` at the **SSPᵈ target** used by the IPDL interpreter. A target substrate, not a newly constructed IPDL setup; no CSP instance.
2. **Source.** The target declarations are `SSP/Dist/Setup.agda:26–35,43–72`; generic contract `Standard2.agda:19–34`. IPDL publication Def. 4.7/Fig. 9 [IP-paper] specifies the native experiment, whose current interpreter is related to this target by the separate source-specific theorem below.
3. **Signature/laws.** Given lawful `Protᵈ : MonoidalCategory` and descended `ℰᵈ : Presheaf`, `StdUC Protᵈ ℰᵈ` supplies `T_X B = X⊗B`, `StdSetup`, U-agreement and UC machinery. `Protᵈ = quotient Protᴹ congᵈ`; tests descend with `≈ᵈ⇒≋`.
4. **Repo.** Actual `open Std2.StdUC Protᵈ ℰᵈ public` at line 32; `ssp⇒dist` at 34–35; preservation/reflection at 43–72. `IPDL/SSP.resource:29–32` returns `SSPᵈ.unit ⇒ API I O`; it does not define a source setup.
5. **Class.** **A**, for this existing target instance only. Do not count a new quotient, setup constructor, or generic UC composition theorem here.
6. **Exact instantiation.** `machines := Protᵈ`, `ℰ-standard := ℰᵈ`; existing quotient functor/test descent instantiate `StdUCMorphism`. No IPDL source functor or CSP setup parameter is supplied by these declarations.
7. **Witnesses.** Ordinary SSP and its distribution quotient are actual setup consumers; the IPDL resource and finite tester run in that quotient target. CSP has no checked consumer at this boundary.
8. **Obstruction/scope.** [INFERENCE] Target category laws cannot manufacture a source network linker or prove its native interpreter square. A hom equality in the target and a theorem about compiled native tests remain different facts from source UC emulation.
9. **Security/errors.** SSP→SSPᵈ preserves and reflects its stated source UC order. Generic order is `∀a:X→X′. ∃s:Y→X′. sub(a)f ≈ᵁ sub(s)g`, with **all ancillas/tests after s**. No quantitative IPDL setup or computational IPDL error theorem is instantiated here.
10. **Obligations.** None to reimplement A0. For a new IPDL/CSP source edge, first provide its lawful category/setup, graded/strong-monoidal interpretation and natural tests, then prove U preservation with actual target-context coverage; reflection additionally needs representative target simulators/grade fullness. Reuse the existing APIs only after those conditions hold.

The already-generic finite-error arithmetic (`Approx/Rational.hybrid:95–99`), fixed-simulator QUC composition and query-aware contextual error scaling are also coverage items from G8–G9 of the owner catalogue, **not new IPDL/CSP constructions**. No direct native instantiation of those quantitative signatures was found in these mapped source modules. Their future applicability is C, not an invented A consumer.

## 3. IPDL rows

### IP1 — Reaction sequencing, sampling, persistent reads, exchange and quiescent evaluation

1. **Construction/status/models.** Native finite reactions and probabilistic protocol evaluation; current Agda IPDL interpreter implements a finite typed rational fragment, not the entire interpreted-signature calculus.
2. **Source.** Publication Defs. 4.1–4.2, Figs. 4–8, Lemma 4.3/Cor. 4.4 [IP-paper]; archive `gancher2023ipdl.txt:509–715`. Full report Fig. 15 [IP-report], `:1425–1469`, states reaction equations.
3. **Signature/laws.** Native `Δ;Γ ⊢ R:I→τ` admits return, read, sampling, conditional and bind. Interpretation assigns bitstring types, functions and finitely supported distributions. A read without an assigned value **blocks**. Protocols are finite single-writer networks; independent reduction choices commute and evaluation to a quiescent distribution is unique. Fig. 15 supplies left/right unit and associativity; `exch` swaps R₁,R₂ each typed in the same Γ (neither may depend on the other's result); `samp-pure` drops an unused total sample; `read-det` reuses one persistent read; `flip-unif` preserves the uniform bit under negation.
4. **Repo.** `IPDL/Syntax.agda:18–45` has unit/bit/product values and indexed `Rxn` constructors `ret/read/coin`, where coin is `Dist-ℚ Bool`. `Native.agda:38–82` decreases reaction/cell work and saturates to `Quiet`. This is not a source-level generic reaction-monad or full signature interpretation theorem. `ReadOrder.agda:100–108` proves exact target resource equality and all-finite-strategy agreement for the mapped independent-read example.
5. **Class.** **C** for a reusable persistent-read/commutative-sampling semantics; **D** for exporting `exch` to arbitrary effectful interactions. No B result follows from bare UC axioms.
6. **Extra data.** Typed read-only/write-once environment, total normalized finite sampling, sequencing and continuation substitution, a blocking semantics, single-writer typing, and a decreasing-work/confluence argument. [INFERENCE] A structured commutative effect interface can express exchange, but it must distinguish a fixed stored value from a new execution of its producing computation. QUC adds none of these data.
7. **Witnesses.** Current IPDL read-order theorem and repeated reads of its hidden coin are positives. SSPᵈ rational bind is a potential shared probability substrate, but ordinary SSP calls are not persistent reads and are a control rather than an established second exchange consumer.
8. **Counterexample.** `ReadOrder.agda:110–126` constructs an environment-controlled oracle that returns true on its first call and false later. Demand-exposing compilations of opposite read orders return true versus false. This refutes a compiler which makes IPDL read demand externally observable, not the native exchange rule. [INFERENCE] Duplicating a fair sample gives unequal bits with probability 1/2, whereas reading the one stored sample twice never does; sampling is not deterministic copying.
9. **Security/errors.** Native exact equations aim at identical experiments, including whether an output is absent, under native contexts. The current example proves `≈ᵈ` plus equality for every finite adaptive `Strategy`; neither alone supplies source attack/simulator interfaces or a PPT theorem. Exact equality spends zero statistical error; no constant-error theorem for arbitrary repeated effects follows.
10. **Obligations.** Give a typed reaction interpretation preserving substitution, bind, sample laws and persistent reads; prove whole-network confluence/scheduling independence for its admitted syntax; preserve blocked output/initialization; derive run equality for all adaptive tests. A generic effect law must explicitly discharge exchange and duplication side conditions, not infer them from a monad or observation presheaf.

### IP2 — `Input/Get/Step` experiments and adaptive tester correspondence

1. **Construction/status/models.** Actual finite adaptive native experiment and its SSPᵈ resource/tester interpretation; **implemented**, not a source setup edge.
2. **Source.** Publication Defs. 4.5–4.8 and Fig. 9 [IP-paper], `gancher2023ipdl.txt:738–838`; the publication's stateful algorithms `Astep/Aout/Adecide` issue queries over k rounds.
3. **Signature/laws.** `Input(i,v)` supplies a value once; later writes have no effect. `Get(o)` returns the optional value **already present before this round's quiescence**. `Step` advances without supplying input or requesting a value. Every query is followed by evaluation to quiescence. A strategy may branch on replies and private randomness. Native PPT experiments additionally bound rounds and the running time of each distinguisher algorithm.
4. **Repo.** `Syntax.agda:66–87` defines `Store`, `assign` and `assign-persistent`. `Native.agda:84–119` defines typed `Port/Query/Reply/API`, `before`, `round`, `turn`; `:121–136` defines `Strategy` and `Experiment.run`. `IPDL/SSP.agda:29–40` defines `resource`, `compileTree`, `tester`; `Correspondence.agreement:90–92` states `runᵖ(tester a ∘ᵖ resource N) ≈Mℚ (Experiment.run N a >>= return⊥)` for every finite Boolean strategy a, including private coins. Lines 3–4 explicitly reject `UCSetupMorphism` status.
5. **Class.** **C**, an existing source-specific interpreter/correspondence reusable over a suitably specified store-based resource. **D** for changing it to a mutable-store, pull-on-demand, or output-after-quiescence API without a new semantics proof.
6. **Extra data.** Typed optional store cells and write-once law; residual state plus initialization; decreasing quiescence; query/reply protocol; adaptive finite strategy syntax; rational distribution interpretation and a compiler for those strategies. A bare setup does not expose state, optionality, rounds, or computational complexity.
7. **Witnesses.** Existing correspondence is positive for all finite strategies, stronger than isolated test examples. IP1's demand oracle is a negative control; SSP's stateful resource API is a real target consumer, but not a theorem that every target context backtranslates to IPDL.
8. **Counterexample.** [INFERENCE from `before/round`] On an uninitialized closed hidden-coin network, the first Get returns None even though it then computes the coin; a second Get can return Some. A compiler which quiesces before answering the first Get is distinguishable. Conflicting successive Inputs distinguish a mutable store from `assign-persistent`.
9. **Security/errors.** Quantifier is `∀ finite adaptive a. equality of the two acceptance distributions` (the code tests equality against the rational distribution setoid observations). It is not `∀ target adversary.∃ source simulator.∀ target ancillary test`. There is no statistical approximation in this theorem and no representation of arbitrary PPT bitstring computation in finite `Strategy` merely by its name.
10. **Obligations.** Extend only with an explicit strategy/resource abstraction and execution commuting square. For UC preservation prove compiler functoriality and a context/test-closing operation; characterize which target ancillas and testers it covers. For reflection supply source representatives of target simulators. For PPT claims supply indexed syntax, encodings, round/time bounds and uniform compilation-cost evidence.

### IP3 — Parallel networks, private channels, typed context algebra and nominal hygiene

1. **Construction/status/models.** Native protocol `||`, `new`, channel embedding and one-hole contexts; current Agda `Net` is a static finite presentation, not a complete nominal/context compiler.
2. **Source.** Publication Def. 4.1/Fig. 4 and Figs. 10–11 [IP-paper]; contexts at `:875–889`. Full report Figs. 14/18 [IP-report], `:1402–1416,1978–2011`. Context embeddings θ are **injective**; `new` binds a channel and hides it from the boundary, modulo alpha-renaming.
3. **Signature/laws.** `P:I∪O₂→O₁`, `Q:I∪O₁→O₂` with disjoint writer/output sets form `P||Q:I→O₁∪O₂`. Components can read each other's outputs: this is not separated package sequencing. A fresh private binding removes its output from the boundary. Typed contexts transform whole protocol judgments. Exact rules include parallel commutativity/associativity, fresh-channel exchange/scope movement with typing conditions, congruence, absorption and the explicitly conditioned substitution/unused-read rules.
4. **Repo.** `Syntax.agda:47–64` supplies type-preserving `Ren`, `rename`, and `Net` with hidden context H and exactly one definition per O++H slot. Its `Ren` has a function and type equation **but no injectivity field**; it is not by itself the paper's channel embedding. `Linker.agda:34–81` supplies a specific paired residual interpreter and flat net, not an arbitrary protocol-context syntax/nominal compiler. No general typed open-network compiler was found in the mapped IPDL modules.
5. **Class.** **C** for a typed finite wiring/name-binding layer. **D** for reading generic monoidal composition or an arbitrary reference map as the native linking/embedding operation.
6. **Extra data.** Finite typed boundaries, disjoint writer ownership, scope/alpha equivalence, freshening, an injective embedding class, wire aliases and private equivalence classes, and admissible cyclic linking. [INFERENCE] Matt's finite connection-algebra contract in the parent structural artifact is a useful common target with CSP, but its connection-order laws need a native execution proof here. Nominal freshness is not ordinary label equality or a trace operation.
7. **Witnesses.** Paper typed contexts and current static `Net` are positive presentations at different expressivity levels. IPDL paired linking and proposed CSP namespaced networks are a common-interface candidate; the latter is **not** a second constructed setup. Reusing SSP tensor without an interpretation of sibling channel reads is a nonexample.
8. **Counterexample.** [INFERENCE] Noninjectively identifying two formerly distinct input channels can turn `read i;read j` from two independently supplied persistent values into one value used twice; that is not harmless alpha-renaming. Duplicate writers are rejected by the native typing discipline. Freshening a private name must not identify it with an external boundary channel, and composing through an identity alias must not accidentally hide that external channel.
9. **Security/errors.** Exact native context congruence preserves native experiment equality; approximate parallel context insertion adds the other component's symbolic size to l (IP5). It does not synthesize a UC simulator. Context embedding and native test extension must preserve interface meaning before generic reobservation or quotient machinery can be used.
10. **Obligations.** Define the typed wiring/context representation and prove alpha/hygiene, single-writer closure, identity aliases, flattening, associativity/tensor interchange and connection-order independence **at execution level**. Prove native contexts close compiled tests, including ancillary interfaces. Only then instantiate the standard category/setup factory; use simulator/grade coverage separately for reflection.

### IP4 — Existing hidden-bit, identity and seeded-feedback squares; limits of general feedback

1. **Construction/status/models.** Concrete two-component IPDL linker correctness, including a cyclic dependency with a seed. **Already implemented source-specific squares**; no universal feedback or generic linker theorem.
2. **Source.** Native network/read/blocking rules are publication Figs. 7–8 [IP-paper]. Direct implementation evidence is `IPDL/Linker.agda:34–81,83–131,193–197,199–267`. The parent structural artifact supplies the separate Matt connection-order and guarded/stored-state controls.
3. **Signature/laws.** `Paired I n m out hidden` holds one input store and two residual cells; both consult the **same** current environment before each small step. `linked` is an SSP protocol, `flat` a `Net I Bit`. The output reaction in `Shared` reads one hidden fair sample **twice**, not two independent samples. Feedback's output reads the seed, returns true when it is true, otherwise waits for the hidden output; the hidden cell waits for the visible one.
4. **Repo.** `Shared.hidden-channel-square:123–124`, `Shared.adaptive-square:126–131`; `Identity.identity-square:193–194`, `flatten-square:196–197`; `Feedback.seeded-feedback-square:257–258`. `seed-releases-cycle:263–264` yields `just true`; `unseeded-cycle-blocks:266–267` yields `nothing` when the supplied seed is false. This last name means the cycle was not released, not that the input query was omitted.
5. **Class.** **C** for a generalized typed linker with shared persistent cells and disciplined blocking. **D** for inferring total cartesian trace/fixed points, instantaneous yanking, or all linking laws from these examples.
6. **Extra data.** Shared-store representation and monotone cell assignment, typed global environment, a choice/iteration procedure with work or admissibility invariant, and a flattening representation map preserving initialization and each turn. A common wiring layer needs explicit private-wire/alias handling; a common trace layer needs its own guardedness and trace law set. Neither is supplied by `UCSetup`.
7. **Witnesses.** Shared hidden bit and identity are distinct positive squares; seeded feedback is a third, including positive/negative observable outcomes. A delayed initialized register is a candidate stored-state comparison from the parent artifact, not an identified implementation of this IPDL feedback.
8. **Counterexample.** The false-seed cycle is a concrete blocked computation, not a chosen Boolean fixed point. [INFERENCE] Replacing it with a solver that invents a value would change Get from None to Some. Parent structural X5 additionally gives Matt's Boolean fixed-point-selection counterexample where connection order changes an external output: consistent local choice does not suffice for operational gluing invariance.
9. **Security/errors.** Squares are exact distributional resource equalities; the shared example also explicitly quantifies all finite adaptive strategies. They do not define a simulator conversion or establish all target-context/native-context equivalence. No unbounded-loop ε preservation follows: parent X1's repeated Bernoulli test can amplify per-call error to 1, so a quantitative extension needs usage or contraction bounds.
10. **Obligations.** Generalize the paired representation to arbitrary finite typed graphs; prove a joint store invariant, blocking/persistence and complete turn correspondence, identity/tensor/associative flattening, connection-order invariance, and adaptive test closing. If claiming a trace, identify the actual tensor/guard and prove the trace laws rather than renaming seeded stateful linking a trace.

### IP5 — Exact rewriting, computational context axioms and simulation-style security soundness

1. **Construction/status/models.** Native exact/approximate equational proof system and computational soundness; publication/full-report theorem, **not implemented as the whole proof system in the mapped finite Agda interpreter**.
2. **Source.** Publication §3.1 (`:252–279`), Defs. 4.11–4.16, Figs. 10–12, Thm. 4.17 and Lemmas 4.18–4.19 [IP-paper], `:890–899,938–1185`; full report Def. C.1/Lemma C.2 [IP-report], `:2023–2039`, and Thm. C.5.
3. **Signature/laws.** Exact ambient axioms must be sound via a distributional protocol bisimulation: joint convex-combination closure, input-assignment closure, evaluation closure and matching value-or-no-value observations. Approximate theory is a **finite** set of axiom families. A derivation tracks `(k,l)` = axiom count and maximum added context size; transitivity sums k and takes max l, while composition with Q adds `|Q|` to l. Soundness assumes a PPT interpretation and polynomial k(λ), l(λ), interface size. Full-report error is `ε_k,l(p,q)=k·max_i ε_i(p+l,q)` for context budget p and distinguisher budget q.
4. **Repo.** Current `Syntax/Native/SSP/ReadOrder/Linker` provide rational finite semantics and selected exact squares, not arbitrary interpreted symbols, typed context syntax, a rewrite derivation checker, PPT families or Thm. 4.17 as a generic Agda API. `Approx.Rational.hybrid` and generic query-aware error scaling already exist elsewhere, but there is no native instantiation proving these specific IPDL context-cost hypotheses.
5. **Class.** **C** for cost-indexed contextual rewriting over sound native axioms. **D** for unconstrained duplication, unused-read elimination, exponentially many approximate steps, or declaring native bisimulation a UC simulator. No new generic hybrid arithmetic is proposed.
6. **Extra data.** A typed context action with substitution/composition/size equations; exact observation soundness; an error-indexed axiom interface; polynomial resource semantics, symbol interpretation and sampling-realization bounds. A simulator is additional **protocol code** on the attacker interface. QUC's Approx-valued tests do not supply any of those operational/PPT facts.
7. **Witnesses.** The paper's coin-toss/OT/GMW proof style composes native exact equalities and approximate crypto assumptions. Current read-order/shared-bit squares witness a narrower exact part. Generic rational hybrid arithmetic is a reusable arithmetic consumer only after a common approximation carrier is established.
8. **Counterexample.** The paper's `subst` explicitly requires `R₁;R₁` to equal one run copied twice (`:1051–1065`); independent fair sampling violates it. `unused` requires the dropped computation not to introduce a timing/blocking dependency (`:1056–1069`); deleting a read of an absent input can make an unavailable output available. [INFERENCE] Exponentially many steps with per-step error `2^-λ` need not leave negligible total error; polynomial usage is essential.
9. **Security/errors/quantifiers.** Def. 4.11 has `∀ PPT context family C. ∀ PPT distinguisher family A (with bound p). ∃ negligible ε. ∀λ: advantage≤ε(λ)` (with the paper's PPT/interface requirements). It does **not** assert one ε uniform over all C,A. §3.1 formulates realization by choosing **one Sim first**, then proving `π≈obs Sim+Ideal`; the intended simulator must not depend on the later test. Thm. 4.17 soundly turns a derivation into that native indistinguishability when its exact/approximate axioms are sound. It neither automatically constructs Sim nor proves coincidence with categorical-crypto's all-grade/all-ancilla UC order. Exact Lemma 4.18 quantifies arbitrary native A,k and well-typed C with zero error. Reflection through the current interpreter is not established.
10. **Obligations.** Reify native judgments/contexts and the k/l accounting; prove all exact axioms' native bisimulation obligations; represent PPT interpretations and approximate axiom hypotheses; recover the context-size shift in the error bound. For a UC connection, construct typed simulator/interface adapters and a test-closing/backtranslation theorem with ancilla coverage, then check dummy-simulator quantifier order and simulator fullness. Do not collapse vanishing into negligible or local-in-test negligible bounds into uniform bounds.

## 4. CSP rows

### CS1 — Process trees, operational labels, native observation and refinement relations

1. **Construction/status/models.** External pinned CSP semantics plus proposed constructive local specialization; **no checked local CSP setup**. Native behavioral relations are not cryptographic simulators.
2. **Source.** [C-Tree] `:112–167`; [C-LTS] `:20–88`; Hoare §3.9 [H-read]; Roscoe–Barrett §1 and §3, Thm. 3.2 [R-read]. Pin relations: [C-Bisim], [C-Weak], [C-DR], [C-Fail], [C-FD], [C-FSim].
3. **Signature/laws.** `PTree E I R` is coinductive with `ret`, `sil`, and `react(v,τc)`; a react node may offer visible events and internal branches simultaneously. Event families carry typed payloads; τ-branch indexes may be infinite. LTS labels distinguish visible events, τ and successful termination `√ r`. Strong bisimulation matches one step by one step; weak bisimulation uses `τ*` and `τ*·a·τ*`; DR additionally transports actual infinite τ divergence both ways. `_⊑F_` pairs raw trace containment and stable-failure containment. `_⊑FD_` compares divergence-strict failures and divergences, with **left containing right behavior**. `FSim impl spec` combines forward weak simulation, spec τ-settlement to a stable state with `Offers(spec′)⊆Offers(impl)`, and impl-divergence→spec-divergence; `FSim Q P` yields `P⊑FD Q`.
4. **Repo.** All these declarations belong to the external pin, not integrated categorical-crypto. Selected ret/sil/react specialization is in-progress; network LTS, native correspondence, category and test presheaf remain unlanded obligations at this boundary. Source lines `FailureSim.agda:45–65,218–219` give the FSim contract/bridge. `DRImpliesFD.agda:64–176` uses `¬-divergent→normal` for DR→FD. `StrongImpliesDR` matches τ one-for-one; do not attach the classical FD bridge to the strong→DR step itself.
5. **Class.** **C** for observation/refinement constructions over explicit LTS and divergence data; **D** for identifying all these equalities/orders or substituting native FSim for a UC converter.
6. **Extra data.** Typed LTS, selected termination/deadlock/stability/divergence observations, relation lifting/coinduction, and a context closure theorem for the chosen relation. Setoid UC tests compare equality, whereas FD is first a preorder; a refinement-oriented theory requires a separately stated ordered-observation design. Raw traces and divergence-closed denotational traces are different carriers.
7. **Witnesses.** Strong bisim→DR and FSim→FD are positive native bridges. `FSimTower` is a real compositional native refinement example. IPDL's distributional bisimulation is a useful contrast, not the same relation or an established second consumer.
8. **Counterexamples.** Pin [C-Fail] `:234–248` proves `div≈deadlock` under weak bisimulation although stable failures differ. [C-OrderCE] `:192–206` gives `div⊑FD Q` for arbitrary Q but refutes raw trace refinement to `a→Stop`. These refute weak-equality use for divergence/refusal-sensitive observations and FD-to-raw-trace implication, not CSP setup feasibility. PM's distinction between false verdict and no verdict does not by itself decide deadlock-versus-divergence compatibility.
9. **Security/errors.** Native simulation/refinement is about steps and behaviors, with no `∀attack.∃converter.∀test` clause and no numerical error. A CSP-backed setup could choose trace, stable-failure, divergence, may/must or combined observations, but its choice must be explicit. No probability, PPT or QUC instance follows from native nondeterminism.
10. **Obligations.** Define network observations and prove structural invariance/test pullback before `StdUC`; prove native relation soundness for every admitted closing context. If using FD, specify raw versus divergence-closed traces and exact observation loss. For constructive integration, replace or explicitly budget normalization/classical principles, including transitive imports; a body not using a postulate is not a postulate-free import closure.

### CS2 — `Ret`, bind and sequential composition are value sequencing, not interface plugging

1. **Construction/status/models.** Existing pinned process-tree return/bind/sequential laws; local CSP integration not checked.
2. **Source.** Hoare §§5.2–5.3 [H-read]; [C-Op] `:100–107,324–344`; [C-Seq] `:56–135`; [C-SeqAssoc] `:169–233`; [C-BindCong] `:80–119`; [C-BindFD] `:1–57`.
3. **Signature/laws.** `Ret:R→PTree R`; `>>= : PTree R→(R→PTree S)→PTree S`; `P>>Q=P>>=(λ_.Q)`. A ret head immediately enters its continuation; sil and react recursively carry bind. Left unit is definitional; right unit and associativity have strong-bisimulation proofs. General bind congruence inspected here is **weak bisimulation**, pointwise in the continuations. General FD monotonicity needs `BindDivSplit` and inherits `Diverges-LEM`; constant sequencing additionally needs `>>-Diverges→`. Pure/τ-free continuation specializations discharge the split, not the inherited classical assumption.
4. **Repo.** These are external pin APIs. `KTree R S=R→PTree E I S` is a value transformer, not a boundary-polarized interactive converter. No local `StdUC` consumer exists for this pin or for the proposed network carrier.
5. **Class.** **C** for a process/Kleisli category with the specified native equality. **D** for treating bind as cryptographic interactive plugging or `Ret` as a wire identity. [INFERENCE] A unit-grade sequential setup is plausible but not the requested adversarial-interface model.
6. **Extra data.** A result-indexed computation type, return/bind, congruence and monad laws at the selected relation; then suitable tests. Interactive wiring additionally requires typed boundaries, persistent port connections and separate monoidal laws, none of which follows from bind. The bare UC graded monad does not identify its arrows with this native bind.
7. **Witnesses.** Strong unit/associativity and weak bind congruence are positive; IPDL reaction bind is a potential second effect-level comparison, **not** an identity of protocol linking signatures. `Ret` versus a channel relay is the control.
8. **Counterexample.** [INFERENCE] A transparent wire should allow an ongoing external event a to pass; `Ret r` has a termination transition rather than an a offer. An a-observing context distinguishes them. Similarly, a network can link mutually dependent continuing components without either returning a value, while bind cannot start k until the first process returns. This is a signature/behavior obstruction to the proposed identification, not to existence of a distinct category.
9. **Security/errors.** Native laws preserve their stated operational/denotational equivalence. They do not establish test/ancilla closure of a future network quotient or a simulator. No native quantitative bound is supplied; a future probabilistic resolution needs its own scheduler and error semantics.
10. **Obligations.** If building a sequential category, prove equality congruence at the exact chosen level and define tests. If building converters, prove a separate gluing identity/composition square and only then use `StdUC`; do not derive tensor interchange from sequential monad associativity. Audit the FD proof closure separately from constructive strong/weak proof bodies.

### CS3 — Internal versus external choice and their relation-indexed algebra

1. **Construction/status/models.** Native CSP nondeterministic choice; external pin, not local ND grades or probability.
2. **Source.** Hoare §§3.2–3.4 [H-read]; [C-Op] `:129–131,225–317`; [C-IChoiceStrong] `:25–45`, [C-IChoiceWeak] `:22–40`; [C-IChoiceAssoc] `:1–10,161–195`; [C-EChoiceAssoc] `:700–704`; [C-EChoiceIdem] `:191–192`.
3. **Signature/laws.** Internal `P⊓Q` makes a τ choice with no initial visible offer. External `P□Q` merges offers; if both offer the same event its continuation is an internal choice. Live/terminated operands have explicit termination-preserving slide behavior; general result type requires `DecEq R`. Internal choice is strongly commutative and weakly idempotent. Its **associativity is FD**, not strong/weak bisimulation. External idempotence and associativity are supplied at FD. Replicated operators include finite lists/Fin; raw τ maps can nevertheless have unbounded fan-out.
4. **Repo.** Pinned operator/theorem modules above are source-present. Current categorical-crypto ND.A/ND.B are grade regradings with separate operational PM/SSP choices, not a consumer proving these native CSP semilattice laws. No local CSP choice API is stable.
5. **Class.** **C** for relation-indexed choice algebra; **D** for one universal choice operator or lifting all denotational laws to strong/weak bisimulation.
6. **Extra data.** LTS/internal-choice resolution, visible-offer algebra, termination behavior, selected observational quotient, and explicit congruence/distributivity laws. Probability would require weights and a distinct semantics; QUC's observation enrichment and ND relabeling do not add those data.
7. **Witnesses.** `⊓-comm` and `⊓-idem` demonstrate distinct valid levels; FD union-reassociation proves internal associativity. The finite `FSimTower` leaf refinement `(P⊓Q)⊑FD P` is a real native consumer. Weighted rational sampling is a nonexample of unweighted CSP choice.
8. **Counterexamples.** [INFERENCE from LTS] `Stop⊓Stop` has τ moves and Stop has none, so internal idempotence is not strong. For distinct initial events a,b,c, `(a→Stop⊓b→Stop)⊓c→Stop` can commit to the a/b-only choice; the opposite association has no weak-bisimilar residual with exactly that branching commitment (matching source explanation [C-IChoiceAssoc]:3–7). An external a/b choice initially allows the environment to select a; an internal choice may select the b-only branch and refuse a, distinguishing stable failures. Assigning “uniform probability” to both silently changes the theory.
9. **Security/errors.** FD choice refinement quantifies behavior membership/refusals/divergences, not a simulator uniform over tests. Native nondeterminism has no statistical error or PPT interpretation. Exact contextual replacement requires the chosen relation's congruence for the actual context fragment.
10. **Obligations.** Specify the chooser/information boundary, return semantics, and whether finite or unbounded branching is admitted. Prove each semilattice/distributivity law at the intended relation, and prove context closure rather than promoting FD laws to bisimulation. A scheduler/grade encoding requires a representation/test theorem before reuse of ND generic APIs.

### CS4 — Synchronizing parallel, interleaving, alphabet confinement and typed wiring

1. **Construction/status/models.** Native `Par`/interleaving and a possible common finite typed wiring structure with IPDL. External laws exist; local constructive open-network/category/native-correspondence work is in progress and unchecked.
2. **Source.** Hoare §§2.2–2.3 and §3.4 [H-read]; [C-Op] `:529–640`; [C-Offers] `:81–109`; [C-DRCong] `:406` onward for `Sep`; [C-Interchange] `:478–503`; [C-InterchangeCE] `:5–38,201–204`; [C-ParAssoc] `:1–15`.
3. **Signature/laws.** `Par A merge : PTree R₁→PTree R₂→PTree R`; synchronize events in A, interleave outside A, merge values on joint termination. If both operands offer the same non-sync event, a visible step leads to an internal choice of which component advanced; this freezes a commitment before later τs. `P⦀Q=Par ∅ES (λ_ _.tt) P Q`. Actual interchange is `(P₁⦀P₂)∥A(Q₁⦀Q₂) ∼ (P₁∥A Q₁)⦀(P₂∥A Q₂)` given four persistent `OffersOnly` witnesses, disjoint component alphabets and two `Sep` witnesses. `Sep` rules out simultaneous same-event offers outside A and is preserved under reachable steps. Ordinary parallel associativity is proved FD-direct, not by a general strong/weak associativity law.
4. **Repo.** Above are pinned native contracts. The recommended local carrier uses typed open networks, disjoint-union tensor, fresh private wiring, flattened plugging and identity **aliases**, not `Ret`; names in that construction are proposals, not checked landing. The coordinator's no-landing boundary remains authoritative.
5. **Class.** **C** for disciplined typed wiring/confinement; **D** for unrestricted global-name parallel interchange. Removing `Sep` at FD interchange while retaining other premises is **E** in the inspected source, not refuted by the separate alphabet counterexample.
6. **Extra data.** Typed port/payload/polarity interfaces, private namespaces, explicit rendezvous participant discipline, persistent confinement, disjoint union, alias equivalence and which wire classes remain external. Prove `OffersOnly`, `Disj`, `Sep` from that discipline where native Par theorems are used. [INFERENCE] A network LTS and structural execution invariance can establish category equations without taking arbitrary process labels modulo an unproved native congruence.
7. **Witnesses.** Pinned restricted interchange and the disjoint-leaf interleaving in `FSimTower` are native positives. IPDL paired/flat networks are a candidate second source consumer of typed boundary/gluing data, but not of the same rendezvous semantics. Shared global event names are the negative control.
8. **Counterexample.** [C-InterchangeCE] chooses P₁ and Q₂ offering h, P₂ and Q₁ stopped, and synchronization set `{h}`. Left regrouping admits a cross-component h synchronization; right regrouping does not. This already separates a one-event trace and refutes strong interchange. It rules out unrestricted shared-name regrouping, **not** all CSP categorification and not removal of `Sep` alone.
9. **Security/errors.** Native interchange can justify a restricted semantic wiring equation; it has no simulator quantifier. A future test presheaf can compare closed network traces/failures/divergence, but must be proved invariant under wiring equality. General nondeterminism has no distribution/error until an explicit resolution model is supplied.
10. **Obligations.** Prove well-typed gluing, participant/confinement closure, transparent aliases, alpha invariance, associative flattening and tensor interchange on execution states. Prove observations and pullback invariant; then instantiate Standard2 and contextual quotient. Prove native Par/rename/hide correspondence only on its admissible fragment, and a test-closing equation for each exposed adversary/ancilla interface. Simulator fullness is separate.

### CS5 — Hiding, divergence, unbounded internal branching and witness-preserving refinement

1. **Construction/status/models.** CSP hiding and exact scope of FD/FSim/DR closure; central native obstruction, not an argument against every CSP setup.
2. **Source.** Hoare §§3.5,3.8 [H-read]; Roscoe–Barrett §1, especially printed p.167 (hiding of `(F,D,I)`) and Thm. 1.2 [R-read]; [C-Op] `:445–527`; [C-HideFD] `:11–40,226–288`; [C-HideFinBr]; [C-HideFSim] `:63–98,309–310`; [C-HideFSimCE] `:1–48,237–265`; [C-Tower] `:19–44`.
3. **Signature/laws.** `P∖A` turns hidden visible offers into τ branches while retaining original τs and surviving visible events; A has decidable membership, not necessarily finite cardinality. Pin hiding is unconditionally monotone for `_⊑F_` (traces plus stable failures) at `HideMonoFD:269–270`. FD theorem `Hide-mono-⊑FD-df:279–282` additionally requires **Q∖A divergence-free**, not merely Q divergence-free. `Hide-mono-fail:226–228` transfers a stable hidden failure into divergence-strict failure; it is not unconditional `⊇F⊥` monotonicity. Operational `Hide-fsim` has no process side condition, but its divergence proof uses inherited `Diverges-LEM` and `¬DivModA→MAcc`. DR hiding uses postulated `modA-transfer` [C-DRCong]:254–296.
4. **Repo.** External pin only; no unchecked local API is claimed. `HideCounterexample` formally proves `¬(FSim Qh Pinf)` and finite-chain positive FSims. Its header explicitly says the denotational FD half of the counterexample is argued mathematically, **not formalized in that module**. `FSimTower` composes leaf FSims, confined interleaving and hiding, then converts once to FD; it inherits the two hiding principles and `Par-Diverges→`.
5. **Class.** **C** for hiding over suitably richer behavior/witness structure or the stated divergence-free fragment. **D** for unrestricted FD hiding monotonicity in the admitted raw-tree model and for repairing it with the existing visible-support `FinBr` certificate.
6. **Extra data.** Actual hidden-event/τ path semantics and stability; either retained operational simulation witnesses with settlement/divergence transport, a sufficient admissibility/branching/accessibility discipline closed under hiding, or a richer denotation retaining infinite traces. A genuine finite-branching compactness condition must control **internal** branching; a finite visible alphabet is not it. For constructive use provide positive settlement/accessibility or a justified classical assumption budget.
7. **Witnesses.** `Hide-mono-⊑F`, post-hide-divergence-free FD monotonicity, and `FSimTower` are positive alternatives with different contracts. Roscoe–Barrett's `(F,D,I)` model is another positive semantics for general hiding, not the pin's finite-trace FD quotient. IPDL private persistent cells are not CSP hiding's infinite-τ behavior.
8. **Counterexample.** Let Q=`μX.h→X`; P=`⊓ₙ(hⁿ;STOP)` with one infinitely τ-branching head. P contains Q's stable failures; neither initially diverges, so P⊑FD Q. Hiding h makes Q diverge, while every chosen branch of P remains finite; hence `(P∖{h})⊑FD(Q∖{h})` fails. The source proves `FinBr Pinf` despite that infinite internal fan-out. `FSim Q P` is impossible because the specification must commit to one finite chain after matching h; its remaining budget decreases while Q repeats forever. Equal finite trace sets do not discharge this per-state obligation. Roscoe–Barrett explains why adding infinite traces distinguishes precisely such cases; its monotonicity theorem is therefore not contradicted.
9. **Security/errors.** Native hiding refinement/equality does not imply cryptographic preservation without closing-test coverage. Hiding can introduce divergence/absence of verdict, so it is not merely forgetting event labels in all observation models. No numeric error semantics is native here; bounded observation or quantitative loops would require separate usage/summability proofs. An FD fact alone cannot be substituted for an FSim witness in the tower.
10. **Obligations.** Choose and prove the exact post-hide divergence invariant or enrich semantics; prove path/stable-refusal transport; retain operational witnesses when needed. Audit direct and inherited classical assumptions. For a common linker, distinguish hidden rendezvous from store-private channels and prove a closed-test equation; do not use test coarsening to conceal a broken identity/connection law.

### CS6 — Renaming versus alpha-renaming, with a no-fan-in congruence boundary

1. **Construction/status/models.** Native event relabeling and the restricted DR-congruent wrapper; not dynamic fresh-name generation.
2. **Source.** Hoare §2.6/§2.6.1 [H-read] uses a one-to-one alphabet map. [C-Rename] `:12–17,20–36,89–125`; [C-DRCong] `:1215–1265` and renaming section give the pin's exact broader/restricted distinction.
3. **Signature/laws.** General relational renaming takes a relation on concrete typed events and a **finite list of source preimages per target**. Enabled multiple preimages create a post-event internal-choice node; none gives no offer and a singleton gives its continuation directly. Internal branch retagging separately uses an event injection ι with partial left inverse. The `renameInv`/`renameMap` DR congruence has at most one preimage per target, so it creates no fan-in choice. The inverse selector may map several target events to the same source event (fan-out) and may be partial; this is different from saying an arbitrary forward noninjective renaming is safe.
4. **Repo.** Pinned `CSP/Rename.agda` and DRCongruence renaming section are present. The restricted congruence's τ/divergence correspondence is constructive one-for-one. The containing DRCongruence module still has the unrelated hiding postulate in its import closure. Local CSP names/freshening compiler is not checked.
5. **Class.** **C** for typed rename structures and the established no-fan-in DR congruence. **E** for unrestricted relational fan-in DR congruence, and for a proposed stronger separation theorem: the inspected scope note does not provide an explicit DR-equivalent P,Q and renaming that separates them. **D** only for treating arbitrary behavior-changing channel identification as harmless alpha-renaming (the concrete witness is in field 8).
6. **Extra data.** Typed alphabets, injection/left inverse for internal indexing, actual preimage enumerator with its semantic adequacy, and a no-fan-in or enabled-preimage separation invariant. Fresh-name binding additionally needs alpha equivalence and freshness, not provided by an event rename function. Bare UC grade regrading does not implement these operational event changes.
7. **Witnesses.** Injective Hoare symbol change and the pin's at-most-one-preimage wrapper are positives. IPDL injective context embeddings are a related naming candidate; its arbitrary `Ren` is not automatically one. Mapping distinct enabled sources to one target is the nonexample.
8. **Obstruction/scope.** Multiple enabled source events mapped to one target introduce a new internal-choice/frozen post-event node. The source scope note argues that trailing τ matching can then fail, but this audit did not identify a concrete DR-equivalent P,Q witnessing failure of renamed DR congruence; that exact claim remains **E**, not a proved no-go theorem. A separate concrete **D** witness is [INFERENCE from the inspected operators]: let `P=(a→Stop)□(b→Skip)` for distinct inhabited visible events a,b, and rename both to a. Originally the trace `a,√tt` is impossible; after renaming, a leads to `Stop⊓Skip`, so `a,√tt` is possible. This shows that identifying visible channels is not harmless alpha-renaming; it does **not** disprove congruence between two processes renamed by the same map. IPDL's merging of independently supplied persistent input channels likewise changes correlation by a different mechanism (IP3).
9. **Security/errors.** Restricted native DR congruence preserves native behavior; it does not ensure the renaming is full on adversary grades or tests. A target event/attack not representable at source prevents automatic reflection. No statistical/PPT effect is claimed.
10. **Obligations.** Prove rename identity/composition, event/preimage completeness, internal index transport and native observation compatibility. For general fan-in, first establish congruence or give an explicit DR-equivalent source pair and separating renamed behavior; only then state necessary/sufficient restrictions or prove a conditioned closure theorem. For nominal network use prove capture-avoiding freshening and boundary preservation. Separately prove source/target context and simulator representability before calling regrading/transport APIs.

### CS7 — Guarded recursion, productive iteration and the fixed-point boundary

1. **Construction/status/models.** Native recursive processes and pinned productive `iter/loop/while`; not an unrestricted trace on a UC setup.
2. **Source.** Hoare §§1.1.2,1.3 L2,2.8 and 3.8 [H-read]; Roscoe–Barrett Thm. 2.1 and Thm. 3.6 [R-read]; [C-Op] `:1024–1078`; [C-IterCong]; [C-IterFD] `:32–65,442–502`; [C-LoopFD] `:1207–1209`. Structural trace/initialized-feedback distinctions are separately in parent X1/X2/X5.
3. **Signature/laws.** `iter : (A→PTree(A⊎R))→A→PTree R`; an `inj₁ a′` return goes through `sil(iter k a′)`, an `inj₂ r` terminates. `loop` threads a state forever via iter; current `while` executes the body and tests its returned state. These are productive coinductive constructions. Hoare's visible-guarded recursive equations have unique solutions; hiding can remove guards and introduces a least-solution/divergence interpretation. Roscoe–Barrett's richer unbounded model proves least fixed points for **CSP-definable functions**, not arbitrary functions on an arbitrary incomplete order.
4. **Repo.** Actual pinned `Operators` back-edge is sil-guarded; no `TERMINATING/NON_TERMINATING` pragma was found in the inspected operator declarations. `IterCong` supplies pointwise weak-bisimilar body→weak-bisimilar iteration. Separately, `IterMonoFD.iter-mono-⊑FD:442–446` maps pointwise body FD refinement to iter FD refinement with **A,R at the same universe level**; stateful loop and same-condition while follow at 461–487. These are genuine FD-fact precongruences, not merely FSim→FD wrappers. Their divergence half uses `iter-div-split`, derived from inherited `Diverges-LEM` and `¬DivModA→MAcc`; pure continuation binding for loop/while adds no new principle beyond `Diverges-LEM`. The older direct `IterateMonoFD.loop0-mono-⊑FD:1207–1209`, re-exported for definitionally identical `loopc`, instead inherits the loop0-specific `loop-Diverges→` postulate. None of those stronger laws is inferred from weak congruence. Nothing here has landed as a local trace/setup API.
5. **Class.** **C** for a specified guarded iteration or suitable domain-theoretic fixed-point structure. **D** for universal unguarded fixed points/trace derived from UC axioms or for replacing native divergence by an arbitrary chosen solution.
6. **Extra data.** Guarded/corecursive step semantics and chosen equality; or an ordered/domain model with the actual definable-function/fixed-point hypotheses. A trace needs its own typed tensor/guard, vanishing/sliding/yanking laws. Stored-state feedback separately needs an initializer. A numerical error presheaf is not contraction or completeness.
7. **Witnesses.** Sil-guarded iter and visible `μX.h→X` are positive native constructions. IPDL true/false seeded cycles are a comparison showing persistent-store progress/blocking rather than general productive process recursion. Parent one-step delayed register is a stored-state control, not instantaneous trace.
8. **Counterexamples.** Hoare explicitly notes unguarded `X=X` has many solutions and hiding can make recursive equations unguarded. [INFERENCE] Total cartesian Boolean fixed points would require a fixed point of negation, which does not exist. Roscoe–Barrett shows unbounded semantics need not be a complete order and some operators are not continuous; ordinary ω iteration cannot simply be presumed to yield the right least fixed point.
9. **Security/errors.** Native weak iteration congruence preserves weak behavior only. Repeated use can amplify an ε difference to 1 under eventual-success observation; finite n use gives `1−(1−ε)^n≤nε` in the probabilistic control from parent X1. Thus future QUC reuse needs bounded-use/query or contractive summability data and correct simulator-cost scaling; CSP's native unweighted τ-choice supplies none.
10. **Obligations.** State which recursion fragment/equality is intended; prove productivity and native unfolding/congruence; discharge divergence-splitting/classical principles for stronger FD results. For wiring feedback establish operational connection-order laws independently. For UC, reify admissible feedback contexts as tests and prove closing/pullback/ancilla equations plus any use bound; prove reflection separately.

### CS8 — Relation coinduction, expansion and sound up-to techniques

1. **Construction/status/models.** Native direct guarded relation-coinduction/expansion APIs exist at the external pin. A generic CSP up-to-context/companion package was **not established** by this audit or the prior pin assessment.
2. **Source.** [C-RelCoind] `:29–65,71–81,96–124`; [C-Expansion] `:50–76,170` onward. Supplemental author formalization [U-code] v1.6: complete lattice/monotone b at `:16–22`, compatible functions/companion at `:24–54`, `enhanced_gfp` and `coinduction` at `:163–169`, associated with [U-paper].
3. **Signature/laws.** `DRFromRel` consumes a state relation, forward/backward visible and τ weak-step matching, plus **nondivergence of both related sides**, and returns DR bisimulation. `WSimFromRel` needs only the forward matching. `FSimFromRel` adds stable spec settlement/offer inclusion and **nondivergence of the implementation**, no backward matching; a variant with explicit divergence transport is suggested by the source but not supplied there. Expansion is asymmetric: the faster side's steps are matched weakly by the slower; the slower's visible step is matched strongly and τ by zero/one step, with divergence transport both ways; it yields DR. Abstract up-to: on a complete lattice with monotone b, a monotone closure f satisfying `f∘b≤b∘f` is compatible; the companion t is the greatest compatible map, and `R≤b(t R)` implies `R≤νb`.
4. **Repo.** Native `rel→dr`, `rel→wsim`, `rel→fsim`, `⪰→≈DR` are source-present; relation coinduction is parameterized in E,I and does not import the DR→FD normalization bridge. The up-to theorem is the author's **external Coq** source, not a categorical-crypto or CSP pin API. No theorem is inferred from absence of an UpTo-named file.
5. **Class.** **C** for coinduction/up-to over a specified behavioral operator and compatible closure; **E** for an actual CSP up-to-context library/instantiation at this pin; **D** for arbitrary closure or arbitrary native-equivalence substitution as a sound up-to technique.
6. **Extra data.** Complete relation lattice/monotone progression, chosen strong/weak/DR/FSim functional, and a proof of compatibility (or another precisely sound up-to criterion). Native context closure must include the operators' Sep/no-fan-in/divergence assumptions. A setup's observation kernel supplies neither the step functional nor compatible closure.
7. **Witnesses.** Pinned direct relation coinduction and expansion→DR are positives. The author formalization is a second proof-theory consumer, not a second constructed cryptographic setup. IPDL convex distributional bisimulation is a candidate only after its own progression/closure operator is defined and proved compatible.
8. **Counterexample.** [INFERENCE] Monotonicity alone is insufficient: choose f constantly the universal relation, and R containing `(a→b→STOP, a→c→STOP)` for distinct b,c. Initial a steps match with residuals in f(R), so the one-step “up-to” condition holds, but b/c behavior separates the roots. Hence compatibility/soundness cannot be omitted. Also weak `div≈deadlock` cannot be used up-to when the goal retains divergence/refusals.
9. **Security/errors.** Coinductive relation proofs justify native behavior facts; they are not executable simulator converters. To affect UC they must first imply equality of every admitted closed experiment, preserving ancillas and simulator order. Approximate coinduction would need a quantitative relation lifting and accumulation bound; the exact native APIs supply none.
10. **Obligations.** Define the intended behavioral progression and test its monotonicity; prove the proposed up-to closure compatible and check the chosen equivalence/observation relation, not a weaker one. Incorporate operator side conditions and prove context closure. Then prove native-to-test soundness; only thereafter use the existing setup-kernel/UC transfer APIs. For constructivity audit relation-coinduction proof bodies separately from stronger downstream FD/import assumptions.

## 6. Assumption audit and exact next proof package

The highest-value reusable package is **typed wiring plus native execution/test-closing theorems**, not a new universal UC theorem or a superclass called “trace/choice/state.” It must preserve the distinctions below.

- **IPDL:** full source has nominal binders/injective embeddings, arbitrary PPT-interpreted function/distribution symbols and k/l context accounting. Current finite typed rational interpreter has persistent Input/Get/Step, before-quiescence Get, finite adaptive strategies and concrete linker squares. The missing work is a general typed/hygienic compiler and its correspondence/context/security coverage, not a replacement interpreter or proof that any linking exists.
- **CSP:** raw ret/sil/react syntax includes simultaneous offers/τs and unbounded internal branching. Strong, weak, DR, FD, FSim and expansion each have different laws. Hoare's denotational algebra cannot be copied wholesale into strong/weak equality. `Sep` and `OffersOnly` are persistent operational properties, not synonyms for fresh names.
- **Classical assumptions:** DR→FD uses `¬-divergent→normal`; DR hiding uses `modA-transfer`; FD bind requires its splitting/classical premises; general FD parallel associativity inherits `offer-LEM` and `Par-Diverges→`; FSim hiding has no process restriction but inherits `Diverges-LEM` and `¬DivModA→MAcc`. `ClassicalFromLEM` is certification-only, not an axiom-free replacement in the proof import closure. Constructive positive cores and imported closure must be reported separately.
- **Hiding:** the unbounded FD counterexample is a failure of one preorder-monotonicity claim. Stable-failures/trace monotonicity, FSim witnesses, the post-hide-divergence-free fragment and infinite-trace denotation are meaningful alternatives. Do not overstate it as impossibility of every quotient/network model.
- **UC:** after a lawful native carrier and test presheaf exist, reuse Standard2, the two-sided contextual quotient and actual transfer APIs. The needed test equation must preserve all relevant ancillary closures; target `GradeStable` is not universally true and is not a default shortcut. Reflection additionally requires representing target simulators. Native bisimulation/FSim is never the simulator itself.
- **Quantitative/availability:** IPDL approximate security uses polynomial contexts/axiom usage and negligible errors locally quantified after tests; no current IPDL/CSP QUC node is present. Optional output/blocking must be preserved, but that alone is not the generic RS availability construction with designated honest caps and a separate availability error. Generic fixed-error QUC nonexpansiveness does not justify arbitrary repeated/hiding/feedback use.

**Exact implementation-facing obligations (research output, not edits requested):**

1. Represent finite typed open wiring with explicit owner/rendezvous rules, private classes and aliases; prove admissibility, freshness and flattening invariance.
2. Prove native identity, composition, tensor and connection-order execution squares, including blocked output and divergence—not merely free-syntax equations.
3. Prove test closing/pullback for every admitted context and characterize compiler/test image and ancillary coverage.
4. Select relation-indexed native replacement rules; discharge Sep/OffersOnly/no-fan-in or divergence/settlement assumptions structurally. Do not replace retained FSim evidence by an FD fact when hiding needs it.
5. Construct the setup/graded interpretation only from those proven data. For security preservation use the actual generic route whose premises hold; for reflection prove grade/simulator representation separately.
6. For computational/quantitative claims add explicit PPT encodings, usage/cost interpretation, resource-aware error scaling and the precise negligible/uniformity quantifiers. Do not infer probability from QUC or fairness from nondeterminism.

No B-only native construction was established: the new native content requires operational structure beyond arbitrary UCSetup/QUCSetup. The substantive results are C constructions with concrete D boundaries; E is confined to the exact unestablished extensions stated above, not used as a substitute for the completed positive/negative analysis.

## Embedded Roscoe–Barrett primary-text extraction

```text
Source: https://web.archive.org/web/20240427181422if_/https://citeseerx.ist.psu.edu/document?repid=rep1&type=pdf&doi=e438c8e6164614b56e7545483862b709c614b527
Primary text: A. W. Roscoe and Geoff Barrett, Unbounded Nondeterminism in CSP. Text extraction only; PDF page markers retained.

=== PDF page 1 ===
Unbounded Nondeterminism in CSP 
by A.W. Roscoe 1 and Geoff Barrett 2 
Oxford University Computing Laboratory 
8-11 Keble Road 
Oxford OX1 3QD 
ABSTRACT. We extend the failures/divergences model for CSP to include a 
component of infinite traces. This allows us to give a denotational semantics 
for a version of CSP including general nondeterministic choice and infinite 
hiding. Unfortunately the model is an incomplete partial order, so it is by no 
means obvious that the necessary fixed points exist. We have two proofs of this 
result, one via a congruence theorem with operational semantics and one via 
a careful analysis of operators' behaviour on a subset of the model. 
0. Introduction 
As is well known to the theoretical community, it is generally far easier to model finite 
nondetermJnism (where a process can only choose between finitely many options at 
any one time) than unbounded nondeterminism (where no such restriction applies). 
The difficulties encountered with unbounded nondeterminism have hitherto forced 
us to restrict the language and semantics of CSP to avoid it: the most obvious 
restrictions being our inability to define the hiding operator P\B when B is infinite 
and the absence of an infinite nondeterminism operator ['] S for arbitrary nonempty 
sets S of processes. 
In an earlier paper [R2] one of us showed how many of the restrictions on un- 
bounded nondeterminism could be lifted by separating the nondeterminism order 
from the order used for finding fixed points. Unfortunately the structure of the 
model used there (failures and divergences using only finite traces) means that the 
semantics given by that model to unboundedly nondeterministic operators is not suf- 
ficiently discriminating. That model can successfully model a process which will, on 
its first step, nondeterministically choose any integer, but cannot tell between a pro- 
cess which can communicate any finite number of a's and one which may also choose 
to communicate an infinite number. The main purpose of this paper is to develop a 
1A.W. Roscoe gratefully acknowledges support from ONR grant N00014-87-G-0242. 
2Geoff Barrett's current address: inmos Ltd., 1000 Aztec West, Almondsbury, Bristol BS12 4SQ 


=== PDF page 2 ===
161 
more refined model which can make this sort of distinction. This is done by adding 
a component of infinite traces so that any CSP process is represented by (F, D, I) 
where F is its set of failures (still with finite traces), D is its set of (finite) divergence 
traces and I is the set of infinite traces it can communicate. 
Unfortunately the obvious orders on this new model fail to be complete, though 
they do have greatest lower bounds for arbitrary nonempty sets, which means that 
the standard iterative technique will produce the least fixed point of monotone f 
provided there is any x with f(x) _< x. The first reaction to this failure was to 
look for a new order coarser than the obvious one which was complete (for this was 
precisely what h~d been done in the paper mentioned above for the IF, D) model 
without the finite subsets axiom). However one can prove that no order which gives 
the right semantics can be complete. Spedfically we find an w-sequence of CSP- 
definable processes whose semantic values are provably ordered in any sensible order 
but which can have no least upper bound. 
If recursions are well defined we must therefore find some spedal property of 
CSP-definable functions which leads them to have fixed points. We have found two 
methods for proving the existence of these fixed points. The first was to define an 
operational semantics for the language and to prove simultaneously that the fixed 
points exist and that the denotational semantics is congruent to the operational one 
via the natural abstraction map. Barrett's contribution to this paper was to find a 
much shorter proof 3 which is also more satisfactory in some ways because it rests 
entirely within the model itself, rather than going outside to operational semantics. 
The rest of the paper is structured as follows. In the next section we develop the 
new model, discover its partial order properties, and show how to define the CSP 
operators over it. The second section gives Barrett's proof that all CSP definable 
functions have fixed points. Because the semantics contains a number of features 
which are difficult or unusual, it is even more important than usual to have evidence 
that they are 'right'. For example many of the operators definable turn out to be 
non-continuous (though monotone) and require iteration past w to reach their fixed 
points - and on first inspection it is not obvious whether the meaning of a recursion 
I~P.F(P) should be the wth iterate I I{F"(±) ] n E N} or the least fixed point (the 
latter is the right answer). Therefore in the final section we outline the proof of the 
congruence theorem which was formerly used to prove the existence of fixed points, 
but now stands in its own right. 
The way this congruence theorem can be used to establish the existence of fixed 
points is explained in an earlier presentation of the new model JR3], which also gives 
the proofs of various results on operational semantics that have been omitted from 
this article due to lack of space. 
3The new proof was discovered in May 1989, shortly after the Tulane workshop. 


=== PDF page 3 ===
~62 
1. Adding infinite traces to the failures model 
The failures/divergences model, developed in [R1,B,BHR,BR], has become the stan- 
dam abstract model for CSP. CSP is based on atomic, handshaken communications 
drawn from an alphabet E, which may be finite or infinite. The model describes every 
process as a pair (F, D}, where F C E* × P(E) is the (nonempty) set of a process' 
failures ((s, X) E F is failure of the process if it can perform the trace s and then 
refuse to accept any communication from the set X) and D C C_ E* is the set of its 
divergences (traces on which the process can loop - perform an infinite sequence of 
internal actions). The usual version of this model - often called H - is defined by a 
number of axioms, (1)-(5) below plus an axiom of bounded nondeterminism 
vY _c ~ X.(s, Y) ~ F ~ (s, X) ~ e. 
This axiom is necessary to make the nondeterminism partial order 
{F,D) ~_ (F',D') vv F D_ F' A D D_ D' 
complete. However, in [R2], a stronger order (in that it orders less things) was devel- 
oped, which gives exactly the same fixed point theory but which no longer requires 
this axiom for completeness. This new 'definedness' order is defined 
P -< Q ¢~ ~[[QI c 9~P] A 
s ¢ :D[P~ ~ Tt~P]s = 7t~Q]s h

where #T denotes the minimal elements of a set T of finite traces and 7~[P~s denotes 
{X I (s, X) e J:Mi. P <- Q means that Q has less divergences than P, but that 
all of P's non-divergent behaviour is copied exactly in Q. This extended model was

Our new model will have the same structure as AP except that it will have an extra

where F C_ E* × 7~(E), D C E* and I C E~. F should be nonempty and the eight

(1) (st, O) E F =~ (s,O) E F 
(2) (t, x) e F ^ Y c x ~ (t, Y) e F 
(3) (t,X) EFAVaEY.(t(a),0)¢F ~ (t, XUY) EF

Axioms (6) and (7) are both new but straightforward because they are simple 
extensions to axioms (1) and (4) respectively. One more axiom is required, which 
can be thought of as an infinite trace analogue to axiom (3). The latter says that 
anything which, on one step, cannot be refused, must be a possible communication. 
The new axiom will say that when one, from the finite convergent behaviour, can 
show that there must be infinite traces, then there are enough of them. 
One can often prove from the failures of a nondivergent process that some infinite 
trace is possible because one can formulate a strategy for forcing one. The most 
simple-minded form of strategy is that based on a single infinite trace u. If (s, {a}) ¢ 
F for all s(a) < u then it is intuitively clear that a user single mindedly striving for 
the infinite trace u must be successful. However there are more subtle versions of 
this. Consider a process whose failure-set is 
F0 = {(s,X) ] s e {a,b}* A {a,b} ~ X}. 
Imagine always offering this process the set {a,b}: it is never refused, so we can 
guarantee that an infinite trace must arise. However we have no finer control over 
exactly which infinite trace it is, though on further reflection we can observe that, 
since every finite sequence s of a's and bs is possible there must be an infinite trace su 
extending every such s. The necessity of some axiom reflecting the forcing of infinite 
traces is demonstrated by the definition of the hiding operator below. Studying this 
will reveal that if a process P with the above failures did not have an infinite trace, 
then P\{a, b} would not have any failures, divergences or infinite traces! 
The final axiom proved quite hard to find - several quite plausible versions turned 
out to be incorrect. There are a number of equivalent (in the presence of the other 
7) versions of axiom (8). Several are given in [R3] and [Blaml. Perhaps the nicest 
formulation is the following, which was derived from the first author's earlier version

(8) (s,O) e F =~ 3T.(Vt e T.(st,{a [ t(a)  T}) e FA {su I u E 7} C_ I) 
This can be interpreted as saying that one can never tell on a single interaction that 
a process is not deterministic, unless it diverges. T represents one of the deterministic 
forms the process might take after the trace 8. It is worth noting that this axiom in 
no way restricts the sets of failures that are possible - if (F, D) is any element of Af' 
and T = {s [ (s, 0) E F} then (F, D, 7 / is always in H. Furthermore, if (F, D, I) E H 
and I C I' C T, then (F, D, I') E H. 
The reader might like to check that the elements of/4 with failure set F0 as defined 
above are precisely (F0, 0, I) where I is a set of nonempty infinite traces such that

Lemma 1.1 For all P E L/, 
P=[~imp(P), where imp(P) = {Q E 7 ) ] P E Q}. 
Proof. The fact that imp(P) is nonempty is a trivial consequence of axiom (8), since

The first author's first reaction on finding that the two "natural" partial orders 
were incomplete was to try to find another one that was but which gave the same 
semantics. After all, that had been one of the main reasons for the development of 
the < order over Af' since it gave exactly the same least fixed point semantics but 
was complete, showing that all desired fixed points actually exist. We should perhaps 
remark at this point that the given orders do actually compute the correct values for 
CSP definable recursions and that the least upper bounds required to compute them 
always exist. Of course the proof of these facts will be the subject of much work later, 
but it is worthwhile seeing some examples here. 
Examples. Abbreviate by a ~° the process that performs n a's and then STOPs. Set 
P = ["[{ an I n E N}, so that P can perform any finite number of a's but not an infinite 
sequence of them. Operationally we can think of P as a process which, as its first 
action, takes a secret decision on exactly how many a's to perform. Now consider the 
recursively defined process 
Q = (a -+ Q){a}il~o}P 
and let F : b/-+ L( be the function associated with the right hand side of this recursion. 
Since the right hand side of the highest level parallel construct initially imposes a 
bound on the number of a's Q can perform, it is clear that Q itself cannot perform an 
infinite sequence of them. On the other hand it is clear that Q can perform as large 
a finite number of a's as it pleases. We would therefore expect P = Q. However, as 
is easily verified, F~(_L) can perform an infinite sequence of a's (it is equal to P M R, 
where R = a -+ R). On the other hand, F~+I(A_) = (a -+ (P M R)){a)II{~)P = P and 
F(P) = P, so this recursion reaches the operationally correct fixed point at w + 1. 
Some more examples of recursions, their fixed points and the ordinal required to reach 
them are summarised below. The reader might enjoy constructing a few of his own.

3. Operational semantics 
In this section we present an operational semantics for CSP with unbounded non- 
determinism, and summarise the main details of the congruence proof referred to in 
the introduction. But first we will define the abstraction functions from transition 
systems to/4 that will play a crucial role in the statement and proof of this theorem. 
The proofs of all but a very few results are omitted from this presentation - readers 
wishing more details should consult [R3]. 
Summary of notation, nomenclature and results. A transition system is a set 
of states with a binary relation 8 ~ for each element 6 of the set E + = E U {r} of 
transitions, where r denotes an internal transition. We should note that E (the set 
of visible actions) is an implicit parameter of almost everything we do from now on, 
as indeed it was in the last section. 
A movphism [R1,R4] is a function from one transition system to another which 
characterises the property of indistinguishability in that no experimenter who can 
only see transitions (visible or invisible) should be able to tell P from F(P) if F is a 
morphism. F : (7 ~ D is said to be a morphism if and only if:

internal actions in exactly the same rigid way that they treat visible ones, and that 
they are functions rather than relations.

This result shows the equivalence of the natural operationally defined abstraction 
function and one which it obtained by iterating a CSP definition through the ordinals. 
This is exactly what we shall want to do on a much wider scale when we seek to prove

perhaps the most important component of the proof of that theorem. 
The operational semantics 
This section is devoted to the definition of the operational semantics for CSP and 
closely related semantics over more general transition systems. 
A crucial starting point for the creation of any semantics is the definition of the 
programming language. The definition we take is just the usual core CSP extended 
by unbounded nondeterminism and infinite hiding. For formal reasons we must fix ab 
initio the range of unbounded nondeterminism allowed. However this may be as large 
as we please. In particular, it is convenient to fix it strictly larger than the cardinality 
of the alphabet }]. Thus the following language is implicitly parameterised both by 
the alphabet E of all possible communications and by the bound ~, an infinite regular 
cardinal on the unbounded nondeterminism. 
Because the unbounded nondeterminism operator (unavoidably) and the guarded 
choice operator (avoidably at a price) are infinitary operators (take a potentially 
infinite number of process arguments) one should, for rigour, be rather careful over 
the definition of the syntax of this version of CSP. On the one hand we can write 
down the usual sort of BNF definition.

When there are infinitary operators in a syntax, like those in this language, the 
idea of what is defined by a syntax like this one is less obvious than it usually is 
and should therefore be discussed briefly. If we are to have a principle of structural 
induction and have a way of defining the semantics of programs we cannot have a 
program of the form ~S or x : B ~ g(x) which is itself in S or in the range of g. 
One can, of course, regard BNF definitions like the above as fixed point equations, 
defining the smallest syntactic class which is closed under the various operations on 
the right. For a language with only finitary constructs this fixed point is reached by w 
iterations (every program is "born on a finite day") but we have to go further, to cater 
for programs like n : N ~ P~ where P, is born on d~, n. The functional implied by the 
right hand side of the above BNF definition is dearly monotone (the more programs 
there are, the more it delivers) but since it is not operating over a set (rather over 
the proper Class of all syntactic objects) it is by no means obvious it even has a fixed 
point. Fortunately it does, and is guaranteed to reach it by ~ iterations, where 
is the bound on nondeterminism and the size of E already mentioned. (See [BRW] 
for some more discussion of this question.) The principle of structural induction is 
then perfectly valid and corresponds to the principle of transfinite induction on the 
"birthday" of a term. 
To simplify the operational semantics a little it is convenient, as was done in 
[BRW], to treat the constructs STOP, SKIP and a ~ P as special cases of the 
construct x : B --* g(x): STOP has B empty, a ~ P has B = {a} and g(a) = P, and 
SKIP = v / --+ STOP. 
Let E be the set of all CSP terms defined by the above. An element of E may 
have free process variables, in which case it is said to be open. If it has none it is 
said to be closed; we denote the set of all closed terms by P. Closed terms are of 
importance since their meaning is fully determined; there axe no slots for processes 
waiting to be fitted in. 
If P,Q E E and p E Vat then P[Q/p] denotes the term where Q has been 
substituted for all free occurrences of p is P. When Q is not closed (though for us 
it usually will be) some care will be necessary to prevent P binding any of Q's free

The Plotkin-style semantics regards the set P of all closed CSP-terms as a tran- 
sition system, since it describes the set of all actions each closed term can perform 
and which new terms it may then become. The clauses of this operational semantics 
are given in the usual "natural deduction" style below.

There are two structure clashes between the operational and denotational seman- 
tics. The first is the obvious one that one is given in terms of transition systems 
and the other in terms of the abstract model U. But perhaps the more difficult one 
to resolve is the clash between the term rewriting style of the operational semantics 
and the denotational style of the other. Of course the latter means that the semantic 
value of each term is deduced from the semantic value of its subcomponents in a 
transparent way and that an abstract fixed point theory is used. In the earlier paper 
on the operational semantics of CSP [BRW] these two issues were resolved separately 
by creating an intermediate, denotational tree semantics. Unfortunately the complete 
metric spaces of trees used in that paper no longer exist because of the introduction 
here of infinite branching. 
The main result of [R3] is that, for each infinite regular cardinal/k, there exists 
a transition system I~ such that for all transition systems C with i(C) <_ ~, there 
exists a unique morphisln HA : C --~ T~. Thus T~ is a final object in the category of 
transition systems with morphisms as arrows. Analogues of the contraction mapping 
theorem and related results hold which are useful when one uses these systems. T~ 
can be used to give an intermediate denotational semantics to CSP in the style of 
[BRW]. However, because of the complexity of this new theory and thanks mainly to 
the construction of the ¢~ in the previous section we do not now need to do so. 
It is useful to extend the operational space defined above to include non-closed 
terms with their variables instantiated by elements of an arbitrary transition system. 
Definition. If C is any transition system then C cse is the system of CSP syntactic 
terms over C: namely the set of all substitutions by elements of C for all free variables 
of general terms in the language. All terms are distinct. Note that C cse contains 
every closed CSP term and every element of C. The transitions of each term are those 
of P if P E C (i.e., P ~ Q in C csP if and only if P ~ Q in C). The transitions of 
proper syntactic terms are determined from the operational semantic clauses above 
(from those of their subterms or otherwise). 
The stipulation that all terms are distinct means that each possible construction 
of a term leads to a different element of the system. For example, in (Ccse) cse, for 
each P E C the terms a -+ a -+ rp1, a --~ ra --* pn and ra --* a --+ pn are all different, 
where the syntactic quotes r7 denote the boundary between the inner and outer 
syntactic construction. However the obvious map from (CCSP) csP to C cse which 
"forgets" these boundaries is easily shown to be a morphism. 
Note that Theorem 3.2 (a) tells us that the image under ¢ of a closed term P is 
independent of whether it is considered to belong to the space P of closed terms or 
any C csP, since there is an obvious morphism embedding P into any C cse. 
We are now in a position to begin the proof of the main theorem of this section, 
namely that them semantics for CSP is congruent to the operational semantics. We 
will eventually complete the proof by performing a structural induction over C cse, 
but before we do that it is helpful to establish that the operational and denotational 
versions of all the non-recursive operators are congruent. 
Theorem 3.5. The operational versions of the various CSP operators are all con- 
gruent to the denotational versions over//. In other words, for each operator (D and
```
